12842
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Hilāl ibn Umayyah accused his wife before the Prophet ﷺ of adultery with Sharīk ibn Saḥmāʾ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Produce evidence, or the prescribed punishment will be applied to your back.”
Hilāl asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, when one of us sees a man with his wife, must he leave to search for evidence?”
The Prophet ﷺ continued saying:
“Produce evidence, or the prescribed punishment will be applied to your back.”
Hilāl said:
“By the One Who sent you with the truth, I am certainly truthful. Allah will surely reveal something that will protect my back from the punishment.”
Gabriel descended and revealed:
“Those who accuse their wives…”
The Prophet ﷺ recited the verses until he reached:
“If he is among the truthful.”
The Prophet ﷺ then left and summoned the woman. Hilāl came and gave his testimony while the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah knows that one of you is lying. Will either of you repent?”
The woman then stood and gave her testimony. When she reached the fifth oath, the people stopped her and said:
“This oath will make the punishment inevitable.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“She hesitated and stepped backward until we thought that she would turn back from completing the oath.
She then said, ‘I will not disgrace my people for the rest of time,’ and proceeded with the oath.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Observe her child. If she gives birth to one with darkened eyes, full buttocks, and thick calves, then he belongs to Sharīk ibn Saḥmāʾ.”
She gave birth to a child matching that description.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Were it not for what has already been revealed in the Book of Allah, I would have dealt with her severely.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4747).

12843
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
A man accused his wife and denied paternity of her child during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned both of them, and they performed mutual imprecation as Allah had prescribed.
He then attributed the child to the woman and permanently separated the two spouses.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4748) and Muslim (1494:9).

12844
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah said:
“Were I to see a man with my wife, I would strike him with the sharp edge of my sword without turning it aside.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard this, he said:
“Are you astonished by the protective jealousy of Saʿd? By Allah, I possess greater protective jealousy than he does, and Allah possesses greater protective jealousy than I do.
Because of Allah’s protective jealousy, He has forbidden all indecent acts, both those committed openly and those committed secretly.
No one possesses greater protective jealousy than Allah, and no one loves to grant an excuse more than Allah. For that reason, Allah sent the messengers as bearers of glad tidings and warners.
No one loves praise more than Allah. For that reason, Allah has promised Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6846) and Muslim (1499:17). The wording is that of Muslim.

12845
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, if I were to find a man with my wife, should I leave him alone until I bring four witnesses?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Yes.”
Saʿd said:
“No, by the One Who sent you with the truth! I would strike him with the sword before that.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Listen to what your leader is saying. He possesses strong protective jealousy, but I possess greater protective jealousy than he does, and Allah possesses greater protective jealousy than I do.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1498:16).

12846
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to travel, he would draw lots among his wives. Whichever wife’s lot was drawn would accompany him.
On one military expedition, my lot was drawn, so I went with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. This occurred after the verses of veiling had been revealed. I was carried inside my covered litter and would remain within it when it was placed down.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ completed that expedition and we were returning toward Madinah, he announced one night that the army would depart.
When the departure was announced, I stood and walked beyond the army to relieve myself. Afterward, I returned toward my riding place and touched my chest, only to discover that a necklace of onyx beads from Ẓafār had broken and fallen.
I returned to search for the necklace, and the search delayed me.
Meanwhile, the men responsible for lifting my litter came and placed it upon the camel that I rode, assuming that I was inside it. Women at that time were light in weight and had not become heavy, since they ate only small amounts of food. The men therefore did not notice the lightness of the litter when they lifted it. I was also a young girl.
They made the camel rise and departed. I found my necklace after the army had already moved on. When I returned to their campsite, no one remained to call or answer.
I stayed at the place where I had been camping, believing that they would notice my absence and return for me. While I sat there, sleep overcame me.
Ṣafwān ibn al-Muʿaṭṭal al-Sulamī, then al-Dhakwānī, had remained behind the army. In the morning, he reached my location and saw the dark outline of a sleeping person. When he saw me, he recognized me, for he had seen me before the command of veiling.
I awoke when he said:
“Indeed, we belong to Allah and to Him we shall return.”
I covered my face with my outer garment. By Allah, we did not exchange a single word, and I heard nothing from him except his declaration of return to Allah.
He lowered his camel and made it kneel, placing his foot upon its foreleg. I mounted it, and he led the camel until we reached the army while they were resting during the intense midday heat.
Those who were destined to be ruined were ruined through this incident. The person who bore the greatest share of the slander was ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.
ʿUrwah said that he was informed that the rumor would be spread and discussed in ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy’s presence. He would approve of it, listen to it, and seek further details.
ʿUrwah also said that among those specifically named as participants in the slander were Ḥassān ibn Thābit, Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah, and Ḥamnah bint Jaḥsh, along with others whose identities he did not know. They were a group, as Allah described them, while the one responsible for its greatest share was said to be ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.
ʿĀʾishah disliked that Ḥassān should be insulted in her presence. She would say that he was the one who composed:
My father, his father, and my honor are a protection for Muḥammad’s honor against you.
ʿĀʾishah continued:
When we arrived in Madinah, I became ill for an entire month. The people were spreading the statements of those responsible for the slander, while I knew nothing about it.
What caused me concern during my illness was that I did not receive from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ the same tenderness that he usually showed me when I was ill. He would enter, greet me, and ask:
“How is she?”
He would then leave. This made me uneasy, although I remained unaware of the evil being spread.
When I had partly recovered, I went out with Umm Misṭaḥ toward al-Manāṣiʿ, which was where we relieved ourselves. We went out only at night, since this occurred before latrines were constructed near our homes. We followed the former custom of the Arabs by going into the open land, and we disliked having latrines close to our houses.
Umm Misṭaḥ was the daughter of Abū Ruhm ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf. Her mother was the daughter of Ṣakhr ibn ʿĀmir and the maternal aunt of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. Her son was Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah ibn ʿAbbād ibn al-Muṭṭalib.
After we had finished and were returning toward my house, Umm Misṭaḥ stumbled over her garment and said:
“May Misṭaḥ be ruined!”
I said:
“What a terrible thing you have said! Are you insulting a man who witnessed Badr?”
She replied:
“Poor girl, have you not heard what he said?”
I asked:
“What did he say?”
She informed me of what the people of the slander had been saying. My illness then became even more severe.
When I returned home, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered, greeted me, and asked:
“How is she?”
I asked him:
“Will you permit me to go to my parents?”
I wanted to confirm the report through them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ permitted me to go.
I asked my mother:
“Mother, what are the people saying?”
She replied:
“My daughter, take the matter lightly. By Allah, rarely is there a beautiful woman beloved to her husband who has co-wives except that they speak much against her.”
I said:
“Glory be to Allah! Have the people truly been saying this?”
I wept throughout that entire night until morning. My tears did not stop, and I could not sleep. In the morning, I continued weeping.
When revelation was delayed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Usāmah ibn Zayd to consult them about separating from his wife.
Usāmah advised the Messenger of Allah ﷺ according to what he knew of the innocence of his family and the affection he held for them. He said:
“She is your wife, and we know nothing about her except good.”
ʿAlī said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah has not placed you under restriction, and there are many other women. Ask the female servant; she will tell you the truth.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Barīrah and asked:
“Barīrah, have you ever seen anything in her that caused you suspicion?”
Barīrah replied:
“By the One Who sent you with the truth, I have never seen anything from her that I could criticize, except that she is a young girl who sometimes falls asleep while guarding her family’s dough, and a domestic animal comes and eats it.”
That same day, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood upon the pulpit and sought someone to excuse him from dealing with ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy. He said:
“Muslims, who will excuse me regarding a man whose harm against my family has reached me? By Allah, I know nothing but good concerning my family. They have also mentioned a man about whom I know nothing but good, and he never entered upon my family except in my company.”
Saʿd ibn Muʿādh, from Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, stood and said:
“I will excuse you, O Messenger of Allah. If he is from the Aws, I will strike his neck. If he is from our brothers of the Khazraj, command us and we will carry out your order.”
A man from the Khazraj then stood. He was Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah, the chief of the Khazraj. He had previously been a righteous man, but tribal zeal overtook him. He said to Saʿd:
“By Allah, you have lied. You will not kill him, and you are unable to kill him. Had he been from your own tribe, you would not have wished him killed.”
Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr, the cousin of Saʿd ibn Muʿādh, stood and said to Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah:
“By Allah, you have lied. We will certainly kill him. You are a hypocrite defending the hypocrites.”
The two tribes, the Aws and Khazraj, became agitated until they nearly fought one another while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood upon the pulpit. He continued calming them until they fell silent, and he too became silent.
I wept throughout that entire day. My tears did not stop, and I could not sleep.
My parents remained with me the next morning. I had wept for two nights and a day without sleeping or ceasing to cry, until I thought that my liver would split from weeping.
While my parents sat beside me and I continued crying, a woman from the Anṣār requested permission to enter. I admitted her, and she sat and wept with me.
While we were in that condition, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered, greeted us, and sat down. He had not sat with me since the rumor began, and an entire month had passed without any revelation concerning me.
After sitting, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ praised Allah and said:
“To proceed, ʿĀʾishah, such-and-such has reached me concerning you. If you are innocent, Allah will declare your innocence. But if you have committed a sin, seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him, for when a servant acknowledges his sin and repents, Allah accepts his repentance.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished speaking, my tears suddenly stopped until I could not feel even a single drop.
I said to my father:
“Answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on my behalf concerning what he has said.”
My father replied:
“By Allah, I do not know what to say to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I said to my mother:
“Answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on my behalf.”
She replied:
“By Allah, I do not know what to say to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Although I was a young girl with little knowledge of the Qur’an, I said:
“By Allah, I know that you have heard this report so often that it has settled in your hearts and you have believed it. If I tell you that I am innocent, you will not believe me. If I confess to something of which Allah knows that I am innocent, you will believe me.
By Allah, I find no example for myself and you except the father of Joseph when he said:
‘Beautiful patience is most fitting, and Allah alone is the One whose help is sought against what you describe.’”
 [Sūrat Yūsuf 12:18]
I then turned away and lay upon my bed.
Allah knew that I was innocent and that He would declare my innocence. But by Allah, I never imagined that Allah would reveal recited revelation concerning me. I considered myself too insignificant for Allah to speak about my affair in revelation. I merely hoped that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would see a dream through which Allah would declare my innocence.
By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not left his seat, and no one had left the house, when revelation descended upon him. The severe condition that normally overtook him during revelation overtook him, until sweat poured from him like pearls, despite the coldness of the day, because of the weight of the words being revealed.
When the condition passed from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he was smiling. His first words were:
“ʿĀʾishah, Allah has declared your innocence.”
My mother said:
“Stand and go to him.”
I replied:
“By Allah, I will not stand for him. I praise no one except Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Allah then revealed the ten verses beginning:
“Indeed, those who came with the slander are a group among you.”
Allah revealed these verses declaring my innocence.
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq had been financially supporting Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah because of their kinship and because Misṭaḥ was poor. Abū Bakr said:
“By Allah, I will never again spend anything upon Misṭaḥ after what he said concerning ʿĀʾishah.”
Allah then revealed:
“Let not those of virtue and wealth among you swear not to give…”
until the words:
“Allah is Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Nūr 24:22]
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said:
“Yes, by Allah, I certainly love that Allah should forgive me.”
He restored the financial support he had been giving Misṭaḥ and said:
“By Allah, I will never withdraw it from him again.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also asked Zaynab bint Jaḥsh about me, saying:
“What do you know, or what have you seen?”
She replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I protect my hearing and sight from speaking without knowledge. By Allah, I know nothing except good.”
She was the wife of the Prophet ﷺ who competed most closely with me in status, but Allah protected her through piety. Her sister Ḥamnah, however, began defending her and was ruined among those who were ruined.
Ibn Shihāb said:
“This is what reached me from the account of those individuals.”
ʿUrwah then narrated that ʿĀʾishah said:
“By Allah, the man concerning whom the accusation was made would say, ‘Glory be to Allah! By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I have never uncovered the private covering of any woman.’”
He was later killed in the path of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4141) and Muslim (2770).

12847
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When the revelation declaring my innocence was sent down, the Prophet ﷺ stood upon the pulpit, mentioned the matter, and recited the Qur’anic verses.
When he descended from the pulpit, he ordered that the two men and the woman receive their prescribed punishment, and they were punished accordingly.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4474), al-Tirmidhī (3181), Ibn Mājah (2567), Aḥmad (24066), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (4/74).
Their names are explicitly mentioned in the following narration of Abū Hurayrah.

12848
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to travel, he would draw lots among his wives. ʿĀʾishah’s lot was drawn during the expedition of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq.
During the middle of the night, ʿĀʾishah went out to relieve herself, and her necklace became undone. She went to search for it.
Misṭaḥ was an orphan under the care of Abū Bakr and belonged to his household.
When ʿĀʾishah returned, she did not see the army.
Ṣafwān ibn al-Muʿaṭṭal al-Sulamī used to remain behind the people so that he could find and collect anything they had left behind, such as cups, leather bags, and water containers.
He saw ʿĀʾishah and covered his face from her, as the narrator believed, then brought his camel close to her.
He reached the army with her, and the people made statements about them.
The narrator then continued the account until he said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would come and stand at the door, asking:
“How is she?”
Then one day, he came and said:
“Receive glad tidings, ʿĀʾishah. Allah has revealed your vindication.”
She replied:
“All praise belongs to Allah, not to you.”
Allah revealed ten verses concerning the matter, beginning:
“Indeed, those who came with the slander are a group among you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then administered the prescribed punishment to Misṭaḥ, Ḥamnah, and Ḥassān.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (8011), whose wording is given here, Abū Yaʿlā (307), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (23/129).

12849
Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
Shortly before ʿĀʾishah’s death, Ibn ʿAbbās requested permission to visit her while she was overcome by illness.
She said:
“I fear that he will praise me.”
It was said to her:
“He is the cousin of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and one of the leading Muslims.”
She said:
“Permit him to enter.”
He entered and asked:
“How do you find yourself?”
She replied:
“In a good condition, provided that I remained mindful of Allah.”
He said:
“Then you are in a good condition, Allah willing. You are the wife of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He married no virgin other than you, and your vindication was revealed from heaven.”
Ibn al-Zubayr then entered after him.
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Ibn ʿAbbās entered and praised me. I wish that I had been something forgotten and completely unnoticed.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4753).

12850
Masrūq narrated:
I entered upon ʿĀʾishah while Ḥassān ibn Thābit was with her, reciting poetry in her praise. He said:
Chaste and dignified, untouched by suspicion, she awakens hungry, never consuming the flesh of unsuspecting women.
ʿĀʾishah said to him:
“But you were not like that.”
Masrūq said:
I asked her:
“Why do you permit him to enter upon you when Allah says:
‘The one among them who assumed the greatest share of it will suffer a tremendous punishment’?”
She replied:
“What punishment could be greater than blindness? He used to defend the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and compose poetry against his enemies.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4755) and Muslim (2488). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s version is abbreviated.

12851
ʿĀʾishah said:
“I have never heard poetry more beautiful than the poetry of Ḥassān. Whenever I quoted it, I hoped that Paradise would be his.”
Among what he said to Abū Sufyān was:
You insulted Muḥammad, so I replied in his defense, and with Allah lies the reward for that.
My father, his father, and my honor are a shield protecting Muḥammad’s honor from you.
Do you insult him when you are no equal to him? May the worse of you be sacrificed for the better.
My tongue is a sharp sword without defect, and my ocean cannot be muddied by buckets.
She was asked:
“Mother of the Believers, is this not idle speech?”
She replied:
“No. Idle speech is what is said in gatherings of women.”
She was asked:
“Does Allah not say:
‘The one among them who assumed the greatest share of it will suffer a tremendous punishment’?”
She replied:
“Did he not suffer a tremendous punishment? Did he not lose his sight and become disabled by a sword wound?”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr (17/193).

12852
ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words:
“The one among them who assumed the greatest share of it”:
“It was ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4749).
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl actively spread the turmoil and was its primary instigator. However, he did not make an explicit accusation himself, and therefore the prescribed punishment for slander was not carried out upon him.

12853
ʿĀʾishah used to recite the verse as:
“When you hastened to spread it with your tongues.”
She said:
“Al-walaq means lying.”
Ibn Abī Mulaykah said:
“She knew this better than anyone else because the verse was revealed concerning her.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4144).
Al-Walaq means hastening and rushing into false speech.
The well-known recitation is:
“When you received and passed it from tongue to tongue.”

12854
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A servant may utter a word without considering what it contains, and because of it he falls into the Fire farther than the distance between the east and the west.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6477) and Muslim (2988:50).

12855
ʿUrwah narrated that ʿĀʾishah related to him the account of the slander and said:
Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī’s wife informed him about the matter and said:
“Abū Ayyūb, have you not heard what the people are saying?”
He asked:
“What are they saying?”
She informed him of what the people of the slander were spreading.
He replied:
“It is not proper for us to speak of this. Glory be to You, O Allah! This is a tremendous slander.”
ʿĀʾishah said that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Why, when you heard it, did you not say, ‘It is not proper for us to speak of this. Glory be to You! This is a tremendous slander’?”
Good: Narrated by al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 335).

12856
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated in a lengthy hadith:
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq used to provide financial support to Misṭaḥ ibn Uthāthah because of their kinship and because Misṭaḥ was poor.
After what Misṭaḥ had said about ʿĀʾishah, Abū Bakr said:
“By Allah, I will never spend anything upon Misṭaḥ again.”
Allah then revealed:
“Let not those among you who possess virtue and wealth swear not to give to their relatives, the poor, and those who emigrated in the path of Allah. Let them pardon and overlook. Do you not love that Allah should forgive you? Allah is Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said:
“Yes, by Allah, I certainly love that Allah should forgive me.”
He resumed the financial support that he had previously given to Misṭaḥ and said:
“By Allah, I will never withdraw it from him again.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4141) and Muslim (2770).
Whoever swears that he will not perform a good deed should expiate his oath and perform the good deed. He should not abandon goodness because of his oath, as stated in the hadith:

12857
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever swears an oath and then sees that another course is better than it should expiate his oath and do what is better.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (11) and Muslim (1650:12).

12858
ʿĀʾishah said:
“I was accused of what I was accused while completely unaware, and I only learned of it afterward.”
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting with me, revelation descended upon him. Whenever revelation came to him, a state resembling sleep would overtake him.
Revelation descended upon him while he was sitting beside me. He then sat upright, wiped his face, and said:
“ʿĀʾishah, receive glad tidings.”
I replied:
“All praise belongs to Allah, not to you.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, those who accuse chaste, unsuspecting believing women…”
until the words:
“Those are declared innocent of what they say. For them are forgiveness and an honorable provision.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (17/227) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (23/121).

12859
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Avoid the seven destructive sins.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what are they?”
He replied:
“Associating partners with Allah, sorcery, killing a soul whom Allah has forbidden to be killed except by right, consuming usury, consuming an orphan’s wealth, fleeing from the battlefield, and accusing chaste, unsuspecting believing women of adultery.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2766) and Muslim (89).

12860
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he laughed and asked:
“Do you know what made me laugh?”
We replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“I laughed at the conversation between a servant and his Lord. The servant will say, ‘My Lord, did You not protect me from injustice?’
Allah will say, ‘Yes.’
The servant will say, ‘Then I will accept no witness against myself except one from my own body.’
Allah will say, ‘Today, your own self is sufficient as a witness against you, along with the noble recording angels.’
His mouth will then be sealed, and his limbs will be commanded, ‘Speak.’
They will testify concerning his deeds.
He will then be permitted to speak and will say to his limbs, ‘Away with you! May you be ruined! It was for your sake that I was defending myself.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2969).

12861
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in the hadith concerning seeing the Lord on the Day of Resurrection:
“It will then be said to him, ‘Now We will bring forth Our witness against you.’
He will think to himself, ‘Who could testify against me?’
His mouth will then be sealed, and his thigh, flesh, and bones will be commanded, ‘Speak.’
His thigh, flesh, and bones will testify concerning his deeds so that he will have no excuse remaining within himself.
That person is the hypocrite, the one with whom Allah is displeased.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2968).

12862
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
I was sitting in a gathering of the Anṣār when Abū Mūsā arrived looking frightened. He said:
“I sought permission to enter upon ʿUmar three times, but permission was not granted, so I returned.”
ʿUmar asked:
“What prevented you from remaining?”
He replied:
“I sought permission three times, but permission was not granted, so I returned. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘If one of you seeks permission three times and permission is not granted, he should return.’”
ʿUmar said:
“By Allah, you must produce evidence for this. Is there anyone among you who heard it from the Prophet ﷺ?”
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb said:
“By Allah, only the youngest person present will accompany you.”
I was the youngest among them, so I went with Abū Mūsā and informed ʿUmar that the Prophet ﷺ had said this.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6245), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (2153).

12863
Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ concerning a debt owed by my father and knocked on the door.
He asked:
“Who is it?”
I replied:
“Me.”
He repeated:
“Me? Me?”
as though he disliked that answer.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6250) and Muslim (2155). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12864
ʿAbdullāh ibn Busr narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to the door of a people, he would not stand directly facing it. Instead, he would stand to its right or left and say:
“Peace be upon you. Peace be upon you.”
This was because homes at that time did not have curtains covering their entrances.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5186), Aḥmad (17694), and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (1078).

12865
Jarīr ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about an accidental glance, and he commanded me to turn my gaze away.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2159).

12866
Ibn Buraydah narrated from his father that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿAlī:
“ʿAlī, do not follow one glance with another. The first glance is excused for you, but the second is not.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2148), al-Tirmidhī (2777), al-Ḥākim (2/194), al-Bayhaqī (7/90), Aḥmad (22974), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Mushkil al-Āthār (1866).

12867
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Beware of sitting along the roads.”
They replied:
“We cannot avoid it, for they are the places where we sit and speak with one another.”
He said:
“If you insist upon sitting there, then give the road its due rights.”
They asked:
“What are the rights of the road?”
He replied:
“Lowering the gaze, refraining from causing harm, returning greetings of peace, commanding what is right, and forbidding what is wrong.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2465) and Muslim (2121). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12868
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The son of Adam has had his allotted share of fornication decreed for him, and he will inevitably encounter it. The fornication of the eyes is looking, the fornication of the ears is listening, the fornication of the tongue is speaking, the fornication of the hand is grasping, and the fornication of the foot is walking. The heart desires and wishes, while the private parts either confirm all of that or deny it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2657:21).
Abū Dāwūd also narrated it through another chain (2152), adding:
“The mouth also commits fornication, and its fornication is kissing.”
Its chain of transmission is good.
Regarding the words:
“And guard their private parts”:

12869
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever guarantees for me what is between his jaws and what is between his legs, I will guarantee Paradise for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6474).

12870
Bahz ibn Ḥakīm narrated from his father, from his grandfather:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, regarding our nakedness, what may we expose and what must we conceal?”
He replied:
“Guard your nakedness except from your wife or those whom your right hand possesses.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what if people are together with one another?”
He replied:
“If you are able to prevent anyone from seeing it, then do not let anyone see it.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what if one of us is alone?”
He replied:
“Allah has a greater right that one should feel modest before Him than before people.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4017), al-Tirmidhī (2769), Ibn Mājah (1920), al-Ḥākim (4/179–180), and al-Bayhaqī (1/199).

12871
ʿĀʾishah said:
“May Allah have mercy upon the first women of the Emigrants. When Allah revealed:
‘Let them draw their head coverings over their chests,’
they tore their outer garments and used them as head coverings.”
In another narration:
“When the verse, ‘Let them draw their head coverings over their chests,’ was revealed, they took their waist garments, tore them from their edges, and covered their heads with them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4758). The second narration is also recorded by al-Bukhārī (4759).

12872
Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah narrated:
While we were with ʿĀʾishah, the women of Quraysh and their virtues were mentioned. ʿĀʾishah said:
“The women of Quraysh certainly possess virtue, but by Allah, I have never seen women better than the women of the Anṣār, more firmly believing in the Book of Allah or more faithful to the revelation.
When the verse from Sūrat al-Nūr was revealed:
‘Let them draw their head coverings over their chests,’
their men returned to them and recited what Allah had revealed. Each man recited it to his wife, daughter, sister, and every female relative.
Every woman among them immediately took her decorated outer garment and wrapped it around her head out of belief in and affirmation of what Allah had revealed in His Book. The following morning, they prayed behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with their heads covered, as though crows were resting upon their heads.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2575).
The words:
“Or those whom their right hands possess”
permit this because of necessity and because such a servant frequently enters upon and leaves the presence of his mistress.

12873
Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ brought Fāṭimah a male servant whom he had given to her. Fāṭimah was wearing a garment which, when she used it to cover her head, did not reach her feet, and when she covered her feet with it, did not reach her head.
When the Prophet ﷺ saw the difficulty she was experiencing, he said:
“There is no blame upon you. It is only your father and your servant.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4106).
The words:
“Or male attendants having no sexual desire”
refer to men who possess no desire for women and no awareness of their physical attractions. Otherwise, they must be prevented from entering upon women, even if the man is effeminate, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12874
Umm Salamah narrated:
An effeminate man was present with her while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in the house. The man said to Umm Salamah’s brother:
“ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah, if Allah grants you victory over al-Ṭāʾif tomorrow, I will direct you to the daughter of Ghaylān. When she approaches, she appears with four folds, and when she turns away, she appears with eight.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard him and said:
“Such men must not enter upon you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4324) and Muslim (2180).

12875
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
An effeminate man used to enter upon the wives of the Prophet ﷺ, and they regarded him as one of the men who possessed no sexual desire.
One day, the Prophet ﷺ entered while this man was with one of his wives. The man was describing a woman, saying:
“When she approaches, she appears with four folds, and when she turns away, she appears with eight.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I see that this man understands what is found here. He must not enter upon you.”
They therefore prevented him from entering.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2181).
Abū Dāwūd also narrated it through another chain (4110), adding:
It was said, “O Messenger of Allah, he will then die of hunger.”
The Prophet ﷺ therefore permitted him to enter twice every Friday to ask for food and then leave.
Its chain of transmission is authentic.
Those relatives and others mentioned in this noble verse were specifically listed because of how frequently they entered upon women. Paternal and maternal uncles were not mentioned, although they are also unmarriageable relatives, because they generally lived elsewhere and did not ordinarily need to enter upon them frequently.
Regarding the words:
“Turn to Allah in repentance, all of you, O believers, so that you may succeed”:

12876
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“People, repent to Allah, for I repent to Him one hundred times each day.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2702:42).

12877
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are three people whom Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has obligated Himself to assist: the one who fights in the path of Allah, the contracted slave who intends to fulfill his contract of emancipation, and the person who marries seeking chastity.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1655), al-Nasāʾī (3120, 3218), Ibn Mājah (2518), and Aḥmad (7416), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4030) and al-Ḥākim (2/160).

12878
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us:
“Young men, whoever among you possesses the means to marry should marry, for marriage is more effective in lowering the gaze and guarding chastity. Whoever is unable to marry should fast, for fasting will diminish his desire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5065) and Muslim (1400:1).
Regarding the words:
“Do not compel your slave women into prostitution if they desire chastity”:

12879
Jābir narrated:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl used to say to one of his slave women:
“Go and earn something for us through prostitution.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“Do not compel your slave women into prostitution if they desire chastity, seeking the temporary gains of worldly life. If anyone compels them, then after their compulsion Allah is Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful toward them.”
In another wording narrated from Jābir:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl had one slave woman named Musaykah and another named Umaymah. He used to compel them to commit fornication. They complained about this to the Prophet ﷺ, so Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Do not compel your slave women into prostitution…”
until the words:
“Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3029). The second wording is also recorded by him (3029:27).

12880
Jābir narrated:
Musaykah, a slave woman belonging to one of the Anṣār, came and said:
“My master compels me to engage in prostitution.”
The following verse was revealed concerning this:
“Do not compel your slave women into prostitution.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2311), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11301), and al-Ḥākim (2/397).
This noble verse prohibits compelling slave women to commit sexual immorality for financial gain, for such earnings are forbidden, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12881
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ prohibited the earnings of slave women.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2283).

12882
Abū Masʿūd al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited:
“The price paid for a dog, the earnings of a prostitute, and the payment given to a fortune-teller.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (68), al-Bukhārī (2237), and Muslim (1567:39).

12883
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ rose during the night to perform voluntary prayer, he would say:
“O Allah, all praise belongs to You. You are the Sustainer of the heavens and the earth and all who are within them. All praise belongs to You. To You belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth and all who are within them. All praise belongs to You. You are the Light of the heavens and the earth. All praise belongs to You. You are the Sovereign of the heavens and the earth…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1120) and Muslim (769).
The words:
“A blessed olive tree”
mean that the olive tree is blessed. Its oil is eaten and used for anointing, as stated in the hadith:

12884
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Eat olive oil and apply it to your bodies, for it comes from a blessed tree.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1851), Ibn Mājah (3319), al-Ḥākim (4/122), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (82, 83).
The words:
“Neither eastern nor western”
mean that the tree stands in a broad, elevated place where no mountain, tree, cave, or other object shades or conceals it. Sunlight reaches it from the beginning of the day until its end, which produces oil of the finest quality, clarity, and purity.
The words:
“Allah guides to His light whomever He wills”
mean that Allah directs whichever of His servants He chooses toward His guidance and grants them success in attaining it, as stated in the hadith:

12885
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, created His creation in darkness and cast upon them from His light. Whoever was touched by that light was guided, and whoever missed it went astray.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr then said:
“For this reason, I say that the Pen has dried in accordance with the knowledge of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2642), who said: “A good hadith.”

12886
Maḥmūd ibn Labīd narrated:
ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān wished to rebuild the mosque, but the people disliked this and preferred that he leave it in its existing form. He said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever builds a mosque for Allah, Allah will build for him a house like it in Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (533:25) and al-Bukhārī (450).

12887
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever builds a mosque for Allah in which the name of Allah, the Exalted, is remembered, Allah will build for him a house in Paradise because of it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (126), Ibn Mājah (735, 2758), Ibn Abī Shaybah (19902), and al-Ḥākim (2/89).

12888
Anas narrated:
While we were in the mosque with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, a Bedouin came and began urinating inside the mosque. The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Stop! Stop!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not interrupt him. Leave him.”
They left him until he finished urinating. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called him and said:
“These mosques are not suitable for urine or filth. They are only for the remembrance of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, prayer, and recitation of the Qur’an.”
Or he said words to that effect.
He then commanded one of the men to bring a bucket of water, and it was poured over the affected place.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (285) and al-Bukhārī (219).
The words:
“Glorifying Him within them in the mornings and evenings are men”
indicate that men are those who regularly maintain and populate the mosques.
As for women, prayer in their homes is better for them than prayer in the mosque, as stated in the hadith:

12889
Umm Ḥumayd, the wife of Abū Ḥumayd al-Sāʿidī, came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I love praying with you.”
He replied:
“I know that you love praying with me. However, your prayer in the innermost part of your house is better for you than your prayer in your private room. Your prayer in your private room is better than your prayer in the courtyard of your house. Your prayer in the courtyard of your house is better for you than your prayer in the mosque of your people, and your prayer in the mosque of your people is better for you than your prayer in my mosque.”
She therefore ordered that a prayer place be constructed for her in the furthest and darkest part of her house. She continued praying there until she met Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (27090), Ibn Khuzaymah (1689), Ibn Ḥibbān (2217), and Ibn Abī Shaybah (2/384).

12890
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever obeys me has obeyed Allah, and whoever disobeys me has disobeyed Allah. Whoever obeys the commander I appoint has obeyed me, and whoever disobeys the commander I appoint has disobeyed me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7137) and Muslim (1835:33).
The words:
“But if you turn away, then he is responsible only for what he has been charged with, and you are responsible for what you have been charged with”
mean that the Messenger is responsible for fulfilling the duty assigned to him: calling people to Allah and conveying the message. You are responsible for what has been assigned to you: responding, accepting, and obeying Allah and His Messenger ﷺ.
The same principle applies between a ruler and his subjects: each must fulfill the responsibility assigned to him, as stated in the following hadith:

12891
Wāʾil al-Ḥaḍramī narrated:
Salamah ibn Yazīd al-Juʿfī asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Prophet of Allah, what do you command us to do if rulers are placed over us who demand their rights from us but withhold our rights?”
The Prophet ﷺ turned away from him. He asked again, but the Prophet ﷺ turned away. When he asked a second or third time, al-Ashʿath ibn Qays pulled him aside.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Listen and obey. They are responsible for the duties placed upon them, and you are responsible for the duties placed upon you.”
In another narration, al-Ashʿath ibn Qays pulled him aside, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave a similar answer.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1846). Muslim also narrated the second version.

12892
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions arrived in Madinah and the Anṣār gave them refuge, all the Arab tribes united against them as though firing from a single bow. The Muslims would not spend the night without their weapons, nor awaken except while armed.
They asked:
“Do you think we will live to see a time when we may spend the night safe and at peace, fearing no one except Allah?”
Allah then revealed:
“Allah has promised those among you who believe and perform righteous deeds that He will certainly grant them succession in the land, as He granted it to those before them; that He will firmly establish for them the religion He has chosen for them; and that He will replace their fear with security…”
until the words:
“Whoever disbelieves after that”—meaning whoever is ungrateful for the blessing—“then those are the defiantly disobedient.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (7025), al-Ḥākim (2/401), al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (pp. 341–342), and al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (1145–1146).

12893
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Give this nation glad tidings of splendor, elevation, victory, and establishment upon the earth. But whoever among them performs deeds of the Hereafter for worldly gain will have no share in the Hereafter.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21220), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (405) and al-Ḥākim (4/311), and narrated by al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (1154).

12894
ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim narrated:
While I was with the Prophet ﷺ, a man came to him complaining of poverty. Another man then came complaining about highway robbery and the dangers of travel.
The Prophet ﷺ asked me:
“ʿAdī, have you seen al-Ḥīrah?”
I replied:
“I have not seen it, although I have been informed about it.”
He said:
“If you live long enough, you will certainly see a woman traveling in her camel litter from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulates the Kaʿbah, fearing no one except Allah.”
I thought to myself:
“Then where will the brigands of Ṭayyiʾ, who have spread violence throughout the land, be?”
He continued:
“If you live long enough, the treasures of Chosroes will certainly be conquered.”
I asked:
“Chosroes, son of Hormuz?”
He replied:
“Chosroes, son of Hormuz. If you live long enough, you will see a man go out with a handful of gold or silver seeking someone who will accept it, but he will find no one willing to take it.
Each one of you will certainly meet Allah on the Day he meets Him without any interpreter standing between him and Allah. Allah will ask, ‘Did I not send you a messenger who conveyed the message to you?’ He will reply, ‘Yes.’
Allah will ask, ‘Did I not give you wealth and bestow favors upon you?’ He will reply, ‘Yes.’
He will look to his right and see nothing but Hell, and he will look to his left and see nothing but Hell.”
ʿAdī said:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Protect yourselves from the Fire, even with half a date. Whoever cannot find even half a date should do so with a kind word.”
ʿAdī continued:
“I later saw a woman travel in her camel litter from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulated the Kaʿbah, fearing no one except Allah. I was also among those who conquered the treasures of Chosroes, son of Hormuz. If you live long enough, you will also see what Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ foretold about a man going out with a handful of wealth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3595).
Regarding the words:
“They worship Me without associating anything with Me”:

12895
Muʿādh ibn Jabal narrated:
I was riding behind the Prophet ﷺ on a donkey named ʿUfayr when he asked:
“Muʿādh, do you know Allah’s right over His servants and the servants’ right upon Allah?”
I replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“Allah’s right over His servants is that they worship Him without associating anything with Him. The servants’ right upon Allah is that He will not punish anyone who does not associate anything with Him.”
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should I not give the people this glad news?”
He replied:
“Do not give them this glad news, lest they rely upon it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2856) and Muslim (30:49).
What Allah promised in this verse was fulfilled during the era of the Companions. Allah established them upon the earth and granted them complete security. They worshipped Allah alone without partners, free from fear and anxiety.
A group of Muslims will also continue worshipping Allah alone throughout the inhabited world with strength and security, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12896
Thawbān narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A group from my nation will continue to remain manifest upon the truth. Those who abandon them will not harm them, until the command of Allah comes while they remain in that condition.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1920).

12897
Jābir ibn Samurah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“This religion will continue to remain established, with a group of Muslims fighting in its defense until the Hour is established.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1922).

12898
Ibn ʿAbbās explained the verse:
“There is no blame upon the blind, nor upon the lame, nor upon the sick, nor upon yourselves if you eat from your own homes, the homes of your fathers, your mothers, your brothers, your sisters, your paternal uncles, your paternal aunts, your maternal uncles, your maternal aunts, the homes whose keys you possess, or the home of your friend. There is no blame upon you whether you eat together or separately.”
He said:
When Allah revealed:
“Believers, do not consume one another’s wealth unjustly,”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:29]
the Muslims said:
“Allah has forbidden us from consuming one another’s wealth unjustly, and food is among the finest forms of wealth. Therefore, it is not lawful for any of us to eat in another person’s home.”
The people consequently refrained from doing so. Allah then revealed:
“There is no blame upon the blind, nor upon the lame, nor upon the sick…”
until the words:
“…or the homes whose keys you possess.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (17/366) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2648).

12899
Ibn ʿAbbās said regarding the verse:
“Believers, do not consume one another’s wealth unjustly, unless it is trade conducted by mutual consent among you.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:29]
After this verse was revealed, a man would feel uneasy about eating in another person’s home. The verse in Sūrat al-Nūr removed that restriction:
“There is no blame upon you whether you eat together or separately.”
A wealthy man might invite one of his relatives to eat, but the invited person would say:
“I feel uneasy about eating this food. A poor person is more entitled to it than I am.”
Allah therefore permitted them to eat food over which His name had been mentioned and also permitted the food of the People of the Book.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3753) and al-Bayhaqī (7/274–275).

12900
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Muslims were eager to go out on military expeditions with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They would hand the keys to their homes to those who were unable to accompany them and say:
“We permit you to eat whatever you wish from our homes.”
Those left behind would say:
“It is not lawful for us. They may have granted permission without truly being pleased to do so.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, therefore revealed:
“There is no blame upon the blind, nor upon the lame, nor upon the sick, nor upon yourselves if you eat from your own homes or the homes of your fathers…”
until the words:
“…or the homes whose keys you possess, or the home of your friend.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2241) and Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2646).
Regarding the words:
“There is no blame upon you whether you eat together or separately”:
This is a concession from Allah allowing a person to eat alone or with a group, although eating together is better.

12901
Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The food most beloved to Allah is that over which many hands gather.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (2046) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (7313).
It is authentically established that the food of one person is sufficient for two and the food of two is sufficient for four. This may be due to the blessing found in eating together.

12902
Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The food of one person is sufficient for two, the food of two is sufficient for four, and the food of four is sufficient for eight.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2059).

12903
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The food of two people is sufficient for three, and the food of three is sufficient for four.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Description of the Prophet ﷺ (1726), al-Bukhārī (5392), and Muslim (2058).

12904
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man passed by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was sitting in a gathering and said:
“Peace be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Ten good deeds.”
Another man passed and said:
“Peace and the mercy of Allah be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Twenty good deeds.”
Another man passed and said:
“Peace, the mercy of Allah, and His blessings be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Thirty good deeds.”
A man then stood and left the gathering without offering the greeting of peace. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How quickly your companion has forgotten! When one of you comes to a gathering, let him offer the greeting of peace. If he then wishes to sit, let him sit. When he stands to leave, let him again offer the greeting of peace, for the first greeting is no more deserving than the final one.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (986) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (493).

12905
Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was granted five things that no one before me was granted: I was supported with fear cast into the hearts of my enemies from the distance of a month’s journey; the earth was made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification, so wherever a man from my nation is overtaken by the time of prayer, let him pray; spoils of war were made lawful for me, although they were not lawful for anyone before me; I was granted intercession; and every prophet was sent specifically to his own people, whereas I was sent to all humanity.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

12906
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was favored over the other prophets in six ways: I was given concise yet comprehensive speech; I was supported through fear cast into the hearts of my enemies; spoils of war were made lawful for me; the earth was made for me a means of purification and a place of prayer; I was sent to all creation; and the line of prophets was concluded with me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (523:5).

12907
ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said one day during a sermon:
“Indeed, my Lord has commanded me to teach you what you do not know from what He has taught me this day…”
Allah also said:
“I have sent you only to test you and to test others through you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865).

12908
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah will grasp the earth on the Day of Resurrection and fold up the heavens in His right hand. He will then say: ‘I am the King. Where are the kings of the earth?’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7382) and Muslim (2787).

12909
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The polytheists said:
“If Muḥammad is truly a prophet as he claims, why does his Lord trouble him by revealing only one or two verses or a single sūrah at a time? Why was the Qur’an not sent down to him all at once?”
Allah then revealed to His Prophet ﷺ an answer to their objection, beginning with:
“Why was the Qur’an not revealed to him all at once?”
and continuing until:
“And they are farthest astray from the path.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2689) and al-Maqdisī in al-Aḥādīth al-Mukhtārah (10/119–120).
The words:
“Those who will be gathered upon their faces toward Hell”
mean that the disbelievers will be driven to Hell upon their faces on the Day of Resurrection.

12910
Anas narrated:
A man asked:
“Prophet of Allah, will the disbeliever truly be gathered upon his face on the Day of Resurrection?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Is the One who enabled him to walk upon two feet in this world not able to make him walk upon his face on the Day of Resurrection?”
Qatādah said:
“Yes, by the might of our Lord.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4760) and Muslim (2806). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12911
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Have you seen the one who takes his desire as his god?”
During the pre-Islamic period, a man might worship a white stone for a long time. If he later found a more attractive stone, he would throw away the first and worship the other. Allah then revealed this verse.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (8/2699–2700) and al-Maqdisī in al-Aḥādīth al-Mukhtārah (10/120–121).

12912
Zayd ibn Khālid al-Juhanī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in the dawn prayer at al-Ḥudaybiyah after rainfall during the night. When he finished the prayer, he turned toward the people and asked:
“Do you know what your Lord has said?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“Allah said: ‘Some of My servants entered the morning believing in Me, while others entered it disbelieving in Me. Whoever said, “We were given rain by the favor and mercy of Allah,” believes in Me and disbelieves in the stars. Whoever said, “We were given rain because of such-and-such a star,” disbelieves in Me and believes in the stars.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (4), al-Bukhārī (846), and Muslim (71).

12913
Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān narrated, each confirming the account of the other:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out during the year of al-Ḥudaybiyah. The lengthy hadith states that Suhayl ibn ʿAmr came and said:
“Bring the document and write an agreement between us and you.”
The Prophet ﷺ summoned the scribe and said:
“Write: In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.”
Suhayl said:
“As for ‘the Most Compassionate,’ by Allah, I do not know who that is. Instead, write: ‘In Your name, O Allah,’ as you used to write.”
The Muslims said:
“By Allah, we will write nothing except: ‘In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Write: In Your name, O Allah.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2731–2733).

12914
Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, extends His hand during the night so that the sinner of the day may repent, and He extends His hand during the day so that the sinner of the night may repent. This will continue until the sun rises from the west.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2759).

12915
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the call announcing the beginning of prayer is made, do not come to it while running. Come with tranquility. Whatever part of the prayer you catch, perform it, and complete whatever you miss, for one of you is regarded as being in prayer for as long as he is proceeding toward the prayer.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Book of Prayer (4), Muslim (602:152), and al-Bukhārī (908).

12916
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
I asked—or the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Which sin is greatest in the sight of Allah?”
He replied:
“That you appoint a rival to Allah even though He created you.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you kill your child out of fear that he will share your food.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you commit adultery with your neighbor’s wife.”
The following verse was revealed confirming the words of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Those who do not invoke another god alongside Allah, do not kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden except by right, and do not commit adultery.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4761) and Muslim (86). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12917
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some polytheists had committed many murders and committed adultery frequently. They came to Muḥammad ﷺ and said:
“What you say and call people toward is certainly good. Could you tell us whether there is any expiation for what we have done?”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“Those who do not invoke another god alongside Allah, do not kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden except by right, and do not commit adultery.”
The following verse was also revealed:
“Say: ‘O My servants who have transgressed against themselves, do not despair of Allah’s mercy. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. He is truly the Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.’”
 [Sūrat al-Zumar 39:53]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4810) and Muslim (122).

12918
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
Ibn Abzā said to me:
“Ask Ibn ʿAbbās about the words of Allah:
‘Whoever deliberately kills a believer, his recompense is Hell, remaining there, and Allah is angry with him, has cursed him, and has prepared for him a tremendous punishment,’
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:93]
and about His words:
‘They do not kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden except by right,’”
until the verse reaches:
“Except those who repent.”
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās, and he replied:
When this verse was revealed, the people of Makkah said:
“We have associated partners with Allah, killed souls whom Allah had forbidden us to kill, and committed indecent acts.”
Allah therefore revealed:
“Except those who repent, believe, and perform righteous deeds,”
until the words:
“Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4765) and Muslim (3023:19). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.
Muslim added at the end:
“As for one who enters Islam, understands it, and then commits murder, there is no repentance for him.”

12919
Salamah ibn Qays al-Ashjaʿī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said during the Farewell Pilgrimage:
“They are only four: do not associate anything with Allah, do not kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden except by right, do not commit adultery, and do not steal.”
Salamah said:
“I have never guarded anything more carefully than these commands since hearing them from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1899), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11309), and al-Ḥākim (4/351).
Regarding the words:
“Except those who repent, believe, and perform righteous deeds”:
The scholars differed over whether deliberate murder can be forgiven through repentance. Ibn ʿAbbās and others held that the deliberate murderer has no repentance, while the majority of scholars held that repentance remains available to him. This issue was previously discussed in detail under verse 93 of Sūrat al-Nisāʾ.
Regarding the words:
“For those, Allah will replace their evil deeds with good deeds”:

12920
Abū Dharr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I know the last person to enter Paradise and the last person to emerge from the Fire. A man will be brought on the Day of Resurrection, and it will be said: ‘Present his minor sins to him, but conceal his major sins.’
His minor sins will be presented, and he will be asked: ‘Did you do such-and-such on such-and-such a day? And did you do such-and-such on another day?’
He will reply, ‘Yes,’ unable to deny them, while fearing that his major sins will also be presented.
It will then be said to him: ‘In place of every evil deed, you will receive a good deed.’
He will say: ‘My Lord, I committed other deeds that I do not see listed here.’”
Abū Dharr said:
“I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laugh until his molar teeth became visible.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (190).

12921
Abū Ṭawīl Shaṭab al-Mamdūd came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked:
“What do you say about a man who committed every kind of sin and left none of them undone? He did not leave any minor or major desire without committing it. Is repentance available to him?”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Have you accepted Islam?”
He replied:
“As for me, I testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner, and that you are the Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Yes. Perform good deeds and abandon evil deeds, and Allah will transform all of them into good deeds for you.”
The man asked:
“Even my acts of betrayal and immorality?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man exclaimed:
“Allah is the Greatest!”
He continued proclaiming Allah’s greatness until he disappeared from sight.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3244) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (7/375–376). The wording is that of al-Ṭabarānī.

12922
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Some people will wish that they had committed even more evil deeds.”
They asked:
“Why, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“They are those whose evil deeds Allah transformed into good deeds.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (4/63).

12923
Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was asked about the major sins. He replied:
“Associating partners with Allah, disobeying one’s parents, killing a person, and giving false testimony.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2653) and Muslim (88:144).

12924
Abū Bakrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ asked three times:
“Shall I inform you of the gravest major sins?”
He then said:
“Associating partners with Allah, disobedience toward one’s parents, and false testimony—or false speech.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had been reclining, but he sat upright and continued repeating it until we thought:
“If only he would stop.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2654) and Muslim (87:143). The wording is that of Muslim.

12925
Jubayr ibn Nufayr narrated:
One day, we were sitting with al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad when a man passed by and said:
“Blessed are these two eyes that saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. By Allah, we wish that we had seen what you saw and witnessed what you witnessed.”
Al-Miqdād became angry, which surprised me because the man had apparently said nothing but good.
Al-Miqdād then turned toward him and said:
“What causes a man to wish that he had been present at an event from which Allah kept him absent? He does not know how he would have behaved had he witnessed it.
By Allah, many people were present during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ whom Allah cast face-first into Hell because they neither responded to him nor believed him.
Do you not praise Allah for bringing you forth knowing no lord except your Lord, affirming what your Prophet brought, while sparing you the trial by means of others?
By Allah, Allah sent the Prophet ﷺ during the most difficult circumstances in which any prophet had ever been sent: during a period without revelation and an age of ignorance in which the people believed that no religion was better than the worship of idols.
He came with a criterion that separated truth from falsehood and even separated a father from his son. A man might see his father, son, or brother as a disbeliever after Allah had opened the lock upon his own heart to faith. He knew that if his relative died in that state, he would enter the Fire. Therefore, his eyes could not be comforted while he knew that his beloved relative would be in the Fire.
This is what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, referred to when He said:
‘Those who say: Our Lord, grant us comfort of the eyes in our spouses and descendants.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23810), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (87), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6552).

12926
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“Five signs have already occurred: the smoke, the moon, the Romans, the mighty seizure, and the inevitable punishment referred to in the words:
‘It will soon become inevitable.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4767) and Muslim (2798:41).

12927
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah, he found the Jews fasting on a particular day, meaning the Day of ʿĀshūrāʾ.
They said:
“This is a tremendous day. It is the day upon which Allah saved Moses and drowned Pharaoh’s people. Moses therefore fasted on it in gratitude to Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have a greater right to Moses than they do.”
He fasted that day and commanded that it be fasted.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3397), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (1130:127).

12928
Rifāʿah al-Zuraqī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said during a lengthy supplication on the Day of Uḥud:
“O Allah, make faith beloved to us and beautify it within our hearts. Make disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience hateful to us, and make us among those who are rightly guided. O Allah, cause us to die as Muslims, keep us alive as Muslims, and join us with the righteous, neither disgraced nor subjected to temptation.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (15492), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (699), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1800), al-Ḥākim (1/506–507), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Duʿāʾ (1075).

12929
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Abraham will meet his father Āzar on the Day of Resurrection, and Āzar’s face will be covered with darkness and dust. Abraham will say to him, ‘Did I not tell you not to disobey me?’
His father will reply, ‘Today, I will not disobey you.’
Abraham will say, ‘My Lord, You promised me that You would not disgrace me on the Day they are resurrected. What disgrace could be greater than the disgrace of my father being kept far from Your mercy?’
Allah, the Exalted, will say, ‘I have forbidden Paradise to the disbelievers.’
It will then be said, ‘Abraham, look beneath your feet.’
He will look and see a bloodstained male hyena. It will be seized by its legs and thrown into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3350).

12930
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The person who enjoyed the most luxurious life in this world among the people of the Fire will be brought on the Day of Resurrection and dipped into the Fire once. It will then be said to him, ‘Son of Adam, did you ever experience anything good? Did you ever enjoy any comfort?’
He will reply, ‘No, by Allah, my Lord.’
The person who endured the greatest misery in this world among the people of Paradise will then be brought and dipped into Paradise once. It will be said to him, ‘Son of Adam, did you ever experience any misery? Did you ever endure any hardship?’
He will reply, ‘No, by Allah, my Lord. I never experienced any misery, nor did I ever see any hardship.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2807).

12931
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ set out with a group of his Companions, intending to travel to the market of ʿUkāẓ. At that time, the devils had been prevented from receiving news from heaven, and flaming missiles were sent against them.
The devils returned to their people, who asked:
“What has happened to you?”
They replied:
“We have been prevented from receiving news from heaven, and flaming missiles have been sent against us.”
Their people said:
“Nothing could have prevented you from the news of heaven except some new event. Travel throughout the eastern and western regions of the earth and discover what has prevented you from receiving the news of heaven.”
Those who traveled toward Tihāmah came upon the Prophet ﷺ while he was at Nakhlah on his way to the market of ʿUkāẓ, leading his Companions in the dawn prayer.
When they heard the Qur’an, they listened attentively and said:
“By Allah, this is what has prevented you from receiving news from heaven.”
When they returned to their people, they said:
“We have heard a wondrous Qur’an. It guides to sound judgment, so we have believed in it, and we will never associate anyone with our Lord.”
 [Sūrat al-Jinn 72:1–2]
Allah then revealed to His Prophet ﷺ:
“Say: It has been revealed to me that a group of the jinn listened.”
The Prophet ﷺ was informed through revelation of what the jinn had said.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (773) and Muslim (449).

12932
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The jinn used to ascend toward heaven to listen to revelation. Whenever they heard a single true statement, they would add nine falsehoods to it. The original statement would be true, while everything they added would be false.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sent, they were prevented from occupying their former listening posts. They mentioned this to Iblīs. Before that time, stars had not been used to drive them away.
Iblīs said:
“This can only be because of some event that has occurred upon the earth.”
He dispatched his forces, and they found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ standing in prayer between two mountains—according to the narrator, in Makkah.
They returned and informed Iblīs, who said:
“This is the event that has occurred upon the earth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1/274), al-Tirmidhī (3324), whose wording is given here, and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11626).

12933
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives”
was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ climbed Mount al-Ṣafā and began calling out:
“Children of Fihr! Children of ʿAdī!”
He called the various clans of Quraysh until they gathered.
Anyone unable to attend personally sent a representative to learn what was happening. Abū Lahab and the people of Quraysh arrived.
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Tell me: if I informed you that cavalry in the valley was preparing to attack you, would you believe me?”
They replied:
“Yes. We have never experienced anything from you except truthfulness.”
He said:
“Then I am a warner to you before the coming of a severe punishment.”
Abū Lahab said:
“May you perish for the rest of this day! Is this why you gathered us?”
The following verses were revealed:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and may he perish. Neither his wealth nor what he earned benefited him.”
 [Sūrat al-Masad 111:1–2]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4770) and Muslim (208). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12934
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives”
was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he said:
“People of Quraysh, purchase your own souls from Allah’s punishment, for I cannot benefit you before Allah in any way.
Children of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, I cannot benefit you before Allah in any way.
ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, I cannot benefit you before Allah in any way.
Ṣafiyyah, aunt of the Messenger of Allah, I cannot benefit you before Allah in any way.
Fāṭimah, daughter of the Messenger of Allah, ask me for whatever you wish from my wealth, but I cannot benefit you before Allah in any way.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4771) and Muslim (206). The wording is that of Muslim.

12935
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned Quraysh. They gathered, and he addressed them both generally and specifically, saying:
“Children of Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy, save yourselves from the Fire.
Children of Murrah ibn Kaʿb, save yourselves from the Fire.
Children of ʿAbd Shams, save yourselves from the Fire.
Children of ʿAbd Manāf, save yourselves from the Fire.
Children of Hāshim, save yourselves from the Fire.
Children of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, save yourselves from the Fire.
Fāṭimah, save yourself from the Fire. I possess no power to protect you from Allah, except that you have ties of kinship with me which I will properly maintain.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (204).

12936
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives”
was revealed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood upon al-Ṣafā and said:
“Fāṭimah daughter of Muḥammad, Ṣafiyyah daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, and children of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, I possess no power to protect you from Allah. Ask me for whatever you wish from my wealth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (205).

12937
Qabīṣah ibn Mukhāriq and Zuhayr ibn ʿAmr narrated:
When the verse:
“Warn your closest relatives”
was revealed, the Prophet of Allah ﷺ went to a mound upon a mountain, climbed its highest rock, and called out:
“Children of ʿAbd Manāf! I am a warner. My example in relation to you is like that of a man who sees an approaching enemy and rushes to warn his family. Fearing that the enemy will reach them before he does, he cries out: ‘O people, awake! An attack is upon you!’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (207).

12938
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
Some people asked the Prophet ﷺ about soothsayers. He replied:
“They are nothing.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, they sometimes tell us something that later proves true.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“That is a word of truth snatched by a jinn, who repeats it into the ear of his ally like the clucking of a chicken. They then mix with it more than one hundred lies.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7561) and Muslim (2228).

12939
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that she heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“The angels descend into the clouds and mention the matter that has been decreed in heaven. The devils secretly listen and hear it, then pass it to the soothsayers, who add one hundred lies of their own.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3210).

12940
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
While we were traveling with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at al-ʿArj, a poet appeared and began reciting poetry.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Seize the devil,”
or:
“Stop the devil. For a man’s body cavity to be filled with pus is better for him than for it to be filled with poetry.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2259).
This hadith applies specifically to this man, who may have been reciting poetry containing falsehood or indecency. The same ruling applies to anyone who uses poetry in this manner.
This is indicated by the words of Allah that follow:
“Except those who believe and perform righteous deeds.”
This is an exception from the general condemnation, as also indicated by the following statement of Ibn ʿAbbās:

12941
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“The poets are followed by those who are astray”:
Allah then excluded some of them from this ruling, saying:
“Except those who believe, perform righteous deeds, and remember Allah frequently.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5016) and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (871).
Supporting the statement of Ibn ʿAbbās is the fact that the Prophet ﷺ permitted Muslim poets to praise the Prophet ﷺ, call people to Islam, and defend the religion.
The phrase:
“He abrogated part of it”
means that Allah restricted and qualified the general ruling through the exception.
The statement of Ibn ʿAbbās may suggest that these verses were revealed in Madinah, even though it is authentically established that Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ was revealed in Makkah.
Al-Naḥḥās stated in al-Nāsikh wa-al-Mansūkh that Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ is Makkan except for these verses, which were revealed in Madinah.
This is supported by what Ibn Abī Shaybah narrated in al-Muṣannaf (26574), through Yaḥyā ibn Wāḍiḥ, from Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Yazīd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qusayṭ, from Abū al-Ḥasan al-Barrād:
When the verse:
“The poets are followed by those who are astray”
was revealed, ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah, Kaʿb ibn Mālik, and Ḥassān ibn Thābit came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ weeping. They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah has revealed this verse while He knows that we are poets.”
He replied:
“Recite what follows it:
‘Except those who believe and perform righteous deeds’—that refers to you.
‘And defend themselves after being wronged’—that also refers to you.”
These poets were among the Anṣār of Madinah. However, the chain is disconnected, because I have not found anyone who listed Abū al-Ḥasan al-Barrād, the freedman of Tamīm al-Dārī, among the Companions.

12942
Kaʿb ibn Mālik said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has revealed concerning poetry what He has revealed.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“The believer strives with both his sword and his tongue. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, the poetry with which you attack them is like the showering of arrows.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (27174), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (19/75), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5786).

12943
