Al-Barāʾ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said to Ḥassān:
“Satirize them—or answer their satire—and Gabriel is with you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3213) and Muslim (2486).

12944
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Indeed, some poetry contains wisdom.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6145).

12945
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated:
After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, the wives of the Prophet ﷺ wished to send ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān to Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq to ask him for their inheritance from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿĀʾishah said to them:
“Did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ not say:
‘We prophets are not inherited from. Whatever we leave behind is charity’?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Kalām wa-al-Ghībah wa-al-Tuqā (27), al-Bukhārī (6730), and Muslim (1758).

12946
Abū Dharr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ related from Allah, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“My servants, I have forbidden injustice for Myself and have made it forbidden among you, so do not wrong one another.
My servants, all of you are astray except those whom I guide, so ask Me for guidance and I will guide you.
My servants, all of you are hungry except those whom I feed, so ask Me for food and I will feed you.
My servants, all of you are unclothed except those whom I clothe, so ask Me for clothing and I will clothe you.
My servants, you commit sins by night and day, and I forgive all sins, so seek My forgiveness and I will forgive you.
My servants, you will never attain the ability to harm Me, nor will you attain the ability to benefit Me.
My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, were all as pious as the most pious heart of any one man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of any one man, that would not decrease My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, all stood together in one place and asked Me, and I gave every person what he requested, that would decrease what I possess no more than a needle decreases the sea when dipped into it.
My servants, these are only your deeds, which I record for you and then repay you for in full. Whoever finds good should praise Allah, and whoever finds something else should blame no one but himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2577).

12947
A man from Balhujaym narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, to what are you calling people?”
He replied:
“I call them to Allah alone: the One who, if harm touches you and you call upon Him, removes it from you; the One who, if you become lost in a barren land and call upon Him, returns you safely; and the One who, if a year of drought afflicts you and you call upon Him, causes vegetation to grow for you.”
I said:
“Then advise me.”
He replied:
“Never insult anyone. Do not regard any good deed as insignificant, even if it is meeting your brother with a cheerful face or pouring water from your bucket into the vessel of someone seeking water. Wear your lower garment to the middle of your shin; if you refuse, then down to your ankles. Beware of letting the lower garment hang beneath the ankles, for doing so is an expression of pride, and Allah does not love pride.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20636). The unnamed Companion was Jābir ibn Sulaym, as identified in the preceding chain (20635).

12948
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
One day, the Prophet ﷺ was sitting publicly among the people when a man came and asked:
“What is faith?”
He replied:
“Faith is that you believe in Allah, His angels, His Book, meeting Him, His messengers, and the final resurrection.”
The man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is Islam?”
He replied:
“Islam is that you worship Allah without associating anything with Him, establish the prescribed prayer, pay the obligatory zakāh, and fast Ramadan.”
He asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is excellence?”
He replied:
“It is to worship Allah as though you see Him, for although you do not see Him, He certainly sees you.”
He asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, when is the Hour?”
He replied:
“The one being questioned knows no more about it than the questioner. However, I will tell you some of its signs: when a slave woman gives birth to her master, that is among its signs; when the barefoot and unclothed become the leaders of the people, that is among its signs; and when the shepherds of dark-colored camels compete in constructing tall buildings, that is among its signs. It is among five matters known only to Allah.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is in the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
The man then departed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bring the man back to me.”
They went to bring him back but could not find anyone.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“That was Gabriel. He came to teach the people their religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4777) and Muslim (9). The wording is that of Muslim.

12949
Abū Ṭalḥah narrated:
On the Day of Badr, the Prophet of Allah ﷺ ordered that twenty-four leading men of Quraysh be thrown into one of the filthy, abandoned wells of Badr.
Whenever he gained victory over a people, he would remain at the battlefield for three nights.
On the third day at Badr, he ordered that his riding animal be prepared and its saddle secured. He then set out on foot, and his Companions followed him, saying:
“We think he is only going out for some need.”
He reached the edge of the well and began calling the dead by their names and the names of their fathers:
“So-and-so son of so-and-so! So-and-so son of so-and-so! Would it have pleased you if you had obeyed Allah and His Messenger? We have certainly found what our Lord promised us to be true. Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?”
ʿUmar asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how can you speak to bodies that possess no souls?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, you do not hear what I am saying any better than they do.”
Qatādah said:
“Allah brought them to life so that He caused them to hear his words as rebuke, humiliation, vengeance, grief, and regret.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3976) and Muslim (2875:78). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12950
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ left the dead of Badr for three days. He then came to them, stood over them, and called out:
“Abū Jahl ibn Hishām! Umayyah ibn Khalaf! ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah! Shaybah ibn Rabīʿah! Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true? I have certainly found what my Lord promised me to be true.”
ʿUmar heard the words of the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how can they hear, and how could they answer, when their bodies have decomposed?”
He replied:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, you do not hear what I am saying any better than they do, but they are unable to respond.”
He then ordered that their bodies be dragged away and thrown into the well of Badr.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2874:77).
Regarding the words:
“They were thrown into the well of Badr”:
Umayyah ibn Khalaf was not actually placed inside the well. Because he was very large, his body became swollen and his limbs came apart when they attempted to drag him. They therefore covered him with stones and earth until his body was concealed. However, he was close to the well and was included among those whom the Prophet ﷺ addressed.

12951
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ stood over the well of Badr and said:
“Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?”
He then said:
“They are now hearing what I say.”
When this was mentioned to ʿĀʾishah, she said:
“The Prophet ﷺ only said:
‘They now know that what I used to tell them was the truth.’”
She then recited:
“Indeed, you cannot make the dead hear,”
 [Sūrat al-Naml 27:80]
and continued to the end of the verse.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3980) and Muslim (932:26).

12952
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that those killed at Badr be thrown into the well, and they were cast into it, except for Umayyah ibn Khalaf. His body had swollen inside his armor until it filled it. When they attempted to move him, his body came apart, so they left him where he was and covered him with enough earth and stones to conceal him.
After the others had been thrown into the well, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood over them and said:
“People of the well, have you found what your Lord promised you to be true? I have certainly found what my Lord promised me to be true.”
His Companions asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are you speaking to people who are dead?”
He replied:
“They have certainly come to know that what I promised them was true.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“People report that he said, ‘They heard what I said to them,’ but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ actually said:
‘They have come to know.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26361), Ibn Ḥibbān (7088), and al-Ḥākim (3/224).
Al-Ḥākim said:
“It is authentic according to the conditions of Muslim.”
This hearing may have been one of the miracles granted to the Prophet ﷺ. These men had been among the fiercest deniers of his message, so Allah, the Exalted, willed that they hear the Prophet’s words as a source of grief and regret for them. This is supported by the preceding statement of Qatādah.

12953
Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When a servant is placed in his grave and his companions turn away and leave, he can hear the sound of their sandals. Two angels then come to him, make him sit, and ask: ‘What did you used to say concerning this man, Muḥammad ﷺ?’
He will reply: ‘I testify that he is the servant and Messenger of Allah.’
It will be said to him: ‘Look at the place you would have occupied in the Fire. Allah has replaced it for you with a place in Paradise.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“He will see both places.
As for the disbeliever or hypocrite, he will say: ‘I do not know. I merely used to repeat what the people said.’
It will be said to him: ‘You neither knew nor followed those who knew.’
He will then be struck between his ears with an iron hammer and will scream with a cry heard by everything near him except the two weighty species.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1338) and Muslim (2870).
The words:
“It is heard by everything near him except the two weighty species”
refer to humankind and the jinn. They do not hear what happens to the occupant of the grave. All of this occurs in a realm different from our present world.
Whoever claims that this proves that the deceased possesses ordinary worldly life inside the grave has contradicted what is outwardly and physically observable.
Life in the next realm—the realm of al-Barzakh—is known to us only through what Allah, the Exalted, and His Messenger ﷺ have informed us. It must not be measured according to the conditions of our worldly life.

12954
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Prompt your dying people to say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (917).
The word “deceased” here refers to a person whose death is approaching but who is still alive. For further details, see The Book of Funerals.
As for prompting the deceased after death, this has not been authentically established.
What is authentically established is seeking forgiveness for the deceased after burial and supplicating that Allah keep him firm. Abū Dāwūd (3221) narrated from ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān:
When the Prophet ﷺ finished burying a deceased person, he would stand beside the grave and say:
“Seek forgiveness for your brother and ask that he be kept firm, for he is now being questioned.”
Its chain of transmission is good.
For further details, see The Book of Funerals, which contains many issues related to this subject.

12955
Ḥudhayfah ibn Usayd al-Ghifārī narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ looked in upon us while we were discussing something and asked:
“What are you discussing?”
They replied:
“We are discussing the Hour.”
He said:
“It will not occur until you see ten signs before it.”
He then mentioned the smoke, the Antichrist, the Beast, the rising of the sun from the west, the descent of Jesus son of Mary ﷺ, Gog and Magog, and three landslides: one in the east, one in the west, and one in the Arabian Peninsula. The last of these signs will be a fire emerging from Yemen that will drive the people toward their place of gathering.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2901).

12956
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hasten to perform righteous deeds before six things occur: the rising of the sun from the west, the smoke, the Antichrist, the Beast, the personal fate of one of you, or the general affair.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2947).

12957
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
A Bedouin came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“What is the Trumpet?”
He replied:
“It is a horn into which one will blow.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4742), al-Tirmidhī (2430, 3244), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7312), and narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/436).
The words:
“Those in the heavens and those on the earth will be terrified”
refer to fear and alarm caused by something terrifying.
The scholars differed concerning those excluded by the words:
“Except whomever Allah wills.”
Many scholars held that they are the martyrs, because they are alive with their Lord and receiving provision, even though the people of this world count them among the dead. They are mentioned in the following narration of Abū Hurayrah:

12958
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ asked Gabriel, peace be upon him, concerning the verse:
“The Trumpet will be blown, and all those in the heavens and on the earth will fall unconscious except whomever Allah wills. Then it will be blown again, and suddenly they will be standing, looking on.”
 [Sūrat al-Zumar 39:68]
He asked:
“Who are those whom Allah does not will to fall unconscious?”
Gabriel replied:
“They are the martyrs, wearing their swords around the Throne of the Most Compassionate. On the Day of Resurrection, the angels will receive them and carry them toward the place of gathering upon noble mounts made of ruby. Their saddles will be softer than silk, and the length of each stride will extend as far as a man can see.
They will travel through Paradise and, after a lengthy journey, will say: ‘Come, let us go to our Lord, Mighty and Majestic, and observe how He judges between His creation.’
My Lord will laugh toward them, and whenever He laughs toward a servant in any place, that servant will not be called to account.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā in al-Maṭālib al-ʿĀliyah (3702).
As for the words in Sūrat al-Zumar:
“The Trumpet will be blown, and all those in the heavens and on the earth will fall unconscious except whomever Allah wills,”
 [Sūrat al-Zumar 39:68]
the falling unconscious refers to the death that follows the initial terror. If the exception refers to a particular person, then it may refer to Moses, peace be upon him.

12959
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The people will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will be the first to regain consciousness. I will then see Moses holding one of the supports of the Throne. I do not know whether he regained consciousness before me or whether he was spared in compensation for his falling unconscious at Mount Sinai.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3398) and Muslim (2374:162).

12960
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Two men insulted one another: one was a Muslim and the other was a Jew.
The Muslim said:
“By the One who chose Muḥammad above all the worlds.”
The Jew replied:
“By the One who chose Moses above all the worlds.”
The Muslim then raised his hand and slapped the Jew across the face.
The Jew went to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him of what had happened between him and the Muslim. The Prophet ﷺ summoned the Muslim and questioned him, and he related what had happened.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not declare me superior to Moses in this manner. The people will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will fall unconscious with them. I will be the first to regain consciousness, and Moses will be grasping the side of the Throne. I do not know whether he will have been among those who fell unconscious and regained consciousness before me, or whether he will have been among those whom Allah excluded.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2411) and Muslim (2373:160).

12961
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
On the Day of the Conquest of Makkah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no migration after the conquest, but there remains striving and sincere intention. When you are called to mobilize, then go forth.”
He also said on the Day of the Conquest of Makkah:
“Allah made this city sacred on the day He created the heavens and the earth. It therefore remains sacred by the sanctity granted to it by Allah until the Day of Resurrection.
Fighting within it was not made lawful for anyone before me, and it was made lawful for me only for a brief period during one day. It remains sacred by the sanctity granted to it by Allah until the Day of Resurrection.
Its thorny plants must not be cut, its game must not be frightened, its lost property must not be picked up except by one who will publicly announce it, and its fresh vegetation must not be cut.”
Al-ʿAbbās said:
“O Messenger of Allah, except for idhkhir grass, because it is needed by their metalworkers and for their houses.”
He replied:
“Except for idhkhir grass.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1834) and Muslim (1353:445). The wording is that of Muslim.

12962
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
A Jewish man from al-Ḥīrah asked me:
“Which of the two terms did Moses fulfill?”
I replied:
“I do not know. I will wait until I meet the great scholar of the Arabs and ask him.”
When I arrived, I asked Ibn ʿAbbās. He replied:
“He fulfilled the longer and better of the two terms. When a messenger of Allah gives his word, he fulfills it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2684).
What Ibn ʿAbbās stated was not merely his own reasoning. It was also narrated as a statement of the Prophet ﷺ. Perhaps he related it on two separate occasions.

12963
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ was asked:
“Which of the two terms did Moses fulfill?”
He replied:
“The more complete and more faithful of the two.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2245).

12964
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī attributed the following statement to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Allah, Blessed and Exalted, did not destroy an entire people with a punishment from the heavens or the earth except before Moses.”
He then recited:
“We certainly gave Moses the Scripture after We had destroyed the earlier generations.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2248).
The earlier nations whom Allah punished before giving Moses, peace be upon him, the Torah include:
First: The People of Noah, Peace Be upon Him
Allah says:
“We certainly sent Noah to his people, and he remained among them for a thousand years minus fifty. Then the flood seized them while they were wrongdoers.”
 [Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:14]
Second: ʿĀd, the People of Hūd, Peace Be upon Him
Allah says:
“As for ʿĀd, they were destroyed by a furious, bitterly cold wind which He imposed upon them for seven nights and eight consecutive days. You would have seen the people lying fallen as though they were hollow trunks of palm trees.”
 [Sūrat al-Ḥāqqah 69:6–7]
Third: Thamūd, the People of Ṣāliḥ, Peace Be upon Him
Allah says:
“As for Thamūd, they were destroyed by the overwhelming blast.”
 [Sūrat al-Ḥāqqah 69:5]
Fourth: The People of Lot, Peace Be upon Him
Allah says:
“We saved him and his entire family, except an old woman who remained behind. Then We utterly destroyed the others and rained upon them a rain of punishment. How terrible was the rain of those who had been warned.”
 [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:170–173]
Fifth: The People of Shuʿayb, Peace Be upon Him
Allah says:
“The earthquake seized them, and by morning they lay lifeless in their homes. Those who denied Shuʿayb became as though they had never lived there. Those who denied Shuʿayb were themselves the losers.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:91–92]
Allah also says:
“Remember when We parted the sea for you, saved you, and drowned Pharaoh’s people while you watched.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:50]
All of these peoples were destroyed before the Torah was revealed to Moses, peace be upon him. Allah therefore says:
“We certainly gave Moses the Scripture after We had destroyed the earlier generations, as enlightenment for the people, guidance, and mercy, so that they might take heed.”
 [Sūrat al-Qaṣaṣ 28:43]

12965
Rifāʿah al-Quraẓī said:
“This verse was revealed concerning ten people, and I was one of them:
‘We have repeatedly conveyed the Word to them so that they may take heed. Those to whom We gave the Scripture before it believe in it. When it is recited to them, they say: We believe in it. It is certainly the truth from our Lord. Even before it, we had submitted to Allah. Those will be given their reward twice because of their patience. They repel evil with good, and they spend from what We have provided them.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (18/276), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (9/2987–2988), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (5/46–47).
Its chain is authentic.
The words:
“Those will be given their reward twice”
mean that the People of the Scripture who believed in their own prophet and then believed in the Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ receive a double reward, as stated in the authentic hadith:

12966
Abū Mūsā narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Three people will receive a double reward: a man from the People of the Scripture who believed in his prophet and then believed in Muḥammad ﷺ; an enslaved servant who fulfills the rights of Allah and the rights of his masters; and a man who possesses a slave woman, educates her well, teaches her well, then emancipates her and marries her. He will receive two rewards.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (97) and Muslim (154). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

12967
Abū Umāmah narrated:
I was beneath the riding animal of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of the Conquest when he delivered a fine and beautiful address. Among what he said was:
“Whoever accepts Islam from among the two Peoples of the Scripture will receive his reward twice. He will have the same rights as us and bear the same obligations as us. Whoever accepts Islam from among the polytheists will receive his reward, have the same rights as us, and bear the same obligations as us.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22234).
The chain contains Ibn Lahīʿah, concerning whom there is well-known criticism because of his later confusion. However, al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (22/441) and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ Mushkil al-Āthār (2571) narrated it through ʿAbdullāh ibn Wahb from ʿAbdullāh ibn Lahīʿah with the same chain.
Ibn Wahb’s narration from Ibn Lahīʿah is more reliable than the narrations of others from him.
Ibn Lahīʿah was also corroborated. Al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (22/441), al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ Mushkil al-Āthār (2571), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/224) narrated it through al-Layth ibn Saʿd from Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān with the same chain.
Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and his shaykh al-Qāsim—namely Ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Dimashqī—are both considered good in hadith.

12968
Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib narrated from his father:
When death approached Abū Ṭālib, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to him and found Abū Jahl and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah beside him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Uncle, say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ a statement with which I may plead on your behalf before Allah.”
Abū Jahl and ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Umayyah said:
“Will you abandon the religion of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ continued presenting the testimony of faith to him, while the two men continued repeating their statement.
The last words Abū Ṭālib spoke to them were:
“I remain upon the religion of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
He refused to say:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Allah, I will continue seeking forgiveness for you unless I am forbidden from doing so.”
Allah then revealed:
“It is not for the Prophet and those who believe to seek forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they are close relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are inhabitants of the Blaze.”
 [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:113]
Allah also revealed concerning Abū Ṭālib, addressing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“You cannot guide whomever you love, but Allah guides whomever He wills, and He knows best who is rightly guided.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4772) and Muslim (24). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12969
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to his uncle:
“Say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ and I will testify on your behalf because of it on the Day of Resurrection.”
His uncle replied:
“Were it not that Quraysh would shame me and say that fear at the approach of death caused me to say it, I would certainly comfort your eyes by uttering it.”
Allah then revealed:
“You cannot guide whomever you love, but Allah guides whomever He wills, and He knows best who is rightly guided.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (25:42).

12970
Al-Mustawrid ibn Shaddād narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Allah, this world in comparison with the Hereafter is only like one of you dipping this finger”—Yaḥyā pointed with his index finger—“into the sea. Let him then observe how much water it brings back.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2858).

12971
ʿAwn ibn Abī Juḥayfah narrated from his father:
The Prophet ﷺ established brotherhood between Salmān and Abū al-Dardāʾ.
Salmān visited Abū al-Dardāʾ and saw Umm al-Dardāʾ wearing neglected clothing. He asked her:
“What is the matter with you?”
She replied:
“Your brother Abū al-Dardāʾ has no interest in worldly life.”
Abū al-Dardāʾ arrived and prepared food for Salmān, saying:
“Eat.”
Salmān replied:
“I am fasting.”
Abū al-Dardāʾ said:
“I will not eat until you eat.”
So Salmān ate.
When night came, Abū al-Dardāʾ attempted to stand for prayer. Salmān said:
“Sleep.”
He slept, then later attempted to rise again. Salmān again said:
“Sleep.”
During the final part of the night, Salmān said:
“Rise now.”
They both prayed.
Salmān then said:
“Your Lord has a right over you, your own self has a right over you, and your family has a right over you. Therefore, give everyone who possesses a right his due.”
Abū al-Dardāʾ went to the Prophet ﷺ and mentioned this to him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Salmān has spoken the truth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1968).

12972
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ—or Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ—said:
“While a man was walking in a fine garment, admiring himself, with his hair neatly combed, Allah caused the earth to swallow him. He will continue sinking into it until the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5789) and Muslim (2088). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
It has been said that this man was Qārūn.
Other hadiths with the same meaning have been mentioned in their respective places.

12973
Ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has distributed your character traits among you just as He has distributed your provision. Allah gives worldly possessions to those He loves and those He does not love, but He grants faith only to those He loves.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/33–34).
Al-Ḥākim said: “This hadith is authentic.”
Its discussion is treated in detail in the Book of Faith.

12974
ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said one day during a sermon:
“Allah has revealed to me that you must be humble, so that no one boasts over another and no one transgresses against another.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865:64).

12975
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one who has an atom’s weight of arrogance in his heart will enter Paradise.”
A man asked:
“A person likes his clothing and footwear to be beautiful.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah is Beautiful and loves beauty. Arrogance is rejecting the truth and looking down upon people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (91).

12976
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Will surely return you to a place of return”:
“It means: to Makkah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4773).

12977
Muṣʿab ibn Saʿd narrated from his father that several verses of the Qur’an were revealed concerning him.
He said:
My mother swore that she would never speak to me until I rejected my religion, and that she would neither eat nor drink.
She said:
“You claim that Allah has commanded you to treat your parents well. I am your mother, and I command you to abandon this religion.”
She remained in that condition for three days until she lost consciousness from exhaustion. One of her sons, named ʿUmārah, stood and gave her water. She then began supplicating against Saʿd.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“We have instructed the human being to treat his parents well.”
 [Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:8]
And:
“But if they strive to make you associate with Me that of which you have no knowledge…”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:15]
The same passage contains:
“Accompany them in this world with kindness.”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:15]
Saʿd continued:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ obtained a large amount of war booty, including a sword. I took it and brought it to the Messenger ﷺ, saying:
“Grant me this sword as an additional share, for you know my condition and service.”
He replied:
“Return it to the place from which you took it.”
I went back, but when I was about to place it among the collected spoils, my soul reproached me. I returned to him and said:
“Give it to me.”
He raised his voice and said:
“Return it to the place from which you took it.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed:
“They ask you concerning the spoils of war.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:1]
Saʿd continued:
I became ill and sent for the Prophet ﷺ. He came to me, and I said:
“Allow me to distribute all my wealth however I wish.”
He refused.
I said:
“Then one half?”
He refused.
I said:
“Then one third?”
He remained silent. Thereafter, bequeathing one third became permissible.
Saʿd continued:
I passed by a group of the Anṣār and Emigrants. They said:
“Come, we will feed you and give you wine to drink.”
This occurred before wine was prohibited.
I joined them in a garden. They had the roasted head of a camel and a wineskin. I ate and drank with them.
The Anṣār and Emigrants were then mentioned, and I said:
“The Emigrants are better than the Anṣār.”
One man picked up one of the jawbones of the camel’s head and struck me with it, injuring my nose.
I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed concerning me and the matter of wine:
“Wine, gambling, stone altars, and divining arrows are only filth from the work of Satan.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:90]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1748:43).
In another narration: “He struck Saʿd’s nose with it and split it.” Saʿd’s nose was therefore mafzūr, meaning split. His mother was Ḥamnah bint Abī Sufyān ibn Umayyah ibn ʿAbd Shams.

12978
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some people from Makkah accepted Islam but concealed their faith. On the Day of Badr, the polytheists forced them to go out with their army. Some of them were wounded and others were killed.
The Muslims said:
“These companions of ours were Muslims and were forced to participate.”
They therefore sought forgiveness for them.
Allah then revealed:
“Indeed, those whose souls the angels take while they are wronging themselves…”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:97]
to the end of the verse.
The Muslims wrote this verse to those who remained in Makkah, informing them that they no longer had an excuse.
They attempted to leave, but the polytheists caught them and subjected them to persecution. Allah then revealed:
“Among the people are those who say, ‘We believe in Allah,’ but when they are harmed for the sake of Allah, they treat the persecution of people like the punishment of Allah…”
to the end of the verse.
The Muslims wrote this revelation to them. They left again, having despaired of all good.
Allah then revealed concerning them:
“Then indeed your Lord, concerning those who emigrated after being persecuted, then struggled and remained patient—indeed, your Lord after that is Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:110]
The Muslims wrote to them:
“Allah has now made a way out for you.”
They departed. The polytheists overtook them, and they fought until some escaped and others were killed.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (14/379–380 and 18/366), whose wording is given here, and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2204).
Al-Bazzār said: “We do not know anyone who narrates it from ʿAmr except Muḥammad ibn Sharīk.”
I say: Its chain is authentic. Muḥammad ibn Sharīk Abū ʿUthmān al-Makkī is trustworthy; Ibn Maʿīn, Aḥmad, Abū Zurʿah, and others declared him trustworthy. Al-Haythamī said in al-Majmaʿ (7/9): “Al-Bukhārī narrated part of it, and al-Bazzār narrated it; its narrators are the narrators of the Ṣaḥīḥ.”

12979
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever calls others to guidance will receive a reward equal to the rewards of all who follow him, without that reducing their rewards in the slightest. Whoever calls others to misguidance will bear a sin equal to the sins of all who follow him, without that reducing their sins in the slightest.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2674).

12980
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No soul is unjustly killed except that the first son of Adam bears a share of its blood, because he was the first person to establish the practice of murder.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6867) and Muslim (1677:27).
Likewise, on the Day of Resurrection, a wrongdoer’s good deeds will be given to those whom he wronged. If his good deeds are exhausted, the sins of the oppressed will be placed upon him. He will thus carry his own sins together with the sins of those he wronged.

12981
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Do you know who the bankrupt person is?”
They replied:
“Among us, the bankrupt person is one who possesses neither money nor property.”
He said:
“The bankrupt person from my nation will come on the Day of Resurrection with prayer, fasting, and zakāh. However, he will also come having insulted this person, falsely accused that person, consumed another’s wealth, shed another’s blood, and struck another.
This person will be given some of his good deeds, and that person will be given some of his good deeds. If his good deeds are exhausted before all his liabilities have been settled, some of their sins will be taken and cast upon him. He will then be thrown into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2581).

12982
Abū Umāmah narrated:
After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had conveyed the message with which he had been sent, he said:
“Beware of injustice. On the Day of Resurrection, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, will swear and say: ‘By My might, no injustice will pass before Me today without judgment.’
A caller will then announce: ‘Where is so-and-so, son of so-and-so?’
He will come with good deeds following him like mountains. The people will raise their eyes toward them until he stands before Allah, the Most Compassionate, Mighty and Majestic.
Allah will then command the caller to announce: ‘Whoever possesses a claim or has suffered an injustice at the hands of so-and-so, son of so-and-so, let him come forward.’
They will come until they gather standing before the Most Compassionate.
The Most Compassionate will say: ‘Settle the claims against My servant.’
They will ask: ‘How shall we settle them on his behalf?’
He will say: ‘Take from his good deeds and give them to those he wronged.’
They will continue taking from his good deeds until none remains, while some claimants are still waiting.
Allah will say: ‘Settle the claims against My servant.’
They will say: ‘He has no good deeds remaining.’
Allah will say: ‘Take from their sins and place them upon him.’”
The Prophet ﷺ then cited the noble verse as evidence:
“They will certainly bear their own burdens and other burdens along with their burdens, and on the Day of Resurrection they will certainly be questioned concerning what they used to fabricate.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (9/3039–3040).
Its chain is good because of Hishām ibn ʿAmmār, who is considered good in hadith, and because of ʿUthmān ibn Abī al-ʿĀtikah, who is also considered good in hadith in his narrations from those other than ʿAlī ibn Zayd al-Alhānī.
The phrase “the Prophet ﷺ then cited this verse” means that he used it as evidence.

12983
Ibn al-Daylamī narrated:
I came to Ubayy ibn Kaʿb and said:
“Something concerning divine decree has entered my heart. Tell me something through which Allah may remove it from my heart.”
He replied:
“If Allah were to punish all the inhabitants of His heavens and earth, He would punish them without wronging them. If He were to show them mercy, His mercy would be better for them than their deeds.
If you spent an amount of gold equal to Mount Uḥud in the path of Allah, Allah would not accept it from you until you believed in divine decree and knew that whatever struck you could never have missed you, and whatever missed you could never have struck you.
If you died believing otherwise, you would enter the Fire.”
I then came to ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd, and he said the same. I came to Ḥudhayfah ibn al-Yamān, and he said the same. I then came to Zayd ibn Thābit, and he narrated the same from the Prophet ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4699), Ibn Mājah (77), and Ibn Ḥibbān (727).
Its chain is good because of Abū Sinān—Saʿīd ibn Sinān al-Burjumī—who is considered good in hadith. Its discussion is treated in detail in the Book of Faith.

12984
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“So-and-so prays during the night, but when morning comes, he steals.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“What you have mentioned—his prayer—will eventually prevent him from doing that.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (9778), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (720), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2560).
Its chain is authentic, and its detailed discussion has already appeared in the Book of Prayer.

12985
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The People of the Scripture used to read the Torah in Hebrew and explain it in Arabic to the Muslims.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not affirm what the People of the Scripture tell you, nor declare it false. Rather, say:
‘We believe in Allah and in what was revealed to us, and in what was revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, and the tribes; and in what was given to Moses and Jesus; and in what was given to the prophets from their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and to Him we submit.’”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:136]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4485), (7362), and (7542).

12986
Abū Namlah al-Anṣārī narrated:
While he was sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the presence of a Jewish man, a funeral procession passed by.
The Jewish man asked:
“Muḥammad, does this deceased person speak?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah knows best.”
The Jewish man said:
“It does speak.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Whatever the People of the Scripture tell you, neither affirm it nor declare it false. Say: ‘We believe in Allah and His messengers.’ If what they say is false, you will not have affirmed it, and if it is true, you will not have denied it.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3644), Aḥmad (17225), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6257).
Ibn Abī Namlah’s name is Namlah. I did not find anyone who mentioned him with either criticism or accreditation, except that Ibn Ḥibbān listed him in al-Thiqāt. A number of narrators reported from him, and al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar graded this hadith of his good in Fatḥ al-Bārī (13/334).

12987
ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said one day during a sermon:
“Indeed, my Lord has commanded me to teach you what you do not know from what He has taught me this day…”
He also related that Allah said:
“I sent you only to test you and to test others through you. I revealed to you a Book that water cannot wash away, which you recite while asleep and awake…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865).
The words:
“Water cannot wash it away”
mean that the Qur’an will never disappear from the earth. It will remain preserved in hearts and written texts until the Day of Resurrection, without alteration or substitution.
This is among the distinctive qualities of the Noble Qur’an. As for the scriptures of other religions, although copies of them remain, they have been altered, and alteration has continued within them.
The words:
“You recite it while asleep and awake”
are addressed to the Prophet ﷺ. The Qur’an remained present with him in both sleep and wakefulness, and this was one of his distinctive qualities.

12988
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every prophet was given signs through which people believed in him. What I was given was revelation that Allah revealed to me. I therefore hope to have the greatest number of followers among them on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4981) and Muslim (152). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

12989
Abū Mālik al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“In Paradise there is a chamber whose exterior can be seen from its interior and whose interior can be seen from its exterior. Allah has prepared it for the one who feeds others, speaks gently, fasts regularly, and prays during the night while the people are asleep.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22905), authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (2137), and Ibn Ḥibbān (509).

12990
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The Romans have been defeated in the nearest land”:
They were defeated, and afterward they became victorious.
The polytheists wanted the Persians to prevail over the Romans because both the Persians and the Makkan polytheists worshipped idols. The Muslims wanted the Romans to prevail over the Persians because the Romans were People of the Scripture.
This was mentioned to Abū Bakr, and Abū Bakr mentioned it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“They will certainly be victorious.”
Abū Bakr informed the polytheists, and they said:
“Set a fixed period between us and you. If we prevail, we will receive such-and-such, and if you prevail, you will receive such-and-such.”
Abū Bakr fixed the period at five years, but the Romans had not become victorious by then. He mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ, who said:
“Why did you not make it less than ten?”
The narrator understood him to mean ten years.
Abū Saʿīd said:
“The term a few years means anything less than ten.”
The Romans later became victorious.
Ibn ʿAbbās said that this is the meaning of the words of Allah:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The Romans have been defeated…”
until:
“On that Day the believers will rejoice in the victory granted by Allah. He grants victory to whomever He wills.”
Sufyān said:
“I heard that the Romans became victorious over them on the Day of Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3193), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11325), Aḥmad (2495), and al-Ḥākim (2/410).
Its chain is authentic. Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is a good, authentic, strange hadith; we know it only through the narration of Sufyān al-Thawrī from Ḥabīb ibn Abī ʿAmrah.”
Al-Ḥākim said: “This hadith is authentic according to the conditions of the two Shaykhs.”

12991
Niyār ibn Mukram al-Aslamī narrated:
When the following verses were revealed:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The Romans have been defeated in the nearest land, yet after their defeat they will be victorious within a few years,”
the Persians were at that time dominant over the Romans.
The Muslims wanted the Romans to prevail because both the Muslims and Romans believed in revealed scripture. This is referred to in the words of Allah:
“On that Day the believers will rejoice in the victory granted by Allah. He grants victory to whomever He wills, and He is the Almighty, the Most Merciful.”
Quraysh wanted the Persians to prevail because neither they nor the Persians followed a revealed scripture or believed in resurrection.
When Allah revealed these verses, Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq went throughout Makkah proclaiming:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The Romans have been defeated in the nearest land, yet after their defeat they will be victorious within a few years.”
Some men of Quraysh said to Abū Bakr:
“Then let this be a matter between us and you. Your companion claims that the Romans will defeat Persia within a few years. Will you not wager with us over it?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
This occurred before wagering was prohibited.
Abū Bakr and the polytheists agreed upon a wager. They asked him:
“How many years will you set? The expression ‘a few’ ranges from three to nine years, so choose a midpoint between us.”
They agreed upon six years.
The six years passed before the Romans had become victorious, so the polytheists took Abū Bakr’s stake.
When the seventh year began, the Romans defeated the Persians. The Muslims criticized Abū Bakr for setting the period at only six years, since Allah had said:
“Within a few years.”
Many people accepted Islam at that time.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3194).
Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is an authentic, good, strange hadith from the narration of Niyār ibn Mukram; we know it only through the narration of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī al-Zinād.”
Al-Bayhaqī also narrated it in al-Asmāʾ wa-al-Ṣifāt (510) by another route from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī al-Zinād with similar wording, and said: “This chain is authentic.”
I say: Its chain is good because of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī al-Zinād, who is considered good in hadith.
The commentators mentioned that the person who wagered with Abū Bakr was Ubayy ibn Khalaf and that the wager consisted of five young she-camels.
When Ubayy ibn Khalaf feared that Abū Bakr might leave Makkah, he approached and detained him, saying:
“I fear that you may leave Makkah, so appoint a guarantor for me.”
Abū Bakr’s son ʿAbdullāh acted as his guarantor.
When Ubayy ibn Khalaf later intended to leave for the Battle of Uḥud, ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Bakr approached and detained him, saying:
“By Allah, I will not release you until you appoint a guarantor for me.”
Ubayy therefore appointed a guarantor.
Ubayy then went to Uḥud. He later returned and died in Makkah from the wound inflicted upon him by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when Ubayy confronted him.
The Romans became victorious over Persia around the time of al-Ḥudaybiyah, approximately seven years after the wager was made.

12992
Masrūq narrated:
While a man was speaking among the people of Kindah, he said:
“A smoke will come on the Day of Resurrection and seize the hearing and sight of the hypocrites, while affecting the believer like a cold.”
We became alarmed, so I went to Ibn Masʿūd. He had been reclining, but he became angry and sat upright.
He said:
“Whoever possesses knowledge should speak, and whoever does not know should say, ‘Allah knows best.’ Part of knowledge is that a person says concerning what he does not know, ‘I do not know.’
Allah said to His Prophet ﷺ:
‘Say: I ask you for no payment for this, nor am I one of those who pretend.’”
 [Sūrat Ṣād 38:86]
Quraysh delayed accepting Islam, so the Prophet ﷺ supplicated against them:
“O Allah, assist me against them with seven years like the seven years of Joseph.”
A famine then seized them until they suffered destruction, eating dead animals and bones. A man would see between the sky and earth what appeared like smoke.
Abū Sufyān came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Muḥammad, you came commanding us to maintain family ties, yet your people are perishing. Supplicate to Allah for them.”
Ibn Masʿūd then recited:
“So await the Day when the sky will bring visible smoke…”
until:
“Indeed, you will return.”
 [Sūrat al-Dukhān 44:10–15]
He asked:
“Would the punishment of the Hereafter be removed from them after it came, only for them then to return to disbelief?”
The words of Allah:
“On the Day We seize them with the greatest seizure, We will certainly take vengeance,”
 [Sūrat al-Dukhān 44:16]
refer to the Day of Badr.
The word:
“Inevitable,”
 [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:77]
also refers to the Day of Badr.
The prophecy:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The Romans have been defeated…”
until:
“They will be victorious,”
had already been fulfilled.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4774) and Muslim (2798). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

12993
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“Five things have already occurred: the smoke, the inevitable punishment, the victory of the Romans, the mighty seizure, and the splitting of the moon.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4767) and Muslim (2798:41). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

12994
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever says one hundred times during a day, ‘Glory and praise belong to Allah,’ will have his sins erased even if they are like the foam of the sea.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Book of the Qur’an (21), al-Bukhārī (6405), and Muslim (2691).

12995
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever says one hundred times in the morning and one hundred times in the evening, ‘Glory and praise belong to Allah,’ no one will bring anything better on the Day of Resurrection except someone who said the same or added to it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2692).

12996
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah says: ‘The son of Adam denies Me, although he has no right to do so, and he insults Me, although he has no right to do so.
His denial of Me is his statement: “He will not recreate me as He created me the first time,” although the first creation is no easier for Me than recreating it.
His insult of Me is his statement that Allah has taken a child, whereas I am the One, the Eternal Refuge. I neither beget nor was I begotten, and no one is comparable to Me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4974).

12997
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every newborn is born upon the natural disposition. It is then his parents who make him Jewish, Christian, or Magian, just as an animal gives birth to a complete and sound animal. Do you perceive any mutilation in it?”
He then recited:
“The natural disposition of Allah upon which He created humanity. There is no alteration in the creation of Allah. That is the upright religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4775) and Muslim (2658). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is shorter.

12998
ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said one day during a sermon:
“Indeed, my Lord has commanded me to teach you what you do not know from what He has taught me this day: every possession I have granted to a servant is lawful. I created all My servants inclining naturally toward the truth, but the devils came to them and diverted them from their religion. They prohibited for them what I had made lawful and commanded them to associate with Me that for which I had sent down no authority…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865).

12999
Ṣuhayb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How remarkable is the affair of the believer! All of his affairs are good, and this applies to no one except the believer. If prosperity reaches him, he gives thanks, and that is good for him. If adversity reaches him, he remains patient, and that is good for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2999).
Al-Bayhaqī narrated it through another chain (3/375), adding at the end:
“Every decree of Allah for the Muslims is good.”

13000
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever gives in charity the equivalent of a single date from lawful earnings—and Allah accepts only what is pure—Allah accepts it with His right hand and then nurtures it for its owner just as one of you raises his young horse, until it becomes like a mountain.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1410) and Muslim (1014).

13001
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed us:
“Luqmān the Wise, peace be upon him, used to say: ‘When Allah, Mighty and Majestic, is entrusted with something, He preserves it.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (5605–5606) and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (517–518).
Regarding the words:
“My son, do not associate anything with Allah. Indeed, polytheism is a tremendous injustice”:

13002
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Which sin is greatest in the sight of Allah?”
He replied:
“That you appoint a rival to Allah even though He created you.”
I said:
“That is indeed tremendous. Then which?”
He replied:
“That you kill your child out of fear that he will share your food.”
I asked:
“Then which?”
He replied:
“That you commit adultery with your neighbor’s wife.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4477) and Muslim (86).

13003
ʿAbdullāh narrated:
When the verse:
“Those who believe and do not mix their faith with injustice,”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:82]
was revealed, it became difficult for the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
They asked:
“Which of us does not wrong himself?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“It is not as you think. It means what Luqmān said to his son:
‘My son, do not associate anything with Allah. Indeed, polytheism is a tremendous injustice.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4776) and Muslim (124). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

13004
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“May his nose be rubbed in the dust! May his nose be rubbed in the dust! May his nose be rubbed in the dust!”
He was asked:
“Who, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The one who reaches the time when one or both of his parents are elderly but still fails to enter Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2551).

13005
Abū Bakrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Shall I inform you of the gravest major sins?”
We replied:
“Certainly, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Associating partners with Allah and disobeying one’s parents.”
He had been reclining, but then sat upright and said:
“And indeed, false speech and false testimony! Indeed, false speech and false testimony!”
He continued repeating it until I thought:
“He will not stop.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5976) and Muslim (87). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13006
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which people suffer the most severe trials?”
He replied:
“The prophets, then those most like them, followed by those most like them. A person is tested in proportion to the strength of his religion. If his religion is firm, his trial will be severe. If there is weakness in his religion, he will be tested according to the level of his religion. Trials will continue to afflict a servant until they leave him walking upon the earth without a single sin.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2398), Ibn Mājah (4023), and Aḥmad (1481), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2900).

13007
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one possessing an atom’s weight of arrogance in his heart will enter Paradise.”
A man asked:
“A person likes his clothing and footwear to be beautiful.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah is Beautiful and loves beauty. Arrogance is rejecting the truth and looking down upon people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (91).

13008
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There may be a disheveled person who is driven away from people’s doors, yet if he were to swear an oath by Allah, Allah would certainly fulfill it for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2622).

13009
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
One day, the Prophet ﷺ was sitting publicly among the people when a man approached him and asked:
“What is faith?”
He replied:
“Faith is that you believe in Allah, His angels, His Book, meeting Him, His messengers, and the final resurrection.”
The man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is Islam?”
He replied:
“Islam is that you worship Allah without associating anything with Him, establish the prescribed prayer, pay the obligatory zakāh, and fast during Ramadan.”
He asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is excellence?”
He replied:
“It is that you worship Allah as though you see Him, for although you do not see Him, He certainly sees you.”
He asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, when will the Hour occur?”
He replied:
“The one being questioned knows no more than the questioner. However, I will inform you of its signs: when a slave woman gives birth to her master, that is among its signs; when barefoot and unclothed people become leaders, that is among its signs; and when the shepherds of dark-colored camels compete in constructing tall buildings, that is among its signs. It is among five matters known only to Allah.”
He then recited:
“Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.”
The man then departed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bring the man back to me.”
They went to bring him back but could not see anyone.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“That was Gabriel. He came to teach the people their religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4777) and Muslim (9). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.
Muslim also narrated it through another chain (10), adding at the beginning that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Ask me.”
The people were too awed to question him, so a man came and sat beside his knees. He asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what is Islam?”
The narration continues similarly. In the definition of faith, he added:
“And that you believe in the entirety of divine decree.”
The remainder of the hadith is similar to the preceding version.
Regarding the words of Allah:
“And He sends down the rain,”
al-ghayth is rain that brings abundant benefit and causes the vegetation of the earth to grow.
Complete knowledge of this rain belongs to Allah: what He causes it to revive, what it does not revive, when it will descend, the quantity in which it will descend, how much of it will be beneficial, and how much will be harmful.
This comprehensive and all-encompassing knowledge belongs exclusively to Allah, the Exalted. The fact that a human being may discover some limited details of this unseen matter does not negate Allah’s complete knowledge of it.

13010
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The keys of the unseen are five, known only to Allah: no one knows what will happen tomorrow; no one knows what is within the wombs; no soul knows what it will earn tomorrow; no soul knows in what land it will die; and no one knows when the rain will come.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1039).

13011
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The keys of the unseen are five:
‘Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4627).

13012
A man from Banū ʿĀmir narrated:
He sought permission to enter upon the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“May I enter?”
The Prophet ﷺ said to his servant:
“Go out to him, for he does not know how to seek permission properly. Tell him to say: ‘Peace be upon you. May I enter?’”
The man said:
I heard him saying this, so I said:
“Peace be upon you. May I enter?”
He then granted me permission, or the narrator said that I entered.
I asked:
“What have you brought us?”
He replied:
“I have brought you nothing but good. I have come to command you to worship Allah alone without partners”—Shuʿbah said, “I think he also said: ‘alone without partners’”—“to abandon al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā, perform five prayers throughout the day and night, fast one month each year, perform pilgrimage to the House, and take wealth from your rich and return it to your poor.”
The man asked:
“Is there any knowledge remaining that you do not possess?”
He replied:
“Allah has taught abundant good, but some knowledge is known only to Allah—the five:
‘Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23127), whose wording is given here, Abū Dāwūd (5179), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (316).

13013
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“Your Prophet ﷺ was given the keys to everything except five:
‘Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (3659) and Abū Yaʿlā (5153).
Regarding the words:
“And He knows what is within the wombs,”
the word mā indicates comprehensiveness.
The meaning is that Allah possesses complete knowledge of everything within the wombs: whether the child will be wretched or blessed, healthy or disabled, tall or short, rich or poor, and whether it will die or live.
The present-tense form in the words:
“And He knows”
indicates His knowledge, glory be to Him, of every newly developing stage and condition through which the human being passes: from a drop of fluid, to a clinging substance, to a lump of flesh.
All of these are matters of the unseen whose complete details and certainty are known only to Allah, glory be to Him.
The fact that people can learn certain limited details of this unseen realm through instruments, intermediary means, and modern technology does not negate Allah’s all-encompassing knowledge.
Regarding the words:
“No soul knows in what land it will die,”
a person, from the day of his birth, does not know where he will travel, where he will reside, or where death will overtake him—whether on land or sea, upon a plain or a mountain.
Complete knowledge of this belongs exclusively to Allah, glory be to Him.
The fact that someone may recognize that another person’s death is near or expect him to die in a particular place does not negate Allah’s comprehensive knowledge.
When Allah decrees that a servant will die in a particular land, He creates a need that causes him to travel there.

13014
Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“There are five matters known only to Allah:
‘Indeed, Allah alone possesses knowledge of the Hour, sends down the rain, and knows what is within the wombs. No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul knows in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware.’”
 [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22986) and al-Bazzār (4409).

13015
Ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When the appointed death of one of you is decreed to occur in a particular land, Allah causes a need to arise that drives him toward it. When he reaches the final place decreed for him, Allah, glory be to Him, takes his soul. On the Day of Resurrection, the earth will say: ‘My Lord, this is what You entrusted to me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4263) and al-Ḥākim (1/41).

13016
Abū ʿIzzah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah decrees that a servant will die in a particular land, He creates for him a need that draws him toward it,”
or he said:
“A need within that land.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2147), Aḥmad (15539), and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (780), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6151) and al-Ḥākim (1/42).

13017
Usāmah ibn Zayd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah never appoints the death of a servant in a particular land without creating for him a need within it.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (20996) and, through his chain, by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (1/144).

13018
Maṭar ibn ʿUkāmis narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah decrees that a servant will die in a particular land, He creates for him a need that takes him there.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2146), ʿAbdullāh ibn Aḥmad in his father’s Musnad (21982), al-Bukhārī in al-Tārīkh al-Kabīr (7/400), and al-Ḥākim (1/42). Its chain is authentic.

13019
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to recite during the dawn prayer on Friday:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The revelation…”
meaning Sūrat al-Sajdah, and:
“Has there come upon the human being…”
meaning Sūrat al-Insān.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (891) and Muslim (880). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī. Muslim’s wording states: “He used to recite during the dawn prayer on Friday…” and continues with the hadith.

13020
Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would not go to sleep until he had recited:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The revelation…”
meaning Sūrat al-Sajdah, and:
“Blessed is the One in Whose hand is all dominion,”
meaning Sūrat al-Mulk.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2892), Aḥmad (14659), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (1209), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (707–708).

13021
Muʿādh ibn Jabal narrated:
I was traveling with the Prophet ﷺ. One morning, while we were journeying, I found myself close to him and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, inform me of a deed that will admit me into Paradise and distance me from the Fire.”
He replied:
“You have asked about a tremendous matter, yet it is easy for the one for whom Allah makes it easy: worship Allah without associating anything with Him, establish prayer, pay zakāh, fast Ramadan, and perform pilgrimage to the House.”
He then said:
“Shall I direct you to the gates of goodness? Fasting is a shield, charity extinguishes sin just as water extinguishes fire, and a man’s prayer during the depths of the night.”
He then recited:
“Their sides leave their beds…”
until he reached:
“…what they used to do.”
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2616), whose wording is given here, and Ibn Mājah (3973).

13022
Abū Umāmah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You must observe the night prayer, for it was the regular practice of the righteous before you. It draws you near to your Lord, expiates evil deeds, and restrains a person from sin.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3549), Ibn Khuzaymah (1135), and al-Ḥākim (1/308).

13023
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Our Lord, Mighty and Majestic, marvels at two men. One is a man who rises from his bedding and blanket, leaving his beloved family, to perform prayer. Our Lord says: ‘My angels, look at My servant. He has risen from his bed and bedding and left his beloved family to pray, desiring what is with Me and fearing what is with Me.’
The other is a man who fights in the path of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and his companions are defeated. He knows the sin incurred by fleeing and the reward obtained by returning, so he returns until his blood is shed, desiring what is with Me and fearing what is with Me.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says to His angels: ‘Look at My servant. He returned, desiring what is with Me and fearing what is with Me, until his blood was shed.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (3949), Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (569), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2557, 2558), and al-Ḥākim (2/112).
The verse may also be understood as referring to attending the night prayer in congregation, as stated in the following narration:

13024
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The verse:
“Their sides leave their beds”
was revealed concerning waiting for the prayer known as al-ʿatamah, meaning the night prayer.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3196).

13025
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī ʿAmrah narrated:
ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān entered the mosque after the sunset prayer and sat alone. I sat beside him, and he said:
“My nephew, I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
‘Whoever performs the night prayer in congregation is as though he stood in prayer for half the night, and whoever performs the dawn prayer in congregation is as though he prayed throughout the entire night.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Mosques (656).

13026
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Blessed and Exalted, says: ‘I have prepared for My righteous servants that which no eye has seen, no ear has heard, and which has never occurred to the heart of any human being.’”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“Recite, if you wish:
‘No soul knows what delight of the eyes has been concealed for them.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4779) and Muslim (2824). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13027
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated:
I attended a gathering in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ described Paradise at length. At the end of his speech, he said:
“Within it is that which no eye has seen, no ear has heard, and which has never occurred to the heart of any human being.”
He then recited:
“Their sides leave their beds as they call upon their Lord in fear and hope, and they spend from what We have provided them. No soul knows what delight of the eyes has been concealed for them as a reward for what they used to do.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2825).

13028
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated, attributing it to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Moses asked his Lord: ‘Who will have the lowest rank among the people of Paradise?’
Allah said: ‘He will be a man who comes after all the people of Paradise have entered it. He will be told: “Enter Paradise.”
He will say: “My Lord, how can I, when all the people have settled into their places and received what they were given?”
It will be said to him: “Would you be satisfied to possess the equivalent of the kingdom of one of the kings of this world?”
He will reply: “I am satisfied, my Lord.”
Allah will say: “You will have that, and another like it, and another like it, and another like it, and another like it.”
At the fifth, he will say: “I am satisfied, my Lord.”
Allah will say: “You will have all of that and ten times as much. You will also have whatever your soul desires and whatever delights your eyes.”
He will say: “I am satisfied, my Lord.”’
Moses asked: ‘My Lord, who will have the highest rank?’
Allah said: ‘Those are the ones whom I chose. I planted their honor with My own hand and sealed it. No eye has seen it, no ear has heard it, and it has never occurred to the heart of any human being.’”
The confirmation of this is found in the Book of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“No soul knows what delight of the eyes has been concealed for them as a reward for what they used to do.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (189).
The words:
“Those whom I chose”
mean: those whom Allah selected and specially favored.
The words:
“I planted their honor”
mean that Allah selected them and personally undertook their care, so no alteration can ever affect the honor prepared for them.

13029
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“We will certainly make them taste the lesser punishment before the greater punishment so that they may return”:
“It refers to the calamities of this world, the event concerning the Romans, the mighty seizure, or the smoke.”
Shuʿbah was uncertain whether Ubayy mentioned the mighty seizure or the smoke.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2799).
It has also been said that the lesser punishment is the punishment of the grave, while the greater punishment is the punishment of the Fire.
It has also been said that the lesser punishment refers to what befell the disbelieving deniers on the Day of Badr, so that perhaps they might return to Allah and repent of their sins before the greater punishment.

13030
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
We used to call Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, the freedman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, only by the name:
“Zayd ibn Muḥammad,”
until the Qur’an revealed:
“Call them by their fathers’ names; that is more just in the sight of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4782) and Muslim (2425). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13031
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Abū Ḥudhayfah ibn ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah ibn ʿAbd Shams, who had participated in the Battle of Badr with the Prophet ﷺ, adopted Sālim and married him to his niece, Hind bint al-Walīd ibn ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah. Sālim had been the freedman of a woman from the Anṣār.
This was similar to the Prophet ﷺ adopting Zayd.
During the pre-Islamic period, when a man adopted another person, the people would attribute the adopted person to him, and he would inherit from the adoptive father.
This continued until Allah revealed:
“Call them by their fathers’ names; that is more just in the sight of Allah. But if you do not know their fathers, then they are your brothers in religion and those under your care.”
They were then attributed to their biological fathers. Whoever had no known father was regarded as a freedman and a brother in religion.
Sahlah bint Suhayl ibn ʿAmr al-Qurashiyyah, then al-ʿĀmiriyyah, the wife of Abū Ḥudhayfah ibn ʿUtbah, came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we used to regard Sālim as our son, but Allah has now revealed concerning him what you know.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5088).

13032
Abū Mālik al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Four practices from the affairs of the pre-Islamic age will remain within my nation and will not be abandoned: boasting about lineage, attacking people’s lineage, seeking rain through the stars, and wailing over the dead.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (934).

13033
Abū Dharr narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“No man knowingly attributes himself to someone other than his biological father except that he has committed an act of disbelief. Whoever falsely claims to belong to a people to whom he does not belong should prepare his place in the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3508) and Muslim (61). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.
Attacking people’s lineage and attributing oneself to someone other than one’s true father are prohibited in Islamic law because of the resulting corruption in marriage, inheritance, and other matters.
It is permissible, however, to address another person affectionately as “my son,” without intending a false attribution of lineage, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13034
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“My son.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2151).

13035
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When a deceased man who owed a debt was brought to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he would ask:
“Did he leave sufficient wealth to repay his debt?”
If he was informed that the deceased had left enough to repay it, he would perform the funeral prayer over him. Otherwise, he would say to the Muslims:
“Perform the funeral prayer over your companion.”
After Allah granted him victories and wealth, he said:
“I have greater authority over the believers than they have over themselves. Therefore, if a believer dies leaving a debt, its payment is my responsibility; and if he leaves wealth, it belongs to his heirs.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in The Book of Guarantees (2298) and Muslim in The Book of Inheritance (1619). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13036
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There is no believer except that I have greater authority over him in this world and the Hereafter. Recite, if you wish:
‘The Prophet has greater authority over the believers than they have over themselves.’
Therefore, whichever believer leaves wealth, his paternal relatives should inherit it, whoever they may be. Whoever leaves a debt or helpless dependents should come to me, for I am his guardian.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4781).

13037
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have greater authority over the believers than they have over themselves. Whoever dies leaving wealth, it belongs to his paternal heirs. Whoever leaves a burdensome debt or helpless dependents, I am his guardian, so let the matter be brought to me.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6745).

13038
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“According to the Book of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, I have the greatest authority over the believers. Whoever among you leaves a debt or helpless dependents should summon me, for I am his guardian. Whoever leaves wealth, his paternal relatives, whoever they may be, have the greatest right to it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1619:16).

13039
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, there is no believer upon the earth except that I have greater authority over him than all other people. Whoever among you leaves a debt or helpless dependents, I am his guardian. Whoever leaves wealth, it belongs to his paternal relatives, whoever they may be.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1619:15).

13040
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have greater authority over every believer than he has over himself. Whoever leaves wealth, it belongs to his family. Whoever leaves a debt or helpless dependents, their care comes to me and is my responsibility.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (867:43).
Regarding the words:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to one another according to the Book of Allah”:

13041
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“As for those with whom you have made binding agreements, give them their share,”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:33]
a man would form a pact with another man despite there being no blood relationship between them, and each would inherit from the other.
This ruling was abrogated by the verse in Sūrat al-Anfāl:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to one another.”
 [Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:75]
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2921).

13042
Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām narrated:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed specifically concerning us—the people of Quraysh and the Anṣār:
“Blood relatives are more entitled to one another according to the Book of Allah.”
He said:
When we, the people of Quraysh, arrived in Madinah without wealth, we found the Anṣār to be excellent brothers. We established bonds of brotherhood with them and made them our heirs.
Abū Bakr was joined in brotherhood with Khārijah ibn Zayd. ʿUmar was joined with another man. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was joined with a man from Banū Zurayq ibn Saʿd al-Zuraqī, although some people name someone else.
Al-Zubayr said:
“I was joined in brotherhood with Kaʿb ibn Mālik. They made us their heirs, and we made them ours.”
On the Day of Uḥud, I was told:
“Your brother Kaʿb ibn Mālik has been killed.”
I went to him and pulled him out from among the fallen. His weapons had weighed him down, as far as we could see.
By Allah, my son, had he died that day, no one but me would have inherited from him.
This continued until Allah revealed this verse specifically concerning us, the people of Quraysh and the Anṣār. We then returned to inheritance according to blood relationships.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (5/1742).

13043
ʿĀʾishah said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“When they came upon you from above you and from below you, when eyes became fixed in terror, hearts rose to the throats, and you entertained various thoughts about Allah”:
