“That occurred on the Day of the Trench.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4103) and Muslim (3020:12).

13044
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
On the Day of the Trench, when hardship had become severe for us, we asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Is there anything we should say?”
He replied:
“Say: ‘O Allah, conceal our vulnerabilities and grant us security from our fears.’”
Allah then defeated the enemy through the wind.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3119).

13045
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory through the east wind, while ʿĀd were destroyed by the west wind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4105) and Muslim (900:17).
The words:
“I was granted victory through al-ṣabā”
refer to the eastern wind.
The word:
“Al-dabūr”
refers to the western wind.

13046
Yazīd ibn Sharīk al-Taymī narrated:
We were sitting with Ḥudhayfah when a man said:
“Had I lived during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I would have fought beside him and shown great courage.”
Ḥudhayfah replied:
“Would you really have done that?
I saw us with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the night of the Confederates. A fierce wind and extreme cold had overtaken us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Will a man bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?”
We remained silent, and no one answered him.
He repeated:
“Will a man bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?”
We remained silent, and no one answered.
He said a third time:
“Will a man bring me news of the enemy, so that Allah may place him with me on the Day of Resurrection?”
We remained silent, and no one answered.
He then said:
“Stand, Ḥudhayfah, and bring us news of the enemy.”
Once he called me by name, I had no choice but to rise.
He said:
“Go and bring me news of the people, but do not alarm them against me.”
When I left him, it was as though I were walking inside a heated bath, until I reached them.
I saw Abū Sufyān warming his back by the fire. I placed an arrow in the center of my bow and intended to shoot him, but I remembered the words of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Do not alarm them against me.”
Had I shot him, I would certainly have struck him.
I returned, again feeling as though I were walking inside a heated bath. When I reached the Prophet ﷺ, I informed him of the enemy’s condition.
After I had finished, I again felt the intense cold. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ covered me with the extra portion of a cloak he was wearing while praying.
I continued sleeping until morning. When morning came, he said:
“Get up, sleepy one.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1788:99).

13047
Zayd ibn Thābit narrated:
When we copied the written sheets into complete Qur’anic manuscripts, I could not find one verse from Sūrat al-Aḥzāb which I had heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite.
I found it with no one except Khuzaymah al-Anṣārī, whose testimony the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had declared equal to the testimony of two men:
“Among the believers are men who remained true to the covenant they made with Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4784).

13048
Anas narrated:
My paternal uncle, Anas ibn al-Naḍr, was absent from the Battle of Badr. He said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I was absent from the first battle in which you fought the polytheists. If Allah allows me to witness another battle against the polytheists, Allah will certainly see what I will do.”
When the Day of Uḥud came and the Muslims retreated, he said:
“O Allah, I apologize to You for what these people have done,”
meaning his fellow Muslims,
“and I declare myself innocent before You of what those people have done,”
meaning the polytheists.
He then advanced and encountered Saʿd ibn Muʿādh. He said:
“Saʿd ibn Muʿādh, Paradise, by the Lord of al-Naḍr! I can smell its fragrance from this side of Uḥud.”
Saʿd later said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I could not accomplish what he accomplished.”
Anas continued:
We found more than eighty wounds upon his body, caused by sword blows, spear thrusts, and arrows. We found that he had been killed and that the polytheists had mutilated his body. No one could identify him except his sister, who recognized him by his fingertips.
Anas said:
“We used to believe—or think—that this verse was revealed concerning him and others like him:
‘Among the believers are men who remained true to the covenant they made with Allah.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2805) and Muslim (1903:48).

13049
Anas ibn Mālik said:
“We believe that this verse was revealed concerning Anas ibn al-Naḍr:
‘Among the believers are men who remained true to the covenant they made with Allah.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4783).

13050
Ṭalḥah narrated:
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to an uninformed Bedouin:
“Ask him who is meant by the one who has fulfilled his vow.”
They themselves did not dare to ask the Prophet ﷺ because of their deep reverence and awe for him.
The Bedouin asked him, but the Prophet ﷺ turned away. He asked again, and the Prophet ﷺ turned away. He asked a third time, and the Prophet ﷺ again turned away.
Ṭalḥah said:
I then appeared at the entrance of the mosque wearing green garments. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw me, he asked:
“Where is the person who asked about the one who fulfilled his vow?”
The Bedouin replied:
“I am here, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“This man is among those who have fulfilled their vow.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3203, 3742), al-Bazzār (943), and Abū Yaʿlā (663).

13051
ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated against the confederate armies, saying:
“O Allah, Revealer of the Book and Swift in reckoning, defeat the confederates. O Allah, defeat them and shake them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4115) and Muslim (1741:21).

13052
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
On the Day of the Trench, we were prevented from performing the noon, afternoon, sunset, and night prayers until Allah relieved us of the enemy. This is referred to in the words of Allah:
“Allah drove back the disbelievers in their rage, without their attaining any good, and Allah spared the believers from fighting. Allah is All-Powerful, Almighty.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then stood and instructed Bilāl to call the prayer.
Bilāl called the commencement of prayer, and the Prophet ﷺ performed the noon prayer in the manner he normally performed it. He then called the commencement of the afternoon prayer, and the Prophet ﷺ performed it as usual. He then called the sunset prayer, and the Prophet ﷺ performed it as usual. He then called the night prayer, and the Prophet ﷺ performed it as usual.
This occurred before the revelation of the words:
“But if you are in fear, then pray while walking or riding.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:239]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (621) and Ibn Abī Shaybah in his Muṣannaf (37656).

13053
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to say:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone. He strengthened His army, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the confederates by Himself. There is nothing after Him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4114) and Muslim (2724:77).

13054
Sulaymān ibn Ṣurad narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say after the confederates had been driven away:
“From now on, we will march against them, and they will not march against us. We will go to them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4110).

13055
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
Saʿd was wounded during the Battle of the Trench. A man from Quraysh named Ḥibbān ibn al-ʿAriqah shot him in the large vein of his arm. The Prophet ﷺ had a tent erected for him inside the mosque so that he could visit him nearby.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from the Trench, he laid down his weapons and bathed. Gabriel, peace be upon him, came to him while shaking dust from his head and said:
“Have you laid down your weapons? By Allah, I have not laid mine down. Go out against them.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Where?”
Gabriel pointed toward Banū Qurayẓah.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to them, and they agreed to submit to his judgment. He referred the decision concerning them to Saʿd.
Saʿd said:
“My judgment concerning them is that their fighting men should be killed, their women and children taken captive, and their wealth distributed.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4122) and Muslim (1769:65).
Muslim added that ʿUrwah said:
I was informed that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You have judged them according to the judgment of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”

13056
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
Banū al-Naḍīr and Banū Qurayẓah fought against the Prophet ﷺ. He expelled Banū al-Naḍīr but allowed Banū Qurayẓah to remain and showed them favor.
When Banū Qurayẓah later fought against him, he had their men killed and distributed their women, children, and wealth among the Muslims. Some of them, however, came to the Prophet ﷺ, and he granted them safety when they accepted Islam.
He also expelled all the Jewish tribes of Madinah: Banū Qaynuqāʿ, who were the people of ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām; the Jews of Banū Ḥārithah; and all the other Jews of Madinah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4028) and Muslim (1766).

13057
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
When Allah commanded the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to give his wives a choice, he came to me first and said:
“I am going to mention a matter to you. There is no harm in your not rushing to decide until you consult your parents.”
He knew that my parents would never instruct me to separate from him.
She continued:
He then said:
“Allah has said:
‘O Prophet, say to your wives…’”
and recited the two verses in full.
I replied:
“Concerning which part of this should I consult my parents? I choose Allah, His Messenger, and the home of the Hereafter.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4785) and Muslim (1475). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13058
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
I remained eager to ask ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb about the two wives of the Prophet ﷺ concerning whom Allah said:
“If you both repent to Allah, then your hearts have certainly inclined away.”
 [Sūrat al-Taḥrīm 66:4]
Eventually, ʿUmar performed pilgrimage, and I performed pilgrimage with him.
He stepped away from the road, and I followed him carrying a water vessel. After relieving himself, he returned, and I poured water over his hands so that he could perform ablution.
I asked:
“Leader of the Believers, who were the two wives of the Prophet ﷺ concerning whom Allah said:
‘If you both repent to Allah, then your hearts have certainly inclined away’?”
He replied:
“How astonishing that you ask this, Ibn ʿAbbās! They were ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah.”
ʿUmar then began relating the account:
A neighbor of mine from the Anṣār and I lived among Banū Umayyah ibn Zayd in the elevated districts of Madinah. We would take turns visiting the Prophet ﷺ. He would go one day, and I would go the next.
When I went, I would bring my neighbor the news of that day concerning revelation and other matters. When he went, he would do the same for me.
We men of Quraysh used to exercise authority over our women. When we came to the Anṣār, we found a people whose women exercised authority over them, and our women began adopting the manners of the Anṣārī women.
I raised my voice at my wife, and she answered me back. I objected to her answering me.
She said:
“Why do you object to my answering you? By Allah, the wives of the Prophet ﷺ answer him back, and one of them may avoid him throughout the day until night.”
This alarmed me, and I said:
“Any of them who does that has certainly failed.”
I dressed and went down to Ḥafṣah. I asked:
“Ḥafṣah, does one of you remain angry with the Prophet ﷺ throughout the day until night?”
She replied:
“Yes.”
I said:
“You have failed and suffered loss. Do you feel safe that Allah may become angry because of the anger of His Messenger ﷺ and that you may consequently be destroyed? Do not demand too much from the Prophet ﷺ, do not answer him back concerning anything, and do not avoid him. Ask me for whatever you need. Do not be deceived because your co-wife is more beautiful than you and more beloved to the Prophet ﷺ.”
He meant ʿĀʾishah.
ʿUmar continued:
We had been discussing that Ghassān was preparing its horses to invade us.
My Anṣārī companion went down on the day of his turn. He returned to us in the evening, struck my door forcefully, and asked:
“Is he there?”
I became alarmed and went out to him.
He said:
“A tremendous event has occurred today.”
I asked:
“What is it? Has Ghassān arrived?”
He replied:
“No, something even greater and more terrible. The Prophet ﷺ has divorced his wives.”
ʿUbayd ibn Ḥunayn narrated that Ibn ʿAbbās heard ʿUmar say:
“The Prophet ﷺ had withdrawn from his wives.”
I said:
“Ḥafṣah has failed and suffered loss. I had suspected that this might soon happen.”
I dressed and performed the dawn prayer with the Prophet ﷺ. He then entered an upper room belonging to him and withdrew there.
I entered upon Ḥafṣah and found her weeping.
I asked:
“Why are you weeping? Did I not warn you about this? Has the Prophet ﷺ divorced you?”
She replied:
“I do not know. He is there, secluded in the upper room.”
I went out and came to the pulpit, where a group of men were gathered, some of them weeping.
I sat with them briefly, but my anxiety overcame me. I approached the upper room where the Prophet ﷺ was and said to a black servant:
“Seek permission for ʿUmar to enter.”
The servant entered, spoke to the Prophet ﷺ, and returned, saying:
“I spoke to the Prophet ﷺ and mentioned you, but he remained silent.”
I returned and sat with the men near the pulpit.
My anxiety again overcame me, so I returned and said to the servant:
“Seek permission for ʿUmar.”
He entered and returned, saying:
“I mentioned you to him, but he remained silent.”
I returned and sat with the men near the pulpit.
My anxiety overcame me a third time. I went to the servant and said:
“Seek permission for ʿUmar.”
He entered and returned, saying:
“I mentioned you to him, but he remained silent.”
As I turned to leave, the servant called me and said:
“The Prophet ﷺ has granted you permission.”
I entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and found him lying upon a woven mat with no bedding between him and it. The mat had left marks upon his side. He was leaning against a leather cushion filled with palm fiber.
I greeted him and asked while standing:
“O Messenger of Allah, have you divorced your wives?”
He raised his gaze toward me and replied:
“No.”
I said:
“Allah is the Greatest!”
Then, while still standing and trying to comfort him, I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, consider our situation. We men of Quraysh used to exercise authority over our women, but when we arrived in Madinah, we found a people whose women exercised authority over them.”
The Prophet ﷺ smiled.
I continued:
“O Messenger of Allah, you should have seen me when I entered upon Ḥafṣah and said: ‘Do not be deceived because your co-wife is more beautiful than you and more beloved to the Prophet ﷺ,’ meaning ʿĀʾishah.”
The Prophet ﷺ smiled again.
When I saw him smile, I sat down.
I looked around his room and, by Allah, saw nothing that attracted the eye except three untanned hides.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, supplicate to Allah to grant abundance to your nation. Persia and Rome have been granted abundance and worldly comforts, even though they do not worship Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ had been reclining, but he sat upright and said:
“Are you still thinking in this manner, Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb? Those are people whose good things have been hastened for them in the worldly life.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, seek forgiveness for me.”
The Prophet ﷺ withdrew from his wives for twenty-nine nights because of the incident in which Ḥafṣah disclosed the secret to ʿĀʾishah. He had said:
“I will not enter upon them for one month.”
He was deeply upset with them after Allah, Mighty and Majestic, had reproached him.
After twenty-nine nights had passed, he entered upon ʿĀʾishah first.
She said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you swore that you would not enter upon us for one month, but this morning is only the twenty-ninth night, which I have counted carefully.”
He replied:
“A month may consist of twenty-nine nights.”
That month contained twenty-nine nights.
ʿĀʾishah said:
Allah then revealed the verse giving the wives a choice. The Prophet ﷺ began with me as the first of his wives. I chose him. He then gave all his other wives the choice, and they said the same as ʿĀʾishah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5191) and Muslim (1479:34).

13059
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Abū Bakr came seeking permission to enter upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He found people sitting at the Prophet’s door, but none of them had been granted permission to enter.
Abū Bakr was given permission and entered. ʿUmar then arrived, sought permission, and was admitted.
They found the Prophet ﷺ sitting silently and sorrowfully, surrounded by his wives.
ʿUmar said to himself:
“I will say something that will make the Prophet ﷺ laugh.”
He said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you should have seen the daughter of Khārijah when she asked me for maintenance. I stood up and struck her neck.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and said:
“They are gathered around me, as you see, asking me for maintenance.”
Abū Bakr then rose toward ʿĀʾishah and struck her neck, while ʿUmar rose toward Ḥafṣah and struck her neck.
Each of them said:
“Are you asking the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for what he does not possess?”
The wives replied:
“By Allah, we will never again ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for anything he does not possess.”
The Prophet ﷺ then withdrew from them for one month, or twenty-nine days.
The following verses were then revealed:
“O Prophet, say to your wives…”
until:
“Allah has prepared a tremendous reward for the righteous among you.”
 [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:28–29]
The Prophet ﷺ began with ʿĀʾishah and said:
“ʿĀʾishah, I wish to present a matter to you, and I would prefer that you not rush to decide until you consult your parents.”
She asked:
“What is it, O Messenger of Allah?”
He recited the verse to her.
She replied:
“Would I consult my parents concerning you, O Messenger of Allah? Rather, I choose Allah, His Messenger, and the home of the Hereafter. I ask you not to tell any of your other wives what I have said.”
He replied:
“None of them will ask me without my informing her. Allah did not send me to cause hardship or seek people’s hardship. Rather, He sent me as a teacher who makes matters easy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1478).

13060
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave us the choice, and we chose Allah and His Messenger. He did not count that choice against us as a divorce.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5262) and Muslim (1477:28).

13061
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, People of the Household, and to purify you completely”:
“It was revealed specifically concerning the wives of the Prophet ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as mentioned by Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr.

13062
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ went out one morning wearing a striped cloak made of black hair.
Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī came, and the Prophet ﷺ admitted him beneath it. Al-Ḥusayn then came and entered with him. Fāṭimah then came, and he admitted her. ʿAlī then came, and he admitted him as well.
The Prophet ﷺ then recited:
“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, People of the Household, and to purify you completely.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2424).

13063
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
When the following verse was revealed:
“Say: Come, let us call our sons and your sons…”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:61]
the Messenger of Allah ﷺ summoned ʿAlī, Fāṭimah, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn and said:
“O Allah, these are my family.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2404:32).

13064
Shaddād Abū ʿAmmār narrated:
I entered upon Wāthilah ibn al-Asqaʿ while a group of people were with him, and they mentioned ʿAlī.
After they left, he said to me:
“Shall I tell you what I saw from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
I went to Fāṭimah to ask her about ʿAlī. She said:
“He has gone to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I sat and waited until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came with ʿAlī, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn, holding each of the two boys by the hand.
He entered and brought ʿAlī and Fāṭimah close, seating them before him. He seated al-Ḥasan and al-Ḥusayn, one upon each thigh.
He then wrapped his garment—or cloak—around them and recited:
“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, People of the Household, and to purify you completely.”
He then said:
“O Allah, these are the members of my household, and the members of my household have the greatest right.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16988), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6976), and narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/416, 3/147).
The words:
“The members of my household have the greatest right”
refer to this honor: the removal of impurity from them and their complete purification.

13065
ʿUmar ibn Abī Salamah, the stepson of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The following verse was revealed to the Prophet ﷺ in the house of Umm Salamah:
“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, People of the Household, and to purify you completely.”
The Prophet ﷺ called Fāṭimah, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn and covered them with a cloak. ʿAlī was behind him, so he covered him with the cloak as well.
He then said:
“O Allah, these are the members of my household. Remove impurity from them and purify them completely.”
Umm Salamah asked:
“Am I with them, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Remain in your place. You are upon goodness.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3205, 3787), al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (19/106), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8295).

13066
Umm Salamah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was in her house when Fāṭimah brought him a stone pot containing a dish of meat and flour.
She entered with it, and he said:
“Call your husband and your two sons.”
ʿAlī, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn came and entered. They sat and ate from the dish while the Prophet ﷺ was seated upon a raised platform on a sleeping mat, with a Khaybarī cloak beneath him.
Umm Salamah said:
I was praying inside the room when Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, People of the Household, and to purify you completely.”
The Prophet ﷺ took the remaining portion of the cloak and covered them with it. He then extended his hand toward the sky and said:
“O Allah, these are the members of my household and my closest family. Remove impurity from them and purify them completely. O Allah, these are the members of my household and my closest family. Remove impurity from them and purify them completely.”
Umm Salamah said:
I placed my head inside the room and asked:
“Am I with you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“You are upon goodness. You are upon goodness.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26508).

13067
Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Why are we women not mentioned in the Qur’an in the same manner as men?”
One afternoon, I was suddenly startled by his voice calling from the pulpit.
I was combing my hair, so I quickly tied it, went into the inner room of my house, and placed my ear near the palm branches to listen.
He was saying from the pulpit:
“People, Allah says in His Book:
‘Indeed, the Muslim men and Muslim women, the believing men and believing women…’
until the end of the verse:
‘Allah has prepared for them forgiveness and a tremendous reward.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11341), whose wording is given here, Aḥmad (26575), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (23/293–294).

13068
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The women asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, why are we not mentioned in the same manner as the men?”
Allah then revealed:
“Indeed, the Muslim men and Muslim women, the believing men and believing women, the devoutly obedient men and devoutly obedient women, the truthful men and truthful women, the patient men and patient women, the humble men and humble women, the charitable men and charitable women, the fasting men and fasting women, the men who guard their chastity and the women who guard their chastity, and the men who remember Allah frequently and the women who remember Him—Allah has prepared for them forgiveness and a tremendous reward.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (19/111) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12/108).

13069
Umm ʿUmārah al-Anṣāriyyah narrated:
She came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“I see that everything is mentioned concerning men, while I do not see women being mentioned in anything.”
The following verse was then revealed:
“Indeed, the Muslim men and Muslim women, the believing men and believing women…”
to the end of the verse.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3211).

13070
Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ proposed marriage on behalf of Julaybīb to an Anṣārī woman through her father.
Her father said:
“Let me first consult her mother.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Very well, then.”
The man went to his wife and told her what had happened.
She said:
“No, by Allah! Could the Messenger of Allah ﷺ find no one other than Julaybīb? We refused her to so-and-so and so-and-so!”
Their daughter was listening from behind her curtain.
As the father prepared to return and inform the Prophet ﷺ of their refusal, the girl said:
“Do you intend to reject the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ? If he has approved him for you, then marry me to him.”
It was as though she removed the difficulty from her parents, and they said:
“You have spoken the truth.”
Her father went to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“If you are pleased with him, then we are pleased with him.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“I am indeed pleased with him.”
So he married her to Julaybīb.
Later, the people of Madinah were called out in alarm, and Julaybīb went forth. He was subsequently found dead, with several polytheists whom he had killed lying around him.
Anas said:
“I later saw that woman, and she was among the most generously provided-for widows in Madinah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12393) and Ibn Ḥibbān (4059).
The words:
“The people of Madinah were called out in alarm”
might outwardly suggest that Julaybīb was killed within Madinah. The correct understanding, however, is that he was killed during one of the military expeditions, as shown by the following hadith of Abū Barzah al-Aslamī.
Thus, “the people of Madinah” means those people from Madinah who were participating in that expedition and knew him.

13071
Abū Barzah al-Aslamī narrated:
Julaybīb was a man who would pass by women and joke with them. I therefore said to my wife:
“Do not let Julaybīb enter upon you, for if he enters upon you, I will do such-and-such.”
Among the Anṣār, when a man had an unmarried woman under his care, he would not marry her off until he knew whether the Prophet ﷺ had any interest in marrying her.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to an Anṣārī man:
“Marry your daughter to me.”
The man replied:
“Yes, with honor and delight, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“I do not want her for myself.”
The man asked:
“Then for whom, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“For Julaybīb.”
The man said:
“O Messenger of Allah, let me consult her mother.”
He went to her mother and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ is proposing marriage for your daughter.”
She replied:
“Yes, with honor and delight.”
He said:
“He is not proposing her for himself. He is proposing her for Julaybīb.”
She exclaimed:
“Julaybīb? Julaybīb? Julaybīb? No, by Allah, we will not marry her to him!”
When the father prepared to leave and inform the Messenger of Allah ﷺ of what the mother had said, the girl asked:
“Who has proposed marriage for me?”
Her mother told her.
The girl said:
“Will you reject the command of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ? Give me in marriage to him. He will not cause me to be lost.”
Her father went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Then proceed with her.”
So he married her to Julaybīb.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ later went out on a military expedition. After Allah granted him victory, he asked his Companions:
“Are you missing anyone?”
They replied:
“We are missing so-and-so and so-and-so.”
He asked again:
“Look carefully. Are you missing anyone?”
They replied:
“No.”
He said:
“But I am missing Julaybīb.”
Then he said:
“Search for him among the dead.”
They searched and found him beside seven men whom he had killed before they killed him.
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, here he is beside seven whom he killed before they killed him.”
The Prophet ﷺ came and stood over him, saying:
“He killed seven, and then they killed him. This man is from me, and I am from him. This man is from me, and I am from him.”
He repeated it two or three times.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then placed him upon his own forearms while a grave was dug for him. Julaybīb had no bier except the forearms of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He then placed him into his grave. The narrator did not mention that he was washed.
Thābit said:
“There was no widow among the Anṣār who received more abundant provision than she did.”
Isḥāq ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Ṭalḥah narrated to Thābit:
“Do you know what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated for her?”
He said:
“O Allah, pour goodness upon her abundantly, and do not make her life one of toil and hardship.”
He said:
“There was no widow among the Anṣār who was more abundantly provided for than she.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad in his Musnad (19784).
In another narration, the woman recited the words of Allah:
“It is not for a believing man or a believing woman…”
to the end of the verse.

13072
Usāmah ibn Zayd narrated:
I was in the mosque when al-ʿAbbās and ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib came to me and said:
“Usāmah, seek permission for us to enter upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him:
“ʿAlī and al-ʿAbbās are seeking permission to enter.”
He asked:
“Do you know what they want?”
I replied:
“No, by Allah.”
He said:
“But I know.”
He then granted them permission.
They entered and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we have come to ask you which member of your family is most beloved to you.”
He replied:
“The most beloved member of my family to me is Fāṭimah bint Muḥammad.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we are not asking you about Fāṭimah.”
He replied:
“Then Usāmah ibn Zayd, the son of the one upon whom Allah bestowed favor and upon whom I bestowed favor.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (2619, 2620).

13073
Anas narrated:
Zayd ibn Ḥārithah came complaining about his marriage, and the Prophet ﷺ kept saying to him:
“Fear Allah and keep your wife.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Had the Messenger of Allah ﷺ been one to conceal anything of the revelation, he would have concealed this.”
Zaynab used to boast to the other wives of the Prophet ﷺ, saying:
“Your families arranged your marriages, but Allah, the Exalted, married me from above the seven heavens.”
Thābit said concerning the words:
“You concealed within yourself what Allah was going to disclose, and you feared the people,”
that they were revealed concerning Zaynab bint Jaḥsh and Zayd ibn Ḥārithah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7420).

13074
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Had Muḥammad ﷺ concealed anything of what was revealed to him, he would have concealed this verse:
‘When you said to the one upon whom Allah bestowed favor and upon whom you bestowed favor, “Keep your wife and fear Allah,” while you concealed within yourself what Allah was going to disclose, and you feared the people, whereas Allah has more right that you should fear Him.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (177:288).
Regarding the words:
“You concealed within yourself what Allah was going to disclose,”
what the Prophet ﷺ concealed within himself at that time was his intention that, if Zayd ibn Ḥārithah divorced his wife Zaynab bint Jaḥsh, he would marry her.
This would console her because she had originally married by the Prophet’s ﷺ instruction, while she was from among the noble women of Quraysh.
This is the most apparent of the interpretations concerning this noble verse.

13075
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The fear and awe of people must not prevent any one of you from speaking the truth when he sees it, witnesses it, or hears it.”
Abū Saʿīd said:
“I wish I had never heard it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (11017).
Abū Saʿīd’s words:
“I wish I had never heard it”
refer to this hadith, because fully acting upon its demand can be extremely difficult.

13076
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My likeness and the likeness of the prophets before me is that of a man who built a house and made it beautiful and complete, except for the place of one brick in a corner. People began walking around it, admiring it and saying: ‘If only this brick were placed here!’ I am that brick, and I am the Seal of the Prophets.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3535) and Muslim (2286:22).

13077
Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My likeness and the likeness of the prophets before me is that of a man who constructed a building and made it beautiful and excellent, except for the place of one brick in one of its corners. People began walking around it, admiring it, and saying: ‘If only this brick were put in place!’ I am that brick, and I am the Seal of the Prophets.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2286).

13078
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been favored over the other prophets in six ways: I was given concise and comprehensive speech; I was granted victory through fear cast into the enemy; spoils of war were made lawful for me; the earth was made for me a means of purification and a place of prayer; I was sent to all creation; and the prophets were sealed with me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (523).
The hadiths establishing that the Prophet ﷺ is the final prophet are numerous and are mentioned in The Book of Faith.

13079
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was traveling along the road to Makkah when he passed a mountain called Jumdān.
He said:
“Proceed! This is Jumdān. The solitary ones have gone ahead.”
They asked:
“Who are the solitary ones, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The men who remember Allah abundantly and the women who remember Him abundantly.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2676).

13080
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Shall I inform you of the best of your deeds, the purest of them in the sight of your Sovereign, the highest of them in your ranks, better for you than spending gold and silver, and better for you than meeting your enemy and striking their necks while they strike yours?”
They replied:
“Certainly.”
He said:
“The remembrance of Allah, the Exalted.”
Muʿādh ibn Jabal said:
“Nothing saves more effectively from the punishment of Allah than the remembrance of Allah.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3377), Ibn Mājah (3790), Aḥmad (21702), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (1/496).

13081
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
Some captives were brought before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Among them was a woman searching frantically. Whenever she found a child among the captives, she took him, pressed him to her chest, and nursed him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked us:
“Do you think this woman would throw her child into the Fire?”
We replied:
“No, by Allah, not while she has the ability to avoid doing so.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah is more merciful to His servants than this woman is to her child.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5999) and Muslim (2754). The wording is that of Muslim.

13082
Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ passed by with a group of his Companions while a small child was in the road.
When the child’s mother saw the group approaching, she feared that her child might be trampled. She rushed forward crying:
“My son! My son!”
She hurried to him and picked him up.
The people said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this woman would never throw her child into the Fire.”
The Prophet ﷺ calmed them and said:
“Nor, by Allah, will Allah, Mighty and Majestic, throw His beloved servant into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12018, 13467), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3476), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (1/58, 4/177).

13083
ʿAṭāʾ ibn Yasār narrated:
I met ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ and said:
“Tell me about the description of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the Torah.”
He replied:
“Yes, by Allah. He is described in the Torah with some of the same qualities by which he is described in the Qur’an:
‘O Prophet, indeed We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner,’
and as a protection for the unlettered people.
‘You are My servant and My messenger. I have named you al-Mutawakkil, the one who places his trust in Allah. You are neither harsh nor hard-hearted, nor one who raises his voice in the marketplaces. You do not repay an evil deed with another evil deed, but rather you pardon and forgive.
Allah will not take him in death until He straightens through him the crooked religion, so that people say: “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,” and through it He opens blind eyes, deaf ears, and covered hearts.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2125).
The statement of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr:
“He is described in the Torah with some of the same qualities by which he is described in the Qur’an”
indicates that ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr had knowledge of the Jewish scripture known as the Old Testament.
It is also broadly referred to as the Torah because its first five books—Genesis, Exodus, Leviticus, Numbers, and Deuteronomy—are attributed to Moses, peace be upon him. The remainder of the Old Testament contains several categories of writings, including the books of the prophets.
Statements resembling what ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr mentioned can still be found in the Book of Isaiah, chapter 42.
The passages of this chapter are as follows:
Behold My servant whom I uphold, My chosen one in whom My soul delights.
I have placed My spirit upon him, and he will bring forth justice to the nations.
He will neither cry out nor raise his voice, nor cause his voice to be heard in the street.
He will not grow weak or be broken until he establishes justice upon the earth, and the islands will await his law.
I am the Lord. I have called you in righteousness, will hold your hand and preserve you, and will make you a covenant for the people and a light for the nations, to open blind eyes and bring prisoners out of confinement and those sitting in darkness out of prison.
I am the Lord; this is My name, and My glory I will not give to another.
Sing to the Lord a new song, His praise from the ends of the earth.
Let those who descend to the sea and all that fills it, the islands and their inhabitants, raise their voices. Let the wilderness and its cities lift up their voices, the settlements inhabited by Kedar.
Let the inhabitants of Sela sing aloud; let them shout from the tops of the mountains.
These qualities attributed to the Prophet ﷺ have parallels in the Noble Qur’an, while their details are found in the hadiths.
“The islands and their inhabitants” is understood here as referring to the Arabian Peninsula.
Kedar was the name of a son of Ishmael, and his descendants are called Ishmaelites. They were among the inhabitants of the Arabian Peninsula.
According to the author, these passages contain a clear prophecy of the mission of the Prophet ﷺ in the Arabian Peninsula from among the descendants of Ishmael, that his religion would reach the furthest parts of the earth, and that he would not die until truth had been established upon the earth.
Its parallel is the statement of Allah:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My favor upon you, and chosen Islam as your religion.”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:3]
This statement of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr indicates that referring to the scriptures of the People of the Book can be important for demonstrating that the Qur’an confirms the revelations before it and for establishing the proof against them.

13084
Abū Usayd narrated:
We went out with the Prophet ﷺ until we reached a garden called al-Shawṭ. We came to two gardens, and the Prophet ﷺ sat between them and said:
“Sit here.”
He then entered.
A woman from al-Jawn had been brought and lodged in a house among the palm trees, in the dwelling of Umaymah bint al-Nuʿmān ibn Sharāḥīl, accompanied by her wet nurse and caretaker.
When the Prophet ﷺ entered upon her, he said:
“Give yourself to me in marriage.”
She replied:
“Would a queen give herself to an ordinary man?”
He reached out his hand toward her to calm her.
She said:
“I seek refuge in Allah from you.”
He replied:
“You have sought refuge in the One who grants refuge.”
He then came out to us and said:
“Abū Usayd, give her two Rāziqī garments to wear and return her to her family.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5255).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated (5256):
Al-Ḥusayn ibn al-Walīd al-Naysābūrī narrated from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿAbbās ibn Sahl, from his father and Abū Usayd, who said:
The Prophet ﷺ married Umaymah bint Sharāḥīl. When she was brought to him, he extended his hand toward her, but she appeared to dislike it. He therefore instructed Abū Usayd to prepare her for departure and clothe her in two Rāziqī garments.

13085
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
A woman offered herself in marriage to the Prophet ﷺ. A man then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, marry her to me.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“What do you possess?”
He replied:
“I possess nothing.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Go and search, even if only for an iron ring.”
The man went away and returned, saying:
“By Allah, I found nothing, not even an iron ring. But I have this lower garment, and she may have half of it.”
Sahl said that the man had no upper garment.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“What would you do with your lower garment? If you wear it, she will have none of it, and if she wears it, you will have none of it.”
The man sat down. After he had remained seated for a long time, he stood to leave. The Prophet ﷺ saw him and called him back—or someone called him on the Prophet’s behalf.
He asked:
“What portions of the Qur’an do you know?”
The man replied:
“I know Sūrah such-and-such and Sūrah such-and-such,”
and listed several sūrahs.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“We have given her to you in marriage in return for what you know of the Qur’an.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5121) and Muslim (1425). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13086
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A woman came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I have a daughter who is such-and-such,”
and she began mentioning her beauty and attractiveness.
She said:
“I have chosen you for her.”
He replied:
“I accept her.”
The woman continued praising her daughter until she mentioned that she had never suffered a headache and had never complained of any illness.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“I have no need for your daughter.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (12580) and Abū Yaʿlā (4234).

13087
Anas narrated:
A woman came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ offering herself to him in marriage. She said:
“O Messenger of Allah, do you have any need for me?”
Anas’s daughter remarked:
“How little modesty she has! How shameful!”
Anas replied:
“She is better than you. She desired marriage to the Prophet ﷺ, so she offered herself to him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5120).

13088
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I used to feel jealous of the women who offered themselves in marriage to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I would say:
“Can a woman really offer herself to a man?”
When Allah revealed:
“You may defer whichever of them you wish and receive whichever you wish, and there is no blame upon you if you seek any of those whom you had set aside,”
I said:
“By Allah, I see that your Lord hastens to fulfill what you desire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4788) and Muslim (1463).

13089
ʿUrwah narrated:
Khawlah bint Ḥakīm was among the women who offered themselves in marriage to the Prophet ﷺ.
ʿĀʾishah said:
“Is a woman not ashamed to offer herself to a man?”
When the verse:
“You may defer whichever of them you wish…”
was revealed, she said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I see that your Lord hastens to fulfill what you desire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5113) and Muslim (1464:50).

13090
Muʿādhah narrated from ʿĀʾishah:
After the revelation of the verse:
“You may defer whichever of them you wish and receive whichever you wish, and there is no blame upon you if you seek any of those whom you had set aside,”
the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would still ask permission from whichever wife’s day it was before spending that day elsewhere.
Muʿādhah asked ʿĀʾishah:
“What would you say to him?”
She replied:
“I would say: ‘If the choice is mine, O Messenger of Allah, then I do not wish to give preference to anyone over myself regarding you.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4789) and Muslim (1476).

13091
ʿĀʾishah said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not die until women had again been made lawful for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3216), al-Nasāʾī (3204), and Aḥmad (24137).

13092
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
After the command of hijab had been revealed, Sawdah went out to attend to a need. She was a large-bodied woman and could easily be recognized by anyone who knew her.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb saw her and said:
“Sawdah, by Allah, you cannot remain unrecognized by us, so consider carefully how you go out.”
She turned back and returned.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in my house eating his evening meal, holding a piece of meat in his hand.
Sawdah entered and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I went out for one of my needs, and ʿUmar said such-and-such to me.”
Revelation then came to the Prophet ﷺ and afterward ceased, while the piece of meat was still in his hand and he had not put it down.
He said:
“You have been permitted to go out for your needs.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4795) and Muslim (2170).

13093
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The wives of the Prophet ﷺ used to go out at night when they needed to relieve themselves, going to al-Manāṣiʿ, an open and spacious area.
ʿUmar used to say to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Screen your wives.”
But the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not yet done so.
One evening, Sawdah bint Zamʿah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, went out. She was a tall woman.
ʿUmar called out to her:
“We have recognized you, Sawdah!”
He did this because he was eager for the command of hijab to be revealed.
Allah then revealed the verse of hijab.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (146) and Muslim (2170:18).

13094
Anas narrated that ʿUmar said:
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, both righteous and wicked people enter upon you. If only you would instruct the Mothers of the Believers to observe hijab.”
Allah then revealed the verse of hijab.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4790).

13095
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that ʿUmar said:
“I was in agreement with my Lord concerning three matters: the Station of Abraham, the hijab, and the captives of Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2399).

13096
Anas narrated:
When Zaynab’s waiting period had ended, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Zayd:
“Mention me to her as a prospective husband.”
Zayd said:
I went to her while she was preparing dough.
When I saw her, she became so great in my estimation because the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had mentioned marrying her that I could not bring myself to look directly at her. I turned my back toward her and stepped backward.
I said:
“Zaynab, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has sent me to mention you to yourself on his behalf.”
She replied:
“I will not do anything until I seek guidance from my Lord.”
She then stood in her place of prayer.
The Qur’an was revealed, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came and entered upon her without seeking permission.
Anas continued:
I remember the Messenger of Allah ﷺ feeding us bread and meat as the day advanced.
The people left, but several men remained in the house talking after the meal.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out, and I followed him. He began visiting the apartments of his wives and greeting them. They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how did you find your new wife?”
I do not know whether I informed him that the men had finally left or whether he informed me.
He returned and entered the house. I attempted to enter with him, but he lowered the curtain between himself and me, and the command of hijab was revealed.
The people were then admonished with what they were admonished.
Another narration adds the words:
“Do not enter the houses of the Prophet unless permission is given to you for a meal, without waiting for its preparation…”
until:
“Allah is not shy of the truth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1428:89).

13097
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
“I am the most knowledgeable of the people concerning this verse—the verse of hijab.
When Zaynab bint Jaḥsh was brought as a bride to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, she was with him in the house. He prepared food and invited the people.
They sat afterward talking.
The Prophet ﷺ would go out and then return, while they were still sitting there talking.
Allah, the Exalted, then revealed:
‘Do not enter the houses of the Prophet unless permission is given to you for a meal, without waiting for its preparation…’
until:
‘From behind a screen.’
The screen was then put in place, and the people stood and left.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4792).

13098
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ married and consummated his marriage. My mother, Umm Sulaym, prepared a dish of ḥays and placed it in a bowl.
She said:
“Anas, take this to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and say: ‘My mother has sent this to you. She sends you greetings of peace and says: “This is only a small offering from us, O Messenger of Allah.”’”
I took it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“My mother sends you greetings of peace and says: ‘This is only a small offering from us, O Messenger of Allah.’”
He said:
“Put it down.”
Then he said:
“Go and invite so-and-so, so-and-so, and so-and-so for me, and also whoever you encounter.”
He named several men.
I invited those he had named and everyone I met.
The narrator asked Anas:
“Approximately how many people were there?”
He replied:
“Around three hundred.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to me:
“Anas, bring the bowl.”
The people entered until the shaded platform and the room were filled.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Let them form circles of ten at a time, and let each person eat from what is directly in front of him.”
They ate until they were satisfied.
One group left and another entered, until everyone had eaten.
Then he said to me:
“Anas, take it away.”
I removed it, and I do not know whether there had been more food when I first placed it down or when I finally removed it.
Some groups remained sitting and talking in the house of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting there, while his wife had turned her face toward the wall. Their prolonged sitting became burdensome to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He went out, greeted his wives, and then returned.
When the men saw that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had returned, they realized that they had burdened him.
They hurried toward the door and all departed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came, lowered the curtain, and entered, while I remained sitting in the room.
Only a short while passed before he came out to me, and these verses had been revealed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out and recited them to the people:
“O you who believe, do not enter the houses of the Prophet unless permission is given to you for a meal, without waiting for its preparation. But when you are invited, enter; and when you have eaten, disperse, without lingering for conversation. Indeed, that used to trouble the Prophet…”
to the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1428:94).

13099
The Mother of the Believers ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I was eating ḥays with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from a bowl when ʿUmar passed by. The Prophet ﷺ invited him, and he joined us in eating.
His finger touched my finger, and he said:
“Ah!” or “Oh! If he were to follow my view concerning you women, no eye would ever see you.”
Then the command of hijab was revealed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11355), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (1053), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (2971).
Her words:
“Then the hijab was revealed”
do not mean that the verse of hijab was revealed immediately after this particular incident. The verse of hijab was revealed in connection with the marriage of the Prophet ﷺ to Zaynab, as mentioned regarding verse 53 of Sūrat al-Aḥzāb.
Thus, her words mean that the command of hijab was revealed after the incident involving her and ʿUmar and the marriage to Zaynab, all of which occurred within a relatively close period of time.
Benefit: Several circumstances have been mentioned as causes connected with the revelation of the verse of hijab. The meaning is that these circumstances occurred close together in time, and the revelation of the verse was therefore attributed to each of them.
Regarding the words:
“But when you are invited, enter; and when you have eaten, disperse”:

13100
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When one of you is invited by his brother, he should accept the invitation, whether it is a wedding feast or something similar.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1429:100).

13101
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“If I were invited to a meal consisting only of a foreleg or even a sheep’s hoof, I would accept the invitation; and if a foreleg or a sheep’s hoof were given to me as a gift, I would accept it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2568).

13102
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
After the command of hijab had been revealed, Aflaḥ, the brother of Abū al-Quʿays, sought permission to enter upon me.
I said:
“I will not permit him to enter until I ask the Prophet ﷺ, because his brother Abū al-Quʿays was not the one who nursed me; rather, it was the wife of Abū al-Quʿays who nursed me.”
The Prophet ﷺ entered upon me, and I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Aflaḥ, the brother of Abū al-Quʿays, sought permission to enter, but I refused to permit him until I asked you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“What prevented you from allowing your paternal uncle through nursing to enter?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, the man himself did not nurse me; rather, the wife of Abū al-Quʿays nursed me.”
He said:
“Permit him to enter, for he is your uncle. May your right hand be dusted!”
ʿUrwah said:
Because of this, ʿĀʾishah used to say:
“Regard as forbidden through breastfeeding whatever is forbidden through blood relationship.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4796) and Muslim (1445).

13103
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā narrated:
Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah met me and said:
“Shall I give you a gift?”
The Prophet ﷺ came out to us, and we said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we have learned how to greet you with peace, but how should we invoke blessings upon you?”
He replied:
“Say: O Allah, send Your blessings upon Muḥammad and upon the family of Muḥammad, just as You sent Your blessings upon the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. O Allah, bless Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, just as You blessed the family of Abraham. Indeed, You are Praiseworthy, Glorious.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6357) and Muslim (406).

13104
Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The miser is the one in whose presence I am mentioned, yet he does not invoke blessings upon me.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3546), Aḥmad (1736), al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (55, 56), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (909) and al-Ḥākim (1/549).

13105
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever invokes one blessing upon me, Allah will send ten blessings upon him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (408).
Aḥmad (7561) also narrated it, and Ibn Ḥibbān (905) authenticated it with the wording:
“Whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, records ten good deeds for him because of it.”

13106
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever invokes one blessing upon me, Allah sends ten blessings upon him, removes ten sins from him, and raises him ten degrees.”
In another narration from Anas:
“Whoever mentions me should invoke blessings upon me, and whoever invokes one blessing upon me, Allah sends ten blessings upon him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (1297), whose wording is given here, Aḥmad (11998, 13754), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (643), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (904) and al-Ḥākim (1/550). Its chain is good because of Yūnus ibn Abī Isḥāq.

13107
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out and encountered Abū Ṭalḥah. Abū Ṭalḥah stood and went forward to meet him, saying:
“May my father and mother be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah. I can see happiness upon your face.”
He replied:
“Yes. Gabriel came to me just now and said: ‘Muḥammad, whoever invokes blessings upon you once, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, records ten good deeds for him, erases ten evil deeds from him, and raises him ten degrees because of it.’”
Ibn Ḥabīb, one of the narrators of the hadith, said:
“As far as I know, he also said: ‘And the angels invoke blessings upon him ten times.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Baghawī in al-Jaʿdiyyāt (3058), and through his route by Ibn Bishrān in his Amālī (1192) and al-Khaṭīb in Talkhīṣ al-Mutashābih (250).

13108
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Do you know what backbiting is?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It is mentioning your brother in a manner that he dislikes.”
Someone asked:
“What if what I say about my brother is actually true?”
He replied:
“If what you say is truly found in him, then you have backbitten him. If it is not found in him, then you have slandered him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2589).

13109
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The Children of Israel used to bathe naked, looking at one another, whereas Moses would bathe by himself.
They said: ‘By Allah, nothing prevents Moses from bathing with us except that he has an enlarged testicle.’
One day he went to bathe and placed his clothes upon a stone. The stone ran away with his clothes, so Moses ran after it saying: ‘My clothes, O stone! My clothes, O stone!’
Eventually the Children of Israel saw Moses and said: ‘By Allah, there is nothing wrong with Moses.’
He took his clothes and then began striking the stone.”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“By Allah, there remained six or seven marks upon the stone from his blows.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (278), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (339).

13110
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Moses was an extremely modest man who kept himself covered. Nothing of his skin could be seen because of his modesty.
Some of the Children of Israel harmed him by saying: ‘He only covers himself so completely because of some defect in his skin—either leprosy, an enlarged testicle, or some other affliction.’
Allah wished to clear Moses of what they were saying. One day Moses went off by himself, placed his clothes upon a stone, and bathed.
When he finished, he went to retrieve his clothes, but the stone ran away carrying them.
Moses took his staff and chased the stone, saying: ‘My clothes, O stone! My clothes, O stone!’
Eventually he reached a gathering of the Children of Israel. They saw him naked and saw that he possessed one of the finest bodies Allah had created. Allah thus cleared him of what they had been saying.
The stone stopped. Moses took his clothes, put them on, and began striking the stone with his staff.
By Allah, the stone retained three, four, or five marks from his blows.
That is the meaning of Allah’s words: ‘O you who believe, do not be like those who harmed Moses, but Allah cleared him of what they said, and he was highly honored in the sight of Allah.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3404).

13111
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Do not be like those who harmed Moses, but Allah cleared him of what they said, and he was highly honored in the sight of Allah”:
His people said that he suffered from an enlarged testicle.
One day he went out to bathe and placed his clothes upon a rock. The rock began moving swiftly away with his clothes, and Moses ran after it naked until it reached the gatherings of the Children of Israel.
They then saw that he did not have the defect they had accused him of.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“This is the meaning of the words: ‘Allah cleared him of what they said, and he was highly honored in the sight of Allah.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (32509), al-Ḥākim (2/422), and Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (19/190–191).
The harm that the Children of Israel inflicted upon Moses, peace be upon him, was far more extensive than this. The Qur’an mentions several examples, including the following:
1-
“Moses said to his people: ‘Seek help through Allah and remain patient. Indeed, the earth belongs to Allah. He gives it as an inheritance to whomever He wills among His servants, and the good end belongs to the God-fearing.’ They said: ‘We were harmed before you came to us and after you came to us.’ He said: ‘Perhaps your Lord will destroy your enemy and make you successors upon the earth, then see how you act.’”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:128–129]
2-
“After Moses departed, his people made from their ornaments a bodily calf that produced a lowing sound. Did they not see that it could neither speak to them nor guide them to a way? They took it for worship and were wrongdoers. When regret overtook them and they realized they had gone astray, they said: ‘If our Lord does not have mercy upon us and forgive us, we will surely be among the losers.’ When Moses returned to his people, angry and deeply grieved, he said: ‘How evil is what you did in my absence! Were you impatient concerning the command of your Lord?’ He threw down the tablets and seized his brother by the head, pulling him toward himself. Aaron said: ‘Son of my mother, the people overpowered me and nearly killed me. Do not let the enemies rejoice over me, and do not place me among the wrongdoing people.’ Moses said: ‘My Lord, forgive me and my brother and admit us into Your mercy, for You are the Most Merciful of those who show mercy.’ Indeed, those who took the calf for worship will be overtaken by wrath from their Lord and humiliation in the worldly life. Thus do We recompense those who fabricate falsehood. But those who commit evil deeds and afterward repent and believe—indeed, your Lord after that is Ever-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”
 [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:148–153]
3-
“And when you said: ‘Moses, we will never believe you until we see Allah openly,’ the thunderbolt seized you while you were looking on.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:55]
4-
“And when you said: ‘Moses, we cannot endure one kind of food, so call upon your Lord for us to bring forth for us some of what the earth grows—its herbs, cucumbers, garlic, lentils, and onions.’ He said: ‘Would you exchange what is better for what is inferior? Go down to any town and you will have what you have asked for.’ Humiliation and poverty were imposed upon them, and they incurred wrath from Allah. That was because they used to disbelieve in the signs of Allah and kill the prophets unjustly. That was because they disobeyed and habitually transgressed.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:61]
5-
“And when Moses said to his people: ‘Allah commands you to slaughter a cow,’ they said: ‘Are you taking us in mockery?’ He replied: ‘I seek refuge in Allah from being among the ignorant.’ They said: ‘Call upon your Lord for us to clarify what kind it should be.’ He said: ‘He says it should be a cow neither old nor young, but of middle age between the two. So do what you are commanded.’ They said: ‘Call upon your Lord for us to clarify its color.’ He said: ‘He says it should be a bright yellow cow, pleasing to those who look at it.’ They said: ‘Call upon your Lord for us to clarify what exactly it is, for all cows look alike to us. If Allah wills, we will surely be guided.’ He said: ‘He says it is a cow neither trained to plow the earth nor irrigate crops, sound and without blemish.’ They said: ‘Now you have brought the truth.’ So they slaughtered it, although they almost failed to do so.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:67–71]
6-
“And when We took your covenant and raised the mountain above you, saying: ‘Hold firmly to what We have given you and listen,’ they said: ‘We hear and we disobey.’ Their hearts were filled with love of the calf because of their disbelief. Say: ‘How evil is what your supposed faith commands you to do, if you are indeed believers.’”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:93]
7-
“And when Moses said to his people: ‘My people, remember Allah’s favor upon you when He placed prophets among you, made you rulers, and gave you what He had not given anyone among the peoples. My people, enter the sacred land which Allah has decreed for you, and do not turn back, lest you return as losers.’ They said: ‘Moses, there is a mighty people there, and we will never enter it until they leave. If they leave it, then we will enter.’ Two men from among those who feared Allah and whom Allah had blessed said: ‘Enter upon them through the gate. Once you enter it, you will surely be victorious. Put your trust in Allah if you are truly believers.’ They said: ‘Moses, we will never enter it as long as they remain there. So go, you and your Lord, and fight. We will sit right here.’ Moses said: ‘My Lord, I have authority over no one except myself and my brother, so separate us from the defiantly disobedient people.’ Allah said: ‘Then it is forbidden to them for forty years. They will wander throughout the land, so do not grieve over the defiantly disobedient people.’”
 [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:20–26]
8-
“And when Moses said to his people: ‘My people, why do you harm me when you know that I am the Messenger of Allah to you?’ When they deviated, Allah caused their hearts to deviate. Allah does not guide the defiantly disobedient people.”
 [Sūrat al-Ṣaff 61:5]

13112
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A man asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about Sabaʾ:
“What is Sabaʾ? Is it a man, a woman, or a land?”
He replied:
“Rather, he was a man who fathered ten sons. Six of them settled in Yemen and four in al-Shām. As for the Yemenite tribes, they are Madhḥij, Kindah, al-Azd, al-Ashʿariyyūn, Anmār, and Ḥimyar—all of them Arabs. As for those who settled in al-Shām, they are Lakhm, Judhām, ʿĀmilah, and Ghassān.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2898) and al-Ḥākim (2/423).

13113
Farwah ibn Musayk al-Ghuṭayfī narrated:
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, should I fight those of my people who have turned away, with the help of those who have accepted Islam?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Then I reconsidered and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, no—rather, the people of Sabaʾ, for they are mightier and stronger.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ instructed me and gave me permission to fight them.
After I had left him, Allah revealed concerning Sabaʾ what He revealed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“What has happened to al-Ghuṭayfī?”
Someone was sent to my residence and found that I had already departed, so I was brought back.
When I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I found him sitting with his Companions.
He said:
“Rather, call the people to Islam. Whoever responds, accept it from him. Whoever does not respond, do not hasten against him until you come back to me.”
A man among the people then asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell us about Sabaʾ. Is it a land or a woman?”
He replied:
“It is neither a land nor a woman. Rather, he was a man who fathered ten Arab sons. Six went south toward Yemen and four went north toward al-Shām. Those who went north were Lakhm, Judhām, Ghassān, and ʿĀmilah. Those who went south were al-Azd, Kindah, Ḥimyar, al-Ashʿariyyūn, Anmār, and Madhḥij.”
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, who are Anmār?”
He replied:
“They are those from whom Khathʿam and Bajīlah descended.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3988), al-Tirmidhī (3222), and Aḥmad (24009, 24088).

13114
Ṣuhayb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How remarkable is the affair of the believer! All of his affairs are good, and this is true of no one except the believer. If prosperity reaches him, he gives thanks, and that is good for him. If hardship reaches him, he remains patient, and that is good for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2999).

13115
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“When Allah decrees a matter in heaven, the angels beat their wings in submission to His words, sounding like a chain striking a smooth rock.
Then, when fear is removed from their hearts, they ask: ‘What did your Lord say?’ They reply: ‘The truth, and He is the Most High, the Most Great.’
Those who steal a hearing listen to this, and these eavesdroppers are positioned one above another.”
Sufyān demonstrated this with his hand, tilting it and spreading his fingers apart.
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“One of them hears a word and passes it to the one beneath him, then the next passes it to the one beneath him, until it is cast upon the tongue of a sorcerer or soothsayer.
Sometimes a shooting star strikes him before he can pass it on, and sometimes he passes it on before the shooting star reaches him. The soothsayer then adds one hundred lies to it.
People then say: ‘Did he not tell us on such-and-such a day that such-and-such would happen?’ So he is believed because of that one word which was heard from heaven.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4800).

13116
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A man from among the Anṣār, one of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ, informed me that one night, while they were sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, a shooting star was cast and lit up the sky.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked them:
“What did you used to say during the pre-Islamic period when something like this occurred?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best. We used to say: ‘A great man has been born tonight,’ or, ‘A great man has died tonight.’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“These stars are not cast because of anyone’s death or birth. Rather, when our Lord—Blessed and Exalted is His Name—decrees a matter, the bearers of the Throne glorify Him. Then the inhabitants of the heaven nearest to them glorify Him, until the glorification reaches the inhabitants of this lowest heaven.
Then those nearest to the bearers of the Throne ask them: ‘What did your Lord say?’ They inform them of what He said.
The inhabitants of each heaven then ask one another until the news reaches the lowest heaven. The jinn snatch some of what they hear and cast it to their allies, while shooting stars are hurled at them. Whatever they transmit exactly as they heard it is true, but they mix falsehood into it and add to it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2229).

13117
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been given five things that no one before me was given. Every prophet before me was sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to every red and black person.
Spoils of war have been made lawful for me, whereas they were not lawful for anyone before me.
The earth has been made pure for me, a means of purification, and a place of prayer, so whenever the time for prayer reaches any man, he may pray wherever he is.
I have been granted victory through fear cast into the enemy from a month’s journey away, and I have been granted intercession.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521). The wording is that of Muslim.

13118
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Successful indeed is the one who accepts Islam, is provided with enough to meet his needs, and whom Allah makes content with what He has given him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1054).

13119
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah does not look at your outward forms or your wealth. Rather, He looks at your hearts and your deeds.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2564:34).
Regarding the words:
“Except those who believe and perform righteous deeds. Those will receive multiplied reward for what they did, and they will be secure in the lofty chambers”:

13120
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The people of Paradise will look up at the inhabitants of the lofty chambers above them just as you look at a brilliant star remaining on the distant eastern or western horizon, because of the difference in rank between them.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are those the dwellings of the prophets which no one else can attain?”
He replied:
“Yes. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, they are men who believed in Allah and affirmed the messengers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3256) and Muslim (2831).

13121
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah said to me: ‘Spend, and I will spend upon you.’”
He also said:
“The Hand of Allah is full. Spending by night and day does not diminish what is with Him.”
And he said:
“Have you considered how much He has spent since He created the heavens and the earth? It has not diminished what is in His Hand. His Throne was upon the water, and in His Hand is the balance; He lowers and raises.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4684) and Muslim (993). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13122
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no morning in which the servants wake except that two angels descend. One of them says: ‘O Allah, give the one who spends a replacement.’ The other says: ‘O Allah, give the one who withholds destruction.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1442) and Muslim (1010).

13123
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
One day the Prophet ﷺ climbed al-Ṣafā and called out:
“O people, beware of an approaching danger!”
Quraysh gathered around him and asked:
“What is the matter?”
He said:
“If I were to tell you that an enemy was going to attack you in the morning or evening, would you believe me?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then I am a warner to you before a severe punishment.”
Abū Lahab said:
“May you perish! Is this why you gathered us?”
Allah then revealed:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and may he perish.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4801) and Muslim (208). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s narration is longer.

13124
Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
One day the Prophet ﷺ came out to us and called out three times:
“People, do you know what my likeness and your likeness are?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“My likeness and your likeness are that of a people who feared that an enemy was approaching them. They sent a man ahead to watch for the enemy. While he was keeping watch, he saw the enemy approaching.
He hurried back to warn his people, fearing that the enemy might overtake him before he could warn them. So he began waving his garment and shouting: ‘People, you are about to be attacked! People, you are about to be attacked!’”
He repeated it three times.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22948).

13125
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah while 360 idols surrounded the Kaʿbah.
He began striking them with a stick in his hand while saying:
“Truth has come, and falsehood has vanished. Indeed, falsehood is bound to vanish.”
 [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:81]
and:
“Say: Truth has come, and falsehood can neither originate anything nor restore it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4720) and Muslim (1781).
This divine declaration has been fulfilled. After the coming of the truth, there is no region of the earth except that the true God is worshipped there alone.
Falsehood can never eliminate truth completely. Rather, truth will remain until the establishment of the Hour, whereas before the coming of this truth there was no region on earth in which the true God alone was worshipped.

13126
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
We were traveling with the Prophet ﷺ, and the people began raising their voices loudly with the declaration of Allah’s greatness.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Be gentle with yourselves. You are not calling upon One who is deaf or absent. You are calling upon One who is All-Hearing and Near, and He is with you.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4202) and Muslim (2704).
The words:
“Say: If I were to go astray”
are phrased as a hypothetical concession in debate with the deniers who accused the Prophet ﷺ of being misguided.
The meaning is that, if he were hypothetically misguided, the consequences of that misguidance would fall upon himself alone and would not extend to anyone else.
Guidance, however, comes through revelation from Allah, Who hears your words and voices and will call you to account for failing to believe in Him. He is also Near to those who call upon Him and ask Him.
For this reason, the Prophet ﷺ instructed people to lower their voices when supplicating, declaring Allah’s greatness, and saying lā ilāha illa Allah.

13127
Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ saw Gabriel with six hundred wings.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3232) and Muslim (174), both through Abū Isḥāq al-Shaybānī, who said:
I asked Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh about the words of Allah:
“And he was at a distance of two bow-lengths or nearer.”
 [Sūrat al-Najm 53:9]
He replied:
“ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd informed me,”
and then related the hadith.

13128
Warrād, the secretary of al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah, narrated:
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah dictated to me in a letter addressed to Muʿāwiyah that the Prophet ﷺ used to say after every obligatory prayer:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things. O Allah, none can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold, and no possessor of worldly fortune can benefit from his fortune against You.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (844) and Muslim (593).

13129
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his head from bowing, he would say:
“Our Lord, to You belongs all praise, filling the heavens and the earth and filling whatever else You will beyond them. You are worthy of praise and glory. This is the truest thing a servant can say, and all of us are Your servants. O Allah, none can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold, and no possessor of worldly fortune can benefit from his fortune against You.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (477).

13130
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, created His creation in darkness, then cast upon them from His light. Whoever was reached by that light was guided, and whoever it missed went astray.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2644), Aḥmad (6644), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6169), and narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/30).

13131
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among the words by which you glorify the majesty of Allah are saying ‘Subḥān Allah,’ ‘Lā ilāha illa Allah,’ and ‘al-Ḥamdu lillāh.’ These words circle around the Throne with a humming sound like the humming of bees, mentioning the person who uttered them. Would any one of you not love to have something that continually mentions him before Allah?”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3809).

13132
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
I was sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in a gathering when a man arrived and greeted the Prophet ﷺ and the people, saying:
“Peace be upon you.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“And upon you be peace, the mercy of Allah, and His blessings.”
When the man sat down, he said:
