“All praise belongs to Allah—abundant, pure, and blessed praise, as our Lord loves and is pleased with.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“What did you say?”
The man repeated exactly what he had said.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, ten angels rushed toward those words, each eager to write them down. They did not know how to record them, until they raised them to the Possessor of Might. He said: ‘Write them exactly as My servant said them.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (341), Aḥmad (12612), Ibn Ḥibbān (845), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (1887).

13133
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“People must stop boasting about their deceased forefathers, who are merely fuel for Hell, or else they will become more insignificant before Allah than the dung beetle that rolls filth with its nose. Allah has removed from you the arrogance of the pre-Islamic period and its boasting about ancestry. A person is either a God-fearing believer or a wretched sinner. All people are children of Adam, and Adam was created from dust.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3955) and Aḥmad (10781).
Regarding the words:
“No female conceives or gives birth except with His knowledge”:

13134
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The keys of the unseen are five, known only to Allah: no one knows what will happen tomorrow; no one knows what is within the wombs; no soul knows what it will earn tomorrow; no soul knows in what land it will die; and no one knows when the rain will come.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1039).
The expression:
“What is within the wombs”
is not limited merely to knowing whether the unborn child is male or female. Rather, it encompasses every aspect of the child’s life and destiny, such as whether he will be blessed or wretched, rich or poor, and other such matters.
Regarding the words:
“No one is granted a long life, nor is anything diminished from his lifespan, except that it is recorded in a Book”:

13135
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever would like his provision to be expanded and his lifespan to be extended should maintain the ties of kinship.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2067) and Muslim (2557).
The meaning is that whether a lifespan is lengthened or shortened through a cause or without an apparent cause, all of it is already known and recorded with Allah, Mighty and Majestic.

13136
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ stood over the well of Badr and said:
“Have you found what your Lord promised you to be true?”
He then said:
“They are now hearing what I am saying.”
When this was mentioned to ʿĀʾishah, she said:
The Prophet ﷺ only said:
“They now know that what I used to tell them was the truth.”
She then recited:
“Indeed, you cannot make the dead hear.”
 [Sūrat al-Naml 27:80]
and continued reciting the verse.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3980) and Muslim (932:26).

13137
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that the slain men be thrown into the well, and they were cast into it, except for Umayyah ibn Khalaf. His body had swollen inside his armor until it filled it. When they tried to move him, his body began to come apart, so they left him where he was and covered him with enough earth and stones to conceal him.
After the others had been cast into the well, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood over them and said:
“People of the well, have you found what your Lord promised you to be true? I have certainly found what my Lord promised me to be true.”
His Companions asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are you speaking to dead people?”
He replied:
“They now know that what I promised them was true.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“People say: ‘They heard what you said to them,’ but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ actually said: ‘They now know.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26361), Ibn Ḥibbān (7088), and al-Ḥākim (3/224).

13138
Abū Ḥāzim narrated:
I was standing behind Abū Hurayrah while he was performing ablution for prayer. He kept washing his arm until the water reached his armpit.
I asked:
“Abū Hurayrah, what kind of ablution is this?”
He replied:
“Children of Farrūkh, are you here? Had I known you were present, I would not have performed ablution in this manner. I heard the Prophet ﷺ, my close beloved, say:
‘The adornment of the believer in Paradise will reach as far as the water of his ablution reaches.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (250).
Regarding the words:
“And their clothing therein will be silk”:

13139
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā narrated:
They were with Ḥudhayfah when he asked for a drink. A Magian gave him water. As soon as the man placed the vessel in Ḥudhayfah’s hand, Ḥudhayfah threw it at him.
He then said:
“Had I not forbidden him from doing this more than once or twice…”
as though explaining that he would not otherwise have acted so severely.
He continued:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Do not wear silk or brocade, and do not drink from vessels of gold and silver.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5426) and Muslim (2067).

13140
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No one’s deeds alone will admit him into Paradise.”
They asked:
“Not even you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Not even me, unless Allah envelops me in His grace and mercy. So remain upright and strive to come close to perfection. None of you should wish for death. If he is a righteous person, perhaps he will increase in goodness; and if he is a sinner, perhaps he will seek Allah’s pleasure through repentance.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5673) and Muslim (2816:75).

13141
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“As for the people of the Fire who are its permanent inhabitants, they will neither die therein nor truly live. But there will be people whom the Fire reaches because of their sins—or he said, because of their wrongdoings—and it will cause them to die a kind of death.
When they have become like charcoal, permission for intercession will be granted. They will be brought out in groups and scattered along the rivers of Paradise.
Then it will be said: ‘People of Paradise, pour water over them.’ They will then grow like a seed that sprouts in the debris carried by a flood.”
A man among the people remarked:
“It is as though the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had lived in the desert.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (185).

13142
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah has left no excuse for a person whose lifespan He prolonged until he reached sixty years of age.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6419).
The meaning of the hadith is that Allah has left such a person no valid excuse to cling to. Reaching sixty years of age should compel a person toward obedience, seeking forgiveness, and turning toward the Hereafter.
A young person, when told to repent from sin, may say:
“I will repent when I grow old.”
But an elderly person who has already lived a long life has nothing before him except repentance.

13143
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The lifespans of my nation are generally between sixty and seventy years, and only a small number of them exceed that.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3550), Ibn Mājah (4236), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2980), and narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/427).

13144
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
Banū Salamah lived in an outlying part of Madinah and wanted to move closer to the mosque. Then this verse was revealed:
“Indeed, We give life to the dead, and We record what they have sent ahead and the traces they leave behind.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to them:
“Your footsteps are being recorded, so do not move.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3226), Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (19/410), and al-Ḥākim (2/428–429).

13145
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The areas surrounding the mosque became vacant, so Banū Salamah wanted to move closer to the mosque.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard about this, he asked them:
“I have been informed that you intend to move closer to the mosque?”
They replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah. That is what we intended.”
He said:
“Banū Salamah, remain in your homes; your footsteps are being recorded. Remain in your homes; your footsteps are being recorded.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (665).
Another route through Abū Naḍrah adds:
“They said: ‘We would not have been pleased if we had actually moved.’”

13146
Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the early part of the day when a group of people came to him barefoot and inadequately clothed. They were wearing striped woolen garments or cloaks with openings cut through them and were carrying swords.
Most of them were from Muḍar—in fact, all of them were from Muḍar.
The face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ changed when he saw the severe poverty they were suffering.
He went inside and then came back out. He instructed Bilāl, who called the adhān and then the iqāmah. The Prophet ﷺ prayed and afterward delivered a sermon.
He recited:
“O mankind, fear your Lord, Who created you from a single soul…”
until the end of the verse:
“Indeed, Allah is ever Watchful over you.”
 [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:1]
He also recited the verse from Sūrat al-Ḥashr:
“Fear Allah, and let every soul consider what it has sent ahead for tomorrow, and fear Allah.”
 [Sūrat al-Ḥashr 59:18]
Then he said:
“Let a man give charity from his dīnār, from his dirham, from his clothing, from a sāʿ of wheat, or from a sāʿ of dates.”
He continued until he said:
“Even if only half a date.”
Then a man from the Anṣār came carrying a pouch so heavy that his hand could barely hold it—in fact, it could not properly hold it.
After that, people followed one another in giving until I saw two heaps, one of food and another of clothing.
I then saw the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ shining with joy, as though it were gilded.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever establishes a good practice in Islam will receive its reward and the reward of everyone who follows it after him, without anything being diminished from their rewards. And whoever establishes an evil practice in Islam will bear its sin and the sin of everyone who follows it after him, without anything being diminished from their sins.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1017).

13147
Abū Dharr narrated:
I asked the Prophet ﷺ about the words of Allah:
“And the sun runs toward its appointed resting place.”
He replied:
“Its resting place is beneath the Throne.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4803) and Muslim (159:251).

13148
Abū Dharr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ asked one day:
“Do you know where this sun goes?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It continues on its course until it reaches its resting place beneath the Throne. It then falls down in prostration and remains in that state until it is told: ‘Rise and return from where you came.’ It then returns and rises from its normal place of rising.
It continues again until it reaches its resting place beneath the Throne and falls down in prostration. It remains there until it is told: ‘Rise and return from where you came.’ So it returns and rises from its normal place of rising.
It will continue doing this without people noticing anything unusual about it, until it reaches that resting place beneath the Throne and is told: ‘Rise, but this time rise from your place of setting.’ Then it will rise from the west.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then asked:
“Do you know when that will occur? It will occur when: ‘No soul will benefit from believing at that time if it had not believed before or earned some good through its faith.’”
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:158]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4802) and Muslim (159).

13149
Abū Dharr narrated:
When the sun set, the Prophet ﷺ said to me:
“Do you know where it goes?”
I replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It goes until it prostrates beneath the Throne. It then seeks permission and permission is granted to it. A time will soon come when it will prostrate but its prostration will not be accepted, and it will seek permission but permission will not be granted.
It will then be told: ‘Return from where you came,’ and it will rise from the west.
That is the meaning of Allah’s words: ‘And the sun runs toward its appointed resting place. That is the determination of the Almighty, the All-Knowing.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3199) and Muslim (159).

13150
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, in the hadith concerning seeing the Lord on the Day of Resurrection:
“Then it will be said to him: ‘Now We will bring Our witness against you.’
He will think to himself: ‘Who could possibly testify against me?’
His mouth will then be sealed, and it will be said to his thigh, flesh, and bones: ‘Speak.’ His thigh, flesh, and bones will then speak concerning his deeds.
This will be so that no excuse remains for him against himself. This is the hypocrite, the one with whom Allah is angry.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2968).

13151
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he laughed.
He asked:
“Do you know what made me laugh?”
We replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“I laughed at the conversation between a servant and his Lord.
The servant will say: ‘My Lord, did You not protect me from injustice?’
Allah will say: ‘Yes.’
The servant will then say: ‘I will accept no witness against myself except one from my own self.’
Allah will say: ‘Today you yourself are sufficient as a witness against you, and the noble recording angels are sufficient as witnesses.’
His mouth will then be sealed, and it will be said to his limbs: ‘Speak.’
They will speak concerning his deeds.
Then he will again be allowed to speak, and he will say to his own limbs: ‘Away with you! Ruin upon you! It was on your behalf that I was arguing!’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2969).

13152
Bahz ibn Ḥakīm narrated from his father, from his grandfather, who said:
When I came to the Prophet ﷺ, I said:
“By Allah, I did not come to you until I had sworn more times than the number of these fingers that I would neither come to you nor accept your religion.”
Bahz joined his two hands together to indicate the number.
He continued:
“I was a man who understood nothing except what Allah and His Messenger taught me. I ask you by the Face of Allah: with what has Allah sent you to us?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“With Islam.”
I asked:
“What are the signs of Islam?”
He replied:
“That you say: ‘I have submitted my face to Allah and renounced everything besides Him’; that you establish prayer and give zakāh. Every Muslim is sacred to another Muslim. They are two brothers who support one another.
Allah does not accept from a polytheist who returns to polytheism after accepting Islam any deed, and you must separate yourself from the polytheists and join the Muslims.
Why am I holding you by your waistbands, trying to keep you away from the Fire? Indeed, my Lord will call me and ask me: ‘Did you convey the message to My servants?’ I will say: ‘My Lord, I conveyed it to them.’ So let those of you who are present convey it to those who are absent.
Then you will be summoned with your mouths restrained as though by bridles, and the first parts of each of you to speak will be his thigh and his palm.”
I asked:
“O Prophet of Allah, is this our religion?”
He replied:
“This is your religion, and wherever you perform a good deed, it will suffice you.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (20043).

13153
Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated that a man from Qays asked him:
“Did you flee from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of Ḥunayn?”
Al-Barāʾ replied:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not flee. Hawāzin were skilled archers that day. When we charged them, they retreated, so we turned toward the spoils. They then faced us again with arrows.
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ upon his white mule while Abū Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith was holding its reins, and the Prophet ﷺ was saying:
“I am the Prophet—there is no lie. I am the son of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4317) and Muslim (1776:80).

13154
Jundub ibn Sufyān narrated:
During one of the military expeditions, the finger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ began to bleed, and he said:
“You are only a finger that has bled, and what you have suffered is in the path of Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2802) and Muslim (1796).

13155
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
I saw the Prophet ﷺ on the Day of the Trench carrying earth until the dust covered the hair of his chest—and he was a man with abundant body hair.
He was chanting the verses of ʿAbdullāh ibn Rawāḥah:
“O Allah, were it not for You, we would never have been guided, nor would we have given charity or prayed.
So send tranquility down upon us, and make our feet firm when we meet the enemy.
Indeed, the enemies have transgressed against us, and whenever they seek to subject us to temptation, we refuse.”
He raised his voice while reciting them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3034), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (1803:125).
As for the narrations claiming that the Prophet ﷺ recited certain lines of poetry while failing to maintain their poetic meter, all of those reports contain defects.
The Prophet ﷺ was the most eloquent of the Arabs, so matters such as poetic meter would not have escaped him, and it is unlikely that such mistakes occurred from him.
Poetry, when free of obscenity, evil, and pre-Islamic calls and sentiments, is permissible to compose, memorize, and recite. Indeed, it may be praiseworthy and desirable when used to instill virtues in people, praise the Prophet ﷺ, call others to Islam, and defend it.
The Prophet ﷺ used to encourage his Companions to defend Islam through their poetry. He would ask them to recite poetry and would listen to them.
The detailed discussion of this subject is given in the commentary on Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ, verses 224–227.

13156
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil took a bone from the gravelly plain of Makkah and crushed it with his hand. He then said to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Will Allah, the Exalted, bring this back to life after what I have just done to it?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Yes. Allah will cause you to die, then resurrect you, and then admit you into Hell.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Then the final verses of Sūrat Yā Sīn were revealed.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as mentioned by Ibn Kathīr, Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (19/487), and al-Ḥākim (2/429).

13157
Busr ibn Jaḥḥāsh al-Qurashī narrated:
One day the Prophet ﷺ spat into his palm and placed his finger upon it. Then he said:
“Allah says: ‘Son of Adam, how can you think yourself beyond My power when I created you from something like this? Then, after I formed you and proportioned you, you walked proudly between two garments while the earth resounded beneath your steps. You accumulated wealth and withheld it. Then, when your soul reached your collarbones, you said: “Now I will give charity!” But how could that be the time for charity?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2707) and Aḥmad (17842), whose wording is given here, and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/502).

13158
Ḥudhayfah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There was a man among those who lived before you who thought very badly of his own deeds. He said to his family: ‘When I die, take me and scatter me into the sea on a hot day.’
They did so. Allah then gathered him together and asked: ‘What caused you to do what you did?’
He replied: ‘Nothing caused me to do it except fear of You.’
So Allah forgave him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6480).
Regarding the words:
“The One who made for you fire from the green tree, and behold, from it you ignite,”
He is the One who brings forth burning fire for you from moist, green trees.
Regarding the words:
“So glory be to the One in Whose hand is the dominion of all things,”
this is similar to His words:
“Say: In whose hand is the dominion of all things?”
 [Sūrat al-Muʾminūn 23:88]
and His words:
“Blessed is the One in Whose hand is all dominion.”
 [Sūrat al-Mulk 67:1]
Malakūt and mulk have the same essential meaning. Their linguistic relationship is similar to forms such as raḥmah and raḥamūt, or jabr and jabarūt, and other comparable constructions.
The Prophet ﷺ used to say while bowing and prostrating:
“Glory be to the Possessor of absolute might and dominion,”
as stated in the following hadith:

13159
ʿAwf ibn Mālik al-Ashjaʿī narrated:
I stood in prayer with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ one night.
He stood and recited Sūrat al-Baqarah. Whenever he passed a verse mentioning mercy, he stopped and asked Allah for it; and whenever he passed a verse mentioning punishment, he stopped and sought refuge with Allah from it.
He then bowed for approximately as long as he had stood, saying while bowing:
“Glory be to the Possessor of absolute might, dominion, majesty, and greatness.”
He then prostrated for approximately as long as he had stood and said the same words in his prostration.
He then stood and recited Sūrat Āl ʿImrān, and afterward continued reciting one sūrah after another.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (873), al-Nasāʾī (1132), and al-Tirmidhī in al-Shamāʾil (306).

13160
Jābir ibn Samurah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Will you not form your rows as the angels form their rows before their Lord?”
We asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, how do the angels form their rows before their Lord?”
He replied:
“They complete the first rows and stand closely together in the row.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (430).

13161
Ḥudhayfah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“We have been favored over other people in three ways: our rows have been made like the rows of the angels; the entire earth has been made a place of prayer for us; and its soil has been made a means of purification for us when we cannot find water.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (522).

13162
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The jinn used to ascend toward the heaven and listen to the revelation. Whenever they heard a single word, they would add nine things to it. The one word they had heard would be true, while what they added would be false.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sent, they were barred from their former listening posts.
They informed Iblīs of what had happened. Before that, the stars had not been hurled at them in this manner.
Iblīs said:
“This can only be because some new event has occurred on earth.”
He sent out his forces, and they found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ standing in prayer between two mountains—Ibn ʿAbbās thought he said in Makkah.
They returned and informed Iblīs.
He said:
“This is the event that has occurred on earth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1/274), al-Tirmidhī (3324), whose wording is given here, and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11562).

13163
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been commanded to fight the people until they say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’ Whoever says, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ has protected his wealth and his life from me, except where a right is due upon them, and his reckoning is with Allah.”
Allah also revealed in His Book regarding a people who behaved arrogantly:
“Indeed, when it was said to them, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ they would become arrogant.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (10/3210).

13164
Mujāhid narrated:
The people were circumambulating the House while Ibn ʿAbbās was sitting with a hooked staff beside him. He said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“‘O you who believe, fear Allah as He deserves to be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.’
 [Āl ʿImrān 3:102]
If a single drop from the Tree of Zaqqūm were to fall into this world, it would ruin the livelihood of the people of the earth. So what will be the condition of the person who has no food except Zaqqūm?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2585), Ibn Mājah (4326), Aḥmad (2735), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7470) and al-Ḥākim (2/294).

13165
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Abraham, the Prophet, peace be upon him, never made a statement contrary to outward reality except on three occasions. Two of them were for the sake of Allah: his saying, ‘Indeed, I am ill,’ and his saying, ‘Rather, this largest one of them did it.’
 [Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:63]
The third concerned Sarah.
Abraham entered the land of a tyrant with Sarah, and she was among the most beautiful of people. He said to her: ‘If this tyrant learns that you are my wife, he will overpower me concerning you. So if he asks you, tell him that you are my sister, for you are indeed my sister in Islam. I know of no Muslim upon the earth besides myself and you.’
When he entered the tyrant’s territory, one of the tyrant’s men saw her and said to him: ‘A woman has entered your land who is fit for no one but you.’
He sent for her, and she was brought to him.
Abraham stood in prayer.
When she entered upon the tyrant, he could not restrain himself and stretched his hand toward her, but his hand was seized with a severe seizure.
He said to her: ‘Call upon Allah to release my hand, and I will not harm you.’
She did so.
He tried again, and his hand was seized even more severely than the first time.
He made the same request, and she supplicated for him.
He tried a third time, and his hand was seized even more severely than on the first two occasions.
He said: ‘Call upon Allah to release my hand, and you have my pledge before Allah that I will not harm you.’
She supplicated, and his hand was released.
He summoned the man who had brought her and said: ‘You brought me a devil, not a human being. Take her out of my land.’ He also gave Hājar to her.
Sarah returned walking. When Abraham saw her, he finished his prayer and asked: ‘What happened?’
She replied: ‘Good. Allah restrained the hand of the wicked man and gave me a servant.’”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“That is your mother, O descendants of the water of the sky.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3357, 3358) and Muslim (2371:154). This is Muslim’s wording.

13166
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
I spent one night at the house of my maternal aunt Maymūnah.
The Prophet ﷺ rose during the night. At one point in the night, he got up and performed a light ablution using water from a hanging waterskin—ʿAmr demonstrated how brief and little it was—and then stood to pray.
I performed ablution in a similar manner, came and stood on his left—or, as Sufyān sometimes said, on his left side—and he moved me around so that I stood on his right.
He then prayed for as long as Allah willed.
Afterward he lay down and slept until the sound of his breathing could be heard.
Then the caller came and informed him that it was time for prayer. He went with him and prayed without performing ablution again.
We said to ʿAmr:
“Some people say that the eyes of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sleep, but his heart does not sleep.”
ʿAmr said:
I heard ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr say:
“The dreams of the prophets are revelation.”
Then he recited:
“Indeed, I see in a dream that I am sacrificing you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (138) and Muslim (763:186). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Regarding the words of Allah:
“Then when they both submitted and he laid him down upon his forehead, We called to him: ‘O Abraham! You have fulfilled the vision.’ Indeed, this is how We reward those who do good. Indeed, this was truly the manifest trial. And We ransomed him with a great sacrifice”:

13167
Abū al-Ṭufayl narrated:
I said to Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Your people claim that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed saʿy between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah and that this is Sunnah?”
He replied:
“They have spoken the truth.”
When Abraham was instructed concerning the rites of pilgrimage, Satan appeared to him at the place of saʿy. Abraham raced him and overtook him.
Gabriel then took him to Jamrat al-ʿAqabah, where Satan appeared to him again—Yūnus, one of the narrators, said: “Satan”—so Abraham threw seven pebbles at him until he departed.
Satan then appeared to him at the middle Jamrah, and Abraham threw seven pebbles at him.
Then Abraham laid the boy down upon his forehead—Yūnus said: “It was there that he laid him upon his forehead.”
Ishmael was wearing a white shirt and said:
“My father, I have no garment besides this in which you can shroud me. Remove it so that you may shroud me in it.”
Abraham began trying to remove it.
Then a voice called from behind him:
“O Abraham! You have fulfilled the vision.”
Abraham turned and saw a white, horned ram with large eyes.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“I remember us selling rams of that very type.”
Gabriel then took Abraham to the furthest Jamrah. Satan appeared to him there, and Abraham threw seven pebbles at him until he departed.
Then Gabriel took him to Minā and said:
“This is Minā.”
The hadith continues at length.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2707), Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (2820), and through his route by al-Bayhaqī (5/153–154), and by Abū Dāwūd (1885) in an abridged form.

13168
Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah narrated:
A woman from Banū Sulaym, who had delivered many of the people of our household, informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah.
In another wording, she asked ʿUthmān ibn Ṭalḥah:
“Why did the Prophet ﷺ summon you?”
He replied that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When I entered the House, I saw the two horns of the ram and forgot to instruct you to cover them. So cover them, for there should not be anything in the House that distracts a person who is praying.”
Sufyān said:
“The two horns of the ram remained in the House until the House caught fire, and they were burned.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2030) and Aḥmad (16637), whose wording is given here.

13169
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
While a Jewish man was displaying his merchandise, he was offered a price he disliked. He said:
“No, by the One who chose Moses above mankind!”
A man from the Anṣār heard him, stood up, and slapped him across the face, saying:
“You say, ‘By the One who chose Moses above mankind,’ while the Prophet ﷺ is among us?”
The Jewish man went to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Abū al-Qāsim, I have a covenant and protection. Why did so-and-so slap my face?”
The Prophet ﷺ asked the Anṣārī:
“Why did you slap his face?”
The man explained what had happened.
The Prophet ﷺ became angry until the anger could be seen upon his face. Then he said:
“Do not make distinctions among the prophets of Allah. The Trumpet will be blown, and everyone in the heavens and earth will fall unconscious except whomever Allah wills. Then it will be blown again, and I will be the first to be raised, but I will find Moses holding onto the Throne. I do not know whether his unconsciousness at Mount Ṭūr was counted for him, or whether he was raised before me. And I do not say that anyone is better than Jonah son of Mattā.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3414) and Muslim (2373). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13170
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“None of you should say: ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā.’”
In another narration:
“It is not appropriate for anyone to say that he is better than Jonah son of Mattā.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3412).

13171
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever says, ‘I am better than Jonah son of Mattā,’ has spoken falsely.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4805).
We have been prohibited from such statements so that they do not involve belittling Jonah, peace be upon him. This does not contradict the fact that the Prophet ﷺ is the leader of the prophets and messengers, for Allah Himself has preferred some messengers over others, as He says:
“Those messengers—We favored some of them over others.”
 [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:253]
Regarding the words:
“And We caused a plant of yaqṭīn to grow over him,”
yaqṭīn refers to a plant without a firm trunk, such as cucumber, melon, or gourd, and it is characterized by abundant leaves.
The words:
“We caused it to grow over him”
mean that Allah caused it to grow beside him so that he could take shade beneath its leaves and eat from its fruit. This was from the perfect arrangement of the All-Knowing, All-Wise.
Regarding the words:
“And We sent him to one hundred thousand or more,”
the author says that one hundred thousand of them were Jews among the captives, while the remainder were Assyrians. It has also been said that the additional number was twenty thousand.
When a prophet is sent to a mixed population, his call to worship Allah alone and abandon polytheism and disbelief applies to all of them, because a prophet cannot approve people worshipping anything besides Allah.
Regarding the words:
“Then they believed, so We allowed them enjoyment for a time,”
when they saw the signs of the approaching punishment, they believed. Ordinarily, according to the divine pattern, belief after the arrival of punishment no longer benefits a person, but the people of Jonah were an exception.
Allah says:
“If only there had been a town whose people believed and whose faith benefited them—except the people of Jonah. When they believed, We removed from them the punishment of disgrace in the worldly life and allowed them enjoyment for a time.”
 [Sūrat Yūnus 10:98]
The reason, according to the commentators, was that Jonah left them in anger before fully establishing the proof against them and before Allah permitted him to depart.
When they saw the punishment approaching, they turned to Allah with supplication and seeking forgiveness. Allah accepted their prayer and removed the punishment from them.

13172
Abū Dharr narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Which words are best?”
He replied:
“The words Allah chose for His angels—or for His servants—are: ‘Glory and praise be to Allah.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2731).

13173
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out for Khaybar, he reached it during the night. Whenever he came upon a people at night, he would not attack them until morning.
In the morning, the Jews came out carrying their spades and baskets.
When they saw him, they exclaimed:
“Muḥammad! By Allah, it is Muḥammad and his army!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah is the Greatest! Khaybar is ruined. Indeed, when we descend into the territory of a people, terrible is the morning of those who were warned.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Jihād (48) and al-Bukhārī (4197).

13174
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
The words:
“Ṣād. By the Qur’an full of reminder”
were revealed concerning them and concerning that particular gathering—meaning the gathering of Abū Ṭālib and Abū Jahl, when Quraysh assembled before them and disputed with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/432).
The details of the incident are as follows:
It is narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās that Abū Ṭālib became ill. Quraysh came to visit him, and the Prophet ﷺ also came.
There was room for one person beside Abū Ṭālib, so Abū Jahl stood in order to prevent the Prophet ﷺ from taking that place.
They complained about the Prophet ﷺ to Abū Ṭālib, so he asked:
“My nephew, what do you want from your people?”
He replied:
“I only want from them one statement through which the Arabs will submit to them and the non-Arabs will pay them tribute.”
Abū Ṭālib asked:
“Only one statement?”
He replied:
“One statement.”
Then he said:
“Uncle, let them say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’”
They exclaimed:
“One God?”
And said:
“We have not heard of this in the former religion. This is nothing but an invention.”
Then the Qur’an was revealed concerning them:
“Ṣād. By the Qur’an full of reminder. Rather, those who disbelieve are in pride and opposition…”
until the words:
“We have not heard of this in the former religion. This is nothing but an invention.”
Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3232), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11372), Aḥmad (2008), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6686).
Its chain contains Yaḥyā ibn ʿUmārah, who is also called Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbbād and ʿAbbād ibn Jaʿfar al-Kūfī. No one narrated from him except al-Aʿmash, and no one declared him reliable except Ibn Ḥibbān.
For this reason, al-Ḥāfiẓ described him as:
“Acceptable,”
meaning when supported by another narration.
It has also been narrated in a mursal form. Therefore, its chain is not free of criticism. Nevertheless, the story became widely known in the books of history and Sīrah, and successive scholars transmitted it, so the author considers its widespread transmission sufficient in this context.

13175
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“The prostration in Sūrat Ṣād is not among the obligatory or strongly prescribed prostrations, but I saw the Prophet ﷺ prostrate when reciting it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1069).

13176
Al-ʿAwwām narrated:
I asked Mujāhid about the prostration in Sūrat Ṣād.
He replied:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās:
“On what basis did you prostrate there?”
He answered:
“Do you not recite:
‘And among his descendants were David and Solomon’
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:84]
and:
‘Those are the ones whom Allah guided, so follow their guidance’
 [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:90]?”
David was therefore among those whom your Prophet ﷺ was commanded to follow. David, peace be upon him, prostrated there, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ also prostrated there.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4807).

13177
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ prostrated during Sūrat Ṣād and said:
“David, peace be upon him, performed this prostration as repentance, while we perform it as gratitude.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11374).

13178
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited Sūrat Ṣād while standing upon the pulpit.
When he reached the verse of prostration, he came down, prostrated, and the people prostrated with him.
On another occasion, he recited it again. When he reached the verse of prostration, the people prepared themselves eagerly to prostrate.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“It is only the repentance-prostration of a prophet, but I see that you have prepared yourselves for prostration.”
So he came down, prostrated, and they prostrated with him.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1410), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1455), Ibn Ḥibbān (2765), and al-Ḥākim (2/431–433).

13179
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
When Solomon son of David, peace be upon them both, built Bayt al-Maqdis, he asked Allah, Mighty and Majestic, for three things:
He asked Allah for judgment that would correspond with His judgment, and he was granted it.
He asked Allah for a kingdom that would not be fitting for anyone after him, and he was granted it.
And when he finished building the mosque, he asked Allah that anyone who came to it with no motive other than praying there would emerge from his sins like the day his mother gave birth to him.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (693).

13180
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A powerful jinn attacked me last night, trying to interrupt my prayer. Allah gave me power over him, so I seized him. I nearly tied him to one of the pillars of the mosque so that all of you—or all of you together—could see him in the morning. Then I remembered the supplication of my brother Solomon: ‘My Lord, forgive me and grant me a kingdom that will not belong to anyone after me.’ So Allah drove him away in humiliation.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4808) and Muslim (541). The wording is that of Muslim.

13181
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood in prayer, and we heard him say:
“I seek refuge in Allah from you.”
Then he said:
“I curse you with the curse of Allah.”
He repeated this three times and stretched out his hand as though trying to seize something.
When he finished the prayer, we said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we heard you say something during the prayer that we had never heard you say before, and we saw you stretch out your hand.”
He replied:
“The enemy of Allah, Iblīs, came with a flame of fire intending to throw it in my face. I said: ‘I seek refuge in Allah from you,’ three times. Then I said: ‘I curse you with the complete curse of Allah,’ three times, but he did not retreat. Then I intended to seize him. By Allah, were it not for the supplication of our brother Solomon, he would have been tied up this morning for the children of Madinah to play with.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (542).

13182
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was praying when Satan came to him. The Prophet ﷺ seized him, threw him down, and strangled him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I even felt the coolness of his tongue upon my hand. Were it not for the supplication of my brother Solomon, peace be upon him, he would have remained tied up until the people could see him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11375), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2350).

13183
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever testifies that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner; that Muḥammad is His servant and Messenger; that Jesus is the servant of Allah, the son of His female servant, His Word which He cast to Mary, and a spirit from Him; and that Paradise is true and the Fire is true—Allah will admit him through whichever of the eight gates of Paradise he wishes.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (28).

13184
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
We used to take turns tending the camels. When my turn came, I brought them back in the evening and reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was standing and speaking to the people.
I heard him say:
“No Muslim performs ablution and perfects it, then stands and prays two rakʿahs, fully turning his heart and face toward them, except that Paradise becomes obligatory for him.”
I said:
“How excellent this is!”
Then someone in front of me said:
“What came before it was even better.”
I looked and saw that it was ʿUmar.
He said:
“I saw that you had just arrived.”
Then he related that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There is no one among you who performs ablution thoroughly and then says: ‘I testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and that Muḥammad is the servant and Messenger of Allah,’ except that the eight gates of Paradise are opened for him, and he may enter through whichever of them he wishes.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (234).

13185
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The angels were created from light, the jinn were created from a smokeless flame of fire, and Adam was created from what has been described to you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2996).

13186
Masrūq narrated:
We entered upon ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd, and he said:
“People, whoever knows something should speak according to what he knows. Whoever does not know should say: ‘Allah knows best.’ Indeed, part of knowledge is to say concerning what one does not know: ‘Allah knows best.’”
Then he said:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said to His Prophet ﷺ:
“Say: I ask you no payment for this, nor am I one of those who pretend or impose upon themselves.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4809) and Muslim (2798:40).
Regarding the words:
“Say: I ask you no payment for this,”
the meaning is that the Prophet’s call to the people was made without demanding any compensation. The call of the prophets, peace and blessings be upon them, was sincerely for Allah alone, without seeking money, status, or worldly position in return.
And regarding the words:
“It is only a reminder for all the worlds,”
the meaning is that this Qur’an to which the Prophet ﷺ calls is a reminder for you and for all peoples and beings of the worlds.

13187
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ would not go to sleep until he had recited Sūrat al-Zumar and Banī Isrāʾīl [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ].
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2920), Aḥmad (24388), al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (712), Ibn Khuzaymah (1163), and al-Ḥākim (2/424).

13188
Abū Dharr narrated from the Prophet ﷺ, among what he related from Allah, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“My servants, I have forbidden oppression for Myself and have made it forbidden among you, so do not oppress one another.
My servants, all of you are astray except those whom I guide, so seek guidance from Me and I will guide you.
My servants, all of you are hungry except those whom I feed, so ask Me for food and I will feed you.
My servants, all of you are unclothed except those whom I clothe, so ask Me for clothing and I will clothe you.
My servants, you commit sins by night and by day, while I forgive all sins, so seek My forgiveness and I will forgive you.
My servants, you will never be able to reach a level at which you could harm Me, nor will you ever reach a level at which you could benefit Me.
My servants, if the first of you and the last of you, the humans among you and the jinn among you, were all as God-fearing as the most God-fearing heart of any one man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first of you and the last of you, the humans among you and the jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of any one man among you, that would not diminish My dominion in the slightest.
My servants, if the first of you and the last of you, the humans among you and the jinn among you, were all to stand together on one plain and ask Me, and I were to give every person what he asked for, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when dipped into it.
My servants, these are only your deeds. I record them for you and then recompense you for them in full. Whoever finds good should praise Allah, and whoever finds other than that should blame no one but himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2577).

13189
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever stands in night prayer reciting ten verses will not be recorded among the heedless. Whoever stands reciting one hundred verses will be recorded among the devoutly obedient. And whoever stands reciting one thousand verses will be recorded among those who receive immense reward.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1398), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1144) and Ibn Ḥibbān (2572).
Regarding the words:
“Fearing the Hereafter and hoping for the mercy of his Lord,”
he hopes for Allah’s mercy and forgiveness while at the same time fearing Allah’s punishment because of his sins. Thus, he combines fear and hope.
This is mentioned in the hadith:

13190
Anas narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ entered upon a young man who was dying and asked him:
“How do you find yourself?”
He replied:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, I hope in Allah, yet I also fear my sins.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“These two qualities do not come together in the heart of a servant at such a moment except that Allah grants him what he hopes for and gives him security from what he fears.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (983) and Ibn Mājah (4261).

13191
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The people of Paradise will see one another’s lofty dwellings just as you see a star in the sky.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6555) and Muslim (2830).

13192
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The people of Paradise will look upon the lofty chambers in Paradise just as you look upon a setting star on the eastern or western horizon.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6556) and Muslim (2831).

13193
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
We asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell us about Paradise. What is its construction like?”
He replied:
“One brick is of gold and one brick is of silver. Its mortar is fragrant musk. Its pebbles are pearls and rubies, and its soil is saffron. Whoever enters it will live in bliss and never suffer; he will remain forever and never die; his clothes will never wear out and his youth will never fade.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8043), ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1420), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7387).

13194
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died while Abū Bakr was at al-Sunḥ. Ismāʿīl explained that this was in the upper part of Madinah.
ʿUmar stood and said:
“By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has not died!”
ʿĀʾishah said that ʿUmar later explained:
“By Allah, that was the only thought occurring in my mind. Allah will surely raise him again, and he will cut off the hands and feet of certain men.”
Abū Bakr came, uncovered the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, kissed him, and said:
“May my father and mother be sacrificed for you. You were pure in life and in death. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, Allah will never make you taste death twice.”
Then he went out and said to ʿUmar:
“You who are swearing, be calm.”
When Abū Bakr began speaking, ʿUmar sat down.
Abū Bakr praised and glorified Allah, then said:
“Whoever used to worship Muḥammad ﷺ, then Muḥammad has indeed died. But whoever worships Allah, then Allah is Living and never dies.”
Then he recited:
“Indeed, you will die, and indeed, they too will die.”
And he recited:
“Muḥammad is only a messenger. Messengers have passed away before him. If he dies or is killed, will you turn back on your heels? Whoever turns back on his heels will never harm Allah in the least, and Allah will reward those who are grateful.”
 [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:144]
Then the people began sobbing and weeping.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3667–3668).
Regarding the words:
“Then on the Day of Resurrection, before your Lord, you will dispute with one another”:

13195
Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām narrated:
When the following verses were revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Indeed, you will die, and indeed, they too will die. Then on the Day of Resurrection, before your Lord, you will dispute with one another,”
al-Zubayr asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will the disputes that occurred between us in this world be brought back before us, along with our personal sins?”
He replied:
“Yes. They will certainly be brought back before you until every person possessing a right is given his right.”
Al-Zubayr said:
“By Allah, the matter is indeed severe.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (1434), al-Bazzār (964), Abū Yaʿlā (668), and al-Ḥākim (2/435).

13196
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The first two disputants to present their case on the Day of Resurrection will be two neighbors.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17372) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/309).

13197
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
One day I was riding behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“Young man, I will teach you some words: Guard the commands of Allah and He will guard you. Guard the commands of Allah and you will find Him before you. When you ask, ask Allah. When you seek help, seek help from Allah.
Know that if the entire nation gathered together to benefit you with something, they could not benefit you except with something Allah had already written for you. And if they gathered together to harm you with something, they could not harm you except with something Allah had already written against you.
The pens have been lifted and the pages have dried.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2516), whose wording is given here, and Aḥmad (2669).

13198
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When one of you goes to his bed, let him take the inside edge of his lower garment and dust off his bed with it, mentioning the name of Allah, because he does not know what may have come onto his bed after he left it.
When he wishes to lie down, let him lie upon his right side and say: ‘Glory be to You, O Allah, my Lord. By You I lay down my side, and by You I raise it. If You retain my soul, then forgive it; and if You release it, then protect it with that by which You protect Your righteous servants.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6320) and Muslim (2714). The wording is that of Muslim.

13199
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Some people from among the polytheists had committed many murders and had frequently committed fornication.
They came to Muḥammad ﷺ and said:
“What you say and call us to is certainly good. If only you could tell us whether there is some expiation for what we have done.”
Then the following verse was revealed:
“And those who do not call upon another deity besides Allah, nor kill the soul which Allah has forbidden except with legal right, nor commit fornication…” [Sūrat al-Furqān 25:68]

And the following verse was also revealed:
“Say: O My servants who have transgressed against themselves, do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. He alone is the Ever-Forgiving, the Most Merciful.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4810) and Muslim (122).

13200
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated:
When we intended to migrate to Madinah, ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Rabīʿah, Hishām ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī, and I agreed to meet at al-Tanāḍub, near the water source of Banū Ghifār above Sarif.
We agreed:
“Whoever among us does not arrive there by morning has been prevented, so the other two should continue without him.”
By morning, ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Rabīʿah and I were at al-Tanāḍub, but Hishām had been prevented from joining us.
When we reached Madinah, we stayed among Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf at Qubāʾ.
Abū Jahl ibn Hishām and al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām then went after ʿAyyāsh ibn Abī Rabīʿah. They were his cousins and also his brothers through his mother. They came all the way to Madinah while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was still in Makkah.
They spoke to ʿAyyāsh and said:
“Your mother has vowed that no comb will touch her hair until she sees you, and that she will not seek shade from the sun until she sees you.”
He felt compassion for her.
I said to him:
“ʿAyyāsh, by Allah, these people only want to tempt you away from your religion, so beware of them. By Allah, if lice begin to trouble your mother, she will comb her hair; and if the heat of Makkah becomes intense enough, she will seek shade.”
He replied:
“I will fulfill my mother’s oath, and I also have some wealth there that I want to retrieve.”
I said:
“By Allah, you know that I am among the wealthiest men of Quraysh. I will give you half of my wealth. Do not go with them.”
But he refused to do anything except leave with them.
When he insisted, I said:
“Since you are determined to do this, take this she-camel of mine. She is a fine, docile riding camel. Stay upon her back, and if anything about these men makes you suspicious, escape upon her.”
So he departed with them riding the camel.
When they had traveled part of the way, Abū Jahl said to him:
“My nephew, by Allah, this camel of mine has become difficult for me to ride. Will you let me ride behind you upon your camel?”
ʿAyyāsh replied:
“Yes.”
They all made their camels kneel so that Abū Jahl could switch mounts. Once they were all standing on the ground, the two men attacked ʿAyyāsh, bound him tightly, took him back to Makkah, and subjected him to persecution until he succumbed to the trial.
ʿUmar said:
We used to say:
“Allah will never accept from anyone who has succumbed to persecution any ransom, compensation, or repentance. They were people who knew Allah and then returned to disbelief because of a hardship that afflicted them.”
Those who had been persecuted also used to say the same thing about themselves.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, Allah revealed concerning them, concerning what we had been saying, and concerning what they had been saying about themselves:
“Say: O My servants who have transgressed against themselves, do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. Surely He is the Ever-Forgiving, the Most Merciful.
And turn back to your Lord in repentance and submit to Him before the punishment comes upon you, after which you will not be helped.
And follow the best of what has been revealed to you from your Lord before the punishment comes upon you suddenly while you are unaware.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
I wrote these verses with my own hand upon a sheet and sent them to Hishām ibn al-ʿĀṣ.
Hishām ibn al-ʿĀṣ said:
When they reached me, I began reading them at Dhū Ṭuwā. I would go up and down there reciting them, yet I could not understand their meaning.
Eventually I said:
“O Allah, make me understand them.”
Then Allah placed in my heart the understanding that these verses had been revealed specifically concerning us, concerning what we used to say about ourselves, and what others used to say about us.
So I returned to my camel, mounted it, and went until I joined the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in Madinah.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (155), al-Ḥākim (2/435), and al-Ḍiyāʾ in al-Mukhtārah (1/317–319).

13201
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among those who lived before you was a man who had killed ninety-nine people. He asked about the most knowledgeable person on earth and was directed to a monk.
He went to him and said that he had killed ninety-nine people and asked whether repentance was possible for him.
The monk replied: ‘No.’ So he killed him too, completing the number at one hundred.
Then he again asked about the most knowledgeable person on earth and was directed to a learned man.
He told him that he had killed one hundred people and asked whether there was any repentance for him.
The scholar replied: ‘Yes. Who could stand between you and repentance? Go to such-and-such a land, for there are people there who worship Allah. Worship Allah with them, and do not return to your own land, for it is an evil land.’
So he set out. When he had reached halfway, death overtook him.
The angels of mercy and the angels of punishment disputed over him.
The angels of mercy said: ‘He came repentant, turning toward Allah with his heart.’
The angels of punishment said: ‘He never performed a good deed.’
Then an angel came to them in the form of a human being, and they appointed him as an arbiter between them.
He said: ‘Measure the distance between the two lands. Whichever land he is closer to will determine his case.’
They measured and found him closer to the land toward which he had intended to travel, so the angels of mercy took his soul.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3470) and Muslim (2766:46).

13202
Abū Ṣirmah narrated that Abū Ayyūb said when death approached him:
“I had concealed from you something that I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Were it not that you commit sins, Allah would create another creation who would commit sins and He would forgive them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2748).

13203
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if you did not commit sins, Allah would remove you and bring another people who would sin, then seek Allah’s forgiveness, and He would forgive them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2749).

13204
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every inhabitant of the Fire will be shown the place he could have occupied in Paradise, and he will say: ‘If only Allah had guided me.’ That will become a source of intense regret for him.”
He also said:
“And every inhabitant of Paradise will be shown the place he would have occupied in the Fire, and he will say: ‘Had Allah not guided me…’ That will become a cause of gratitude for him.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (10652), whose wording is given here, al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11390), and al-Ḥākim (2/435–436).

13205
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The arrogant will be gathered on the Day of Resurrection like tiny ants in human form. Humiliation will overwhelm them from every direction until they are driven into a prison in Hell called Būlas. The fiercest fire will rise over them, and they will be given to drink from Ṭīnat al-Khabāl—the discharge of the people of the Fire.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2492), Aḥmad (6677), and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (557).

13206
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
A Jewish scholar came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Muḥammad,” or, “O Abū al-Qāsim, Allah, the Exalted, will hold the heavens on one finger on the Day of Resurrection, the earths on one finger, the mountains and trees on one finger, the water and soil on one finger, and the rest of creation on one finger. Then He will shake them and say: ‘I am the King! I am the King!’”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed in amazement at what the scholar said, affirming it, and then recited:
“They did not estimate Allah with the estimation due to Him. The whole earth will be in His grasp on the Day of Resurrection, and the heavens will be folded in His right hand. Glory be to Him, and Exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4811) and Muslim (2786). The wording is that of Muslim.

13207
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah will grasp the earth on the Day of Resurrection and fold the heaven in His right hand. Then He will say: ‘I am the King. Where are the kings of the earth?’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7382) and Muslim (2787). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13208
ʿUbaydullāh ibn Miqsam narrated that he watched ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar demonstrating how the Messenger of Allah ﷺ described it.
He said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will take His heavens and His earths in His two hands and say: ‘I am Allah.’”
Ibn ʿUmar would close and open his fingers while narrating:
“I am the King.”
The pulpit moved so strongly beneath the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that I wondered whether it might fall with him upon it.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2788:25).

13209
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Between the two blasts of the Trumpet there will be forty.”
They asked Abū Hurayrah:
“Forty days?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
They asked:
“Forty months?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
They asked:
“Forty years?”
He replied:
“I refuse to specify.”
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“Then Allah will send down water from the sky, and they will grow just as vegetation grows.”
He also said:
“Every part of the human being will decay except one bone: the tailbone. From it the creation will be reassembled on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4814) and Muslim (2955). The wording is that of Muslim.

13210
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
We used to take turns tending the camels. When my turn came, I brought them back in the evening and reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was standing and speaking to the people.
I heard him say:
“No Muslim performs ablution and perfects it, then stands and prays two rakʿahs, fully turning his heart and face toward them, except that Paradise becomes obligatory for him.”
I said:
“How excellent this is!”
Then someone in front of me said:
“What came before it was even better.”
I looked and saw that it was ʿUmar.
He said:
“I saw that you had just arrived.”
Then he related that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There is no one among you who performs ablution thoroughly, then says: ‘I testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and that Muḥammad is the servant of Allah and His Messenger,’ except that the eight gates of Paradise are opened for him, and he may enter through whichever of them he wishes.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (234).

13211
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever spends a pair of anything in the path of Allah will be called from the gates of Paradise: ‘Servant of Allah, this is good!’
Whoever was among the people of prayer will be called from the Gate of Prayer. Whoever was among the people of jihād will be called from the Gate of Jihād. Whoever was among the people of charity will be called from the Gate of Charity. And whoever was among the people of fasting will be called from the Gate of Fasting, the Gate of al-Rayyān.”
Abū Bakr said:
“There is no hardship for someone who is called from one of these gates, but will anyone be called from all of them, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes, and I hope that you will be among them, O Abū Bakr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3666) and Muslim (1027:85).

13212
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“In Paradise there is a gate called al-Rayyān. Those who fasted will enter through it on the Day of Resurrection, and no one other than them will enter through it.
It will be called out: ‘Where are those who used to fast?’ They will enter through it. Once the last of them has entered, it will be closed, and no one else will enter through it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1896) and Muslim (1152).

13213
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No Muslim servant supplicates for his brother in his absence except that the angel says: ‘And for you the same.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2732).

13214
Abū al-Zubayr narrated:
Ibn al-Zubayr used to say after every prayer, once he had given salām:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things. There is no might and no power except through Allah. There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, and we worship none but Him. To Him belongs all blessing, all favor, and all beautiful praise. There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah, making the religion sincerely His, even though the disbelievers dislike it.”
He said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to proclaim these words of tawḥīd after every prayer.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (594).

13215
ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays—Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī—narrated that when the Prophet ﷺ feared a people, he would say:
“O Allah, we place You before them, and we seek refuge in You from their evil.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1537), Aḥmad (19720), al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (601), Ibn Ḥibbān (4765), and al-Ḥākim (2/142).

13216
ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated:
I asked ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ:
“Tell me about the severest thing the polytheists ever did to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He replied:
While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was praying in the courtyard of the Kaʿbah, ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ approached him, seized him by the shoulder, twisted his garment around his neck, and strangled him severely with it.
Abū Bakr came forward, seized ʿUqbah by the shoulder, pushed him away from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and said:
“Would you kill a man merely because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah,’ while he has brought you clear proofs from your Lord?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4815).

13217
ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
I never saw Quraysh truly intend to kill the Messenger of Allah ﷺ except on one occasion.
I saw them sitting in the shade of the Kaʿbah while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was praying near the Station of Abraham.
ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ went up to him, placed his cloak around his neck, and pulled him so violently that the Prophet ﷺ fell to his knees.
The people began shouting, thinking that he had been killed.
Abū Bakr came running and seized the Messenger of Allah ﷺ by both upper arms from behind while saying:
“Would you kill a man merely because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah’?”
Then they withdrew from the Prophet ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood and completed his prayer. When he had finished, he passed by them while they were still sitting in the shade of the Kaʿbah and said:
“People of Quraysh, by the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, I have not been sent to you except with slaughter.”
He gestured toward his throat with his hand.
Abū Jahl said:
“Muḥammad, you were never an ignorant man.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“You are among them.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah in al-Muṣannaf (37716), Abū Yaʿlā (7339), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6569).

13218
Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was once beaten until he lost consciousness.
Abū Bakr stood up and began calling out:
“Woe to you! Would you kill a man merely because he says, ‘My Lord is Allah’?”
They asked:
“Who is this?”
They replied:
“It is the mad son of Abū Quḥāfah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār (7506), Abū Yaʿlā (3691), and al-Ḥākim (3/67).

13219
Al-Ḥasan narrated:
ʿUbaydullāh ibn Ziyād visited Maʿqil ibn Yasār during the illness from which Maʿqil eventually died.
Maʿqil said to him:
“I will relate to you a hadith that I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
He said:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“There is no servant whom Allah places in charge of subjects, and who fails to surround them with sincere care and advice, except that he will not smell the fragrance of Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7150) and Muslim (142).

13220
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When one of you dies, his place is shown to him morning and evening. If he is among the people of Paradise, he is shown his place among the people of Paradise; and if he is among the people of the Fire, he is shown his place among the people of the Fire.
It is said to him: ‘This is your place until Allah raises you on the Day of Resurrection.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (48), al-Bukhārī (1379), and Muslim (2866).

13221
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
Allah says: “Whoever shows hostility to a devoted servant of Mine, I declare war against him.
My servant does not draw near to Me with anything more beloved to Me than what I have made obligatory upon him.
My servant continues drawing near to Me through voluntary deeds until I love him.
When I love him, I become his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he grasps, and his foot with which he walks.
If he asks Me, I will surely give him; and if he seeks refuge in Me, I will surely protect him.
I do not hesitate concerning anything I am to do as I hesitate concerning taking the soul of the believer: he dislikes death, and I dislike causing him distress.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6502).

13222
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Supplication is worship.”
Then he recited:
“Your Lord has said: ‘Call upon Me and I will answer you. Indeed, those who are too arrogant to worship Me will enter Hell humiliated.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1479), al-Tirmidhī (3372), Ibn Mājah (3828), and Aḥmad (18352).
Allah becomes angry with the one who does not ask Him, as stated in the hadith:

13223
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever does not ask Allah, Allah becomes angry with him.”
In another wording:
“Whoever does not supplicate to Allah…”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3373), whose wording is given first; Ibn Mājah (3827) and Aḥmad (9719), who narrate the alternate wording; and al-Ḥākim (1/491).
Al-Ḥākim adds:
“Indeed, Allah becomes angry with the one who does not do it [that is, supplicate], and no one besides Him does that.”
That is, regarding supplication.
He then said: “Its isnād is authentic, for Abū Ṣāliḥ al-Khūzī and Abū al-Malīḥ al-Fārisī were not mentioned with criticism; rather, they are counted among the unknown narrators because of the scarcity of their hadith.”
The author says: This is not as he stated. Abū Ṣāliḥ al-Khūzī was differed over, though his hadith is at the level of Good.
The discussion of him is given in detail in the Book of Supplications.

13224
Jābir narrated:
When those who had migrated across the sea returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he asked:
“Will you not tell me about some of the remarkable things you saw in the land of Abyssinia?”
Some young men among them replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
They said:
While we were sitting, an elderly woman from among the women associated with their monks passed by us carrying a water jar upon her head.
She passed by a young man from among them, and he placed one of his hands between her shoulders and pushed her. She fell onto her knees, and her water jar broke.
When she got back up, she turned toward him and said:
“You treacherous man, you will come to know when Allah sets up the Throne of Judgment, gathers the first and the last, and the hands and feet speak concerning what people used to earn. Then you will know tomorrow how my affair and yours will be before Him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“She spoke the truth. She spoke the truth. How can Allah sanctify a nation in which the right of the weak is not taken from the strong?”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4010), Ibn Ḥibbān (5058), and Ibn Abī al-Dunyā in al-Ahwāl (242).

13225
Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
Three men gathered near the Sacred House: two men from Quraysh and one from Thaqīf, or two from Thaqīf and one from Quraysh.
Their hearts possessed little understanding, while their bellies carried much fat.
One of them said:
“Do you think Allah hears what we say?”
Another said:
“He hears us when we speak aloud, but He does not hear us when we speak quietly.”
The third said:
“If He hears us when we speak aloud, then He also hears us when we speak quietly.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“You did not conceal yourselves out of fear that your hearing, your sight, and your skins would testify against you…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4817) and Muslim (2775).

13226
Sufyān ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Thaqafī narrated:
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me something about Islam concerning which I will not need to ask anyone after you.”
He replied:
“Say: ‘I believe in Allah,’ and then remain steadfast.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (38).
Regarding the words:
“The angels descend upon them, saying: ‘Do not fear, nor grieve, but rejoice in the Paradise which you were promised,’”
the angels descend upon them at the time of death and give them glad tidings of Allah’s pleasure and of their salvation from His punishment.

13227
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever loves to meet Allah, Allah loves to meet him; and whoever dislikes meeting Allah, Allah dislikes meeting him.”
ʿĀʾishah, or one of the Prophet’s wives, said:
“But we all dislike death.”
He replied:
“That is not what is meant. Rather, when death approaches the believer, he is given glad tidings of Allah’s pleasure and honor. Nothing then becomes more beloved to him than what lies ahead of him, so he loves to meet Allah, and Allah loves to meet him.
But when death approaches the disbeliever, he is given news of Allah’s punishment and chastisement. Nothing then becomes more hateful to him than what lies ahead of him, so he hates to meet Allah, and Allah hates to meet him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6507) and Muslim (2683).

13228
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
One day the Prophet ﷺ was sitting openly among the people when Gabriel came to him and asked:
“What is faith?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Faith is that you believe in Allah, His angels, His Book, meeting Him, His messengers, and that you believe in resurrection.”
Gabriel asked:
“What is Islam?”
He replied:
“Islam is that you worship Allah without associating anything with Him, establish the prayer, give the obligatory zakāh, and fast Ramadan.”
Gabriel asked:
“What is excellence in worship?”
He replied:
“That you worship Allah as though you see Him; and if you do not see Him, then indeed He sees you.”
Gabriel asked:
“When is the Hour?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“The one being asked knows no more about it than the one asking. But I will tell you some of its signs: when the slave woman gives birth to her master, and when the shepherds of black camels compete with one another in constructing tall buildings. It is among five matters that no one knows except Allah.”
Then the Prophet ﷺ recited:
“Indeed, with Allah alone is knowledge of the Hour…” [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
Gabriel then departed.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bring him back.”
But they could no longer see anyone.
So the Prophet ﷺ said:
“That was Gabriel. He came to teach the people their religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (50) and Muslim (9).

13229
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated that al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“How does revelation come to you?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Sometimes it comes to me like the ringing of a bell, and this is the hardest form for me. Then it ceases from me after I have fully grasped what was said. At other times, the angel appears to me in the form of a man and speaks to me, and I understand what he says.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“I saw revelation descend upon him on an intensely cold day, and when it ceased from him, his forehead was dripping with sweat.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Qurʾān (7), al-Bukhārī (2), and Muslim (2333).
Other hadiths concerning this are mentioned in the Book of Revelation.

13230
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Ḥamrāʾ al-Zuhrī narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ while he was standing at al-Ḥazwarah in the marketplace of Makkah say:
“By Allah, you are the best land of Allah and the most beloved land of Allah to Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Had I not been driven out from you, I would never have left.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3925), Ibn Mājah (3108), Aḥmad (18715), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3708).
Regarding the words:
“And those around it,”
this refers immediately to the Arab settlements surrounding Makkah. However, this warning extends to all lands on earth, because the Prophet ﷺ was sent by Allah to all mankind.
Allah says:
“We have not sent you except to all mankind as a bearer of glad tidings and a warner.” [Sūrat Sabaʾ 34:28]

13231
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been given five things that no one before me was given: every prophet used to be sent specifically to his own people, whereas I have been sent to every red and black person. War spoils have been made lawful for me, though they were not made lawful for anyone before me. The earth has been made for me pure, a means of purification, and a place of prayer, so whenever the time for prayer reaches a man, he may pray wherever he is. I have been supported with fear cast into my enemies from the distance of a month’s journey, and I have been granted intercession.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (335) and Muslim (521). The wording is that of Muslim.
There are many other hadiths with the same meaning, and they are mentioned in the Book of Jihād and the Book of Sīrah.

13232
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A Bedouin asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“When will the Hour be?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked him:
“What have you prepared for it?”
He replied:
“Love of Allah and His Messenger.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“You will be with those whom you love.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2639) and al-Bukhārī (3688).

13233
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Give this nation glad tidings of splendor, elevation, victory, and establishment in the land. But whoever among them performs a deed of the Hereafter for the sake of this world will have no share in the Hereafter.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21220), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (405) and al-Ḥākim (4/311), and narrated by al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (1154).

13234
Ibn ʿAbbās was asked about the words of Allah:
“Except affection concerning kinship.”
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said:
“It means kinship with the family of Muḥammad ﷺ.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“You have been too hasty. There was no clan of Quraysh except that the Prophet ﷺ had some family relationship with them.”
He explained the meaning as:
“Except that you maintain the ties of kinship that exist between me and you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4818).

13235
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah is more joyful at the repentance of His servant when he repents to Him than one of you who is riding his mount through a barren wilderness, then it escapes from him while carrying his food and drink.
He loses all hope of finding it, goes to a tree, and lies down in its shade, having completely despaired of recovering his mount.
Then, while he is in that state, suddenly he finds it standing beside him. He seizes its reins and, from the intensity of his joy, says: ‘O Allah, You are my servant and I am Your lord!’ He made the mistake because of the intensity of his joy.”
