Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2747).

13236
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was never given a choice between two matters except that he chose the easier of the two, so long as it was not sinful. If it involved sin, he was the person farthest from it.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never took revenge for himself, except when one of Allah’s sacred limits was violated; then he would act for the sake of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ḥusn al-Khuluq (2), al-Bukhārī (3560), and Muslim (2327:77).

13237
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was neither obscene in speech nor deliberately vulgar. He was not loud and quarrelsome in the marketplaces, and he did not repay evil with evil. Rather, he pardoned and overlooked.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (2016) and in al-Shamāʾil (347), Aḥmad (25417), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6443).

13238
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was not one who insulted others, used obscene language, or frequently cursed. When reproaching one of us, he would merely say: “What is the matter with him? May his forehead be dusty.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6031, 6046).

13239
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mounted his camel to set out on a journey, he would say Allāhu akbar three times and then say:
“Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, for we could never have subdued it ourselves, and surely to our Lord we will return.
O Allah, we ask You on this journey of ours for righteousness, God-consciousness, and deeds that please You. O Allah, make this journey easy for us and shorten its distance for us. O Allah, You are the Companion on the journey and the Guardian over the family. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardship of travel, from a distressing sight, and from an evil return concerning wealth and family.”
When he returned, he would say the same words and add:
“We return, repenting, worshipping, and praising our Lord.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1342).
The word:
“muqrinīn”
means “capable of mastering it.” That is, we would not have been able to subdue and make use of it had Allah not subjected it to us.

13240
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah does not wrong a believer regarding even a single good deed. He is given good because of it in this world and rewarded for it in the Hereafter.
As for the disbeliever, he is given his worldly provision because of the good deeds he performed for Allah’s sake, until when he reaches the Hereafter, he has no good deed left for which he may be rewarded.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2808).

13241
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that he entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the upper room where the Prophet ﷺ had withdrawn from his wives after taking an oath concerning them.
The Prophet ﷺ was lying upon a woven mat with nothing between him and it. Beneath his head was a leather pillow stuffed with palm fiber. Near his feet was a pile of acacia leaves used for tanning, and some hides were hanging near his head.
ʿUmar said:
I saw the marks of the mat upon the side of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I began to weep.
He asked:
“What is making you weep?”
I replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, Chosroes and Caesar are enjoying all that they enjoy, while you are the Messenger of Allah!”
He said:
“Are you not pleased that this world should be theirs and the Hereafter ours?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4913) and Muslim (1479:31).

13242
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUkaym narrated:
We were with Ḥudhayfah at al-Madāʾin. Ḥudhayfah asked for something to drink, and a local landowner brought him a drink in a silver vessel.
Ḥudhayfah threw it at him and said:
“I am telling you this because I had already ordered him not to give me drink in it.”
Then he said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not drink from vessels of gold and silver, and do not wear brocade or silk, for they are for them in this world and for you in the Hereafter on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2067).

13243
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Dhū al-Ḥulayfah when he came across a dead sheep with one of its legs raised.
He said:
“Do you think this is worthless to its owner? By the One in Whose hand is my soul, this world is more insignificant to Allah than this is to its owner. If this world were worth even the wing of a mosquito before Allah, He would never give a disbeliever even a sip of water from it.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4110) and al-Ḥākim (4/306).

13244
Abū Umāmah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No people went astray after having been upon guidance except that they were given to argumentation.”
Then he recited:
“Rather, they are a contentious people.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3253), Ibn Mājah (48), Aḥmad (22164), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/447–448).
Allah’s words:
“They presented it to you only for argument”
refer to this example, which they raised as a form of false disputation.
They knew that Allah’s words:
“Indeed, you and whatever you worship besides Allah are fuel for Hell; you will enter it.” [Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:98]
referred to these idols.
This is the view of the majority of the commentators.

13245
Abū Yaḥyā, the freedman of Ibn ʿAqīl al-Anṣārī, narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās, who said:
“I know a verse of the Qur’an about which no man has ever asked me. I do not know whether the people already understand it and therefore have not asked, or whether they have failed to notice it and therefore have not asked.”
He then continued speaking to us.
When he left, we reproached one another for not having asked him which verse he meant.
I said:
“I will ask him when he returns tomorrow.”
The next day when he returned, I said:
“Ibn ʿAbbās, yesterday you mentioned a verse of the Qur’an about which no one had ever asked you, and that you did not know whether people already understood it or had simply failed to notice it. Tell me about that verse and about the verses you recited before it.”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Quraysh:
“People of Quraysh, there is no goodness in anyone who is worshipped besides Allah.”
Quraysh knew that the Christians worshipped Jesus son of Mary, and they also knew what Muḥammad ﷺ said concerning him.
They said:
“Muḥammad, do you not claim that Jesus was a prophet and a righteous servant from among the servants of Allah? If what you say is true, then their gods are just as you describe.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“When the son of Mary is presented as an example, behold, your people raise a clamor over it.”
I asked:
“What does ‘raise a clamor’ mean?”
He replied:
“They make a loud uproar.”
Then concerning the words:
“Indeed, he is surely a sign of the Hour,”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“It refers to the coming forth of Jesus son of Mary, peace be upon him, before the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2918), whose wording is given here, al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (12/153), and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Mushkil al-Āthār (986).

13246
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Aim to do what is right, come as close to perfection as you can, and receive glad tidings, for no one’s deeds will admit him into Paradise.”
They asked:
“Not even you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Not even me, unless Allah envelops me in mercy from Him. And know that the deed most beloved to Allah is the one performed most consistently, even if it is small.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6467) and Muslim (2818). The wording is that of Muslim.

13247
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no one among you except that he has two dwellings: one dwelling in Paradise and one dwelling in the Fire.
If he dies and enters the Fire, the people of Paradise inherit his dwelling in Paradise. This is the meaning of Allah’s words: ‘Those are the inheritors.’” [Sūrat al-Muʾminūn 23:10]
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4341) and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (373).

13248
Ṣafwān ibn Yaʿlā narrated from his father:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ reciting from the pulpit:
“They will call out: ‘O Mālik, let your Lord put an end to us!’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4819) and Muslim (871). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Regarding the words:
“They will call: ‘O Mālik,’”
they will be calling Mālik, the keeper of Hell.
Regarding the words:
“Let your Lord put an end to us,”
they mean:
“Let your Lord decree death upon us so that we may finally be relieved of the punishment.”
Regarding the reply:
“He will say: ‘Indeed, you will remain,’”
death will not be decreed for them. Rather, they will remain in the everlasting punishment.
It is reported in some narrations that the people of the Fire will call upon Mālik, the keeper of Hell, and he will not answer them for forty years. Then he will say:
“Indeed, you will remain.”
However, this report is mawqūf upon ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ rather than being established as a statement of the Prophet ﷺ.
Al-Haythamī said in Majmaʿ al-Zawāʾid (10/396):
“It was narrated by al-Ṭabarānī, and its narrators are the narrators of the Ṣaḥīḥ.”

13249
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Seek the Night of Decree during the odd-numbered nights of the last ten nights of Ramadan.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2017), and also by al-Bukhārī (2020) and Muslim (1169).
The other hadiths concerning the Night of Decree are mentioned in the Book of Fasting.

13250
Masrūq narrated:
A man came to ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd and said:
“I left a man in the mosque interpreting the Qur’an according to his own opinion. He interprets the verse, ‘The Day when the sky will bring a visible smoke,’ by saying that on the Day of Resurrection a smoke will come upon the people and seize their breath until it affects them like a severe cold.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd replied:
“Whoever knows something should speak according to his knowledge, and whoever does not know should say: ‘Allah knows best.’ Indeed, part of a person’s understanding is that he says concerning what he does not know: ‘Allah knows best.’”
He then said:
When Quraysh stubbornly resisted the Prophet ﷺ, he supplicated against them for years of famine like the years of Joseph.
A severe drought and hardship afflicted them, to such an extent that a man would look toward the sky and, because of the intensity of his hunger and weakness, see something resembling smoke between himself and the sky.
They even reached the point of eating bones.
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, ask Allah to forgive Muḍar, for they are perishing.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“For Muḍar? You are indeed bold!”
Then he supplicated to Allah for them.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Indeed, We will remove the punishment for a little while; indeed, you will return.”
Rain was then sent upon them. But once prosperity returned to them, they returned to their former ways.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Then wait for the Day when the sky will bring a visible smoke, enveloping the people. This is a painful punishment.”
And:
“On the Day We seize with the greatest seizure, indeed, We will take vengeance.”
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“This means the Day of Badr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4821, 4822, 4823) and Muslim (2798:40). The wording is that of Muslim.
Al-Bukhārī also narrated it in the Book of Tafsīr (4824), as did Muslim, both through Manṣūr from Abū al-Ḍuḥā. In that version:
Abū Sufyān came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Muḥammad, you have come commanding obedience to Allah and the maintenance of family ties, yet your people are perishing. Supplicate to Allah for them.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“Then wait for the Day when the sky will bring a visible smoke, enveloping the people. This is a painful punishment…”
until:
“Indeed, you will return.”
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Would the punishment of the Hereafter ever be removed?”
The hadith continues.

13251
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Five things have already occurred: al-Lizām, the Romans, the Great Seizure, the Moon, and the Smoke.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4825) and Muslim (2798:41), both through al-Aʿmash from Abū al-Ḍuḥā Muslim ibn Ṣubayḥ, from Masrūq, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd. The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
The second view is that this smoke is one of the major signs of the Hour.
The following hadith supports this interpretation:

13252
Ḥudhayfah ibn Usayd al-Ghifārī narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ looked in upon us while we were discussing something and asked:
“What are you discussing?”
They replied:
“We are discussing the Hour.”
He said:
“It will not occur until you see ten signs before it.”
He then mentioned:
the Smoke, the Dajjāl, the Beast, the rising of the sun from the west, the descent of Jesus son of Mary, Gog and Magog, three great sinkings of the earth—one in the east, one in the west, and one in the Arabian Peninsula—and the last of these will be a fire emerging from Yemen that will drive the people toward their place of gathering.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2901).
There is no contradiction between the hadith of ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd and the hadith of Ḥudhayfah ibn Usayd.
The smoke mentioned in Ibn Masʿūd’s hadith is different from the smoke mentioned in the hadith of Ḥudhayfah.

13253
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When the people of Paradise have entered Paradise and the people of the Fire have entered the Fire, death will be brought and placed between Paradise and the Fire. Then it will be slaughtered.
A caller will then announce: ‘People of Paradise, there is no more death! People of the Fire, there is no more death!’
The people of Paradise will thereby increase in joy upon their joy, while the people of the Fire will increase in sorrow upon their sorrow.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6548) and Muslim (2850:43).

13254
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A caller will announce: You will remain healthy and never become ill; you will live and never die; you will remain young and never grow old; and you will live in bliss and never experience misery.”
This is the meaning of the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“And it will be proclaimed to them: ‘This is Paradise, which you have been made to inherit because of what you used to do.’” [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:43]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2837).

13255
Jābir narrated:
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, do the people of Paradise sleep?”
He replied:
“No. Sleep is the brother of death.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3517).

13256
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A man from among the Arabs would worship a stone. Then, if he found another stone that he considered better, he would take that one and discard the first.
So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Have you seen the one who takes his own desire as his god?”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11421), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/452–453).

13257
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: “The son of Adam offends Me by cursing time, while I am the disposer of time. The command is in My hand; I alternate the night and the day.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4826) and Muslim (2246:2). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13258
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: “The son of Adam offends Me by saying: ‘Wretched time!’ Let none of you say, ‘Wretched time,’ for I am the disposer of time. I alternate its night and day, and whenever I will, I can bring them to an end.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2246:3).

13259
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not curse time, for Allah is the disposer of time.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2246:5).

13260
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Paradise and the Fire disputed with one another. The Fire said: ‘I have been favored with the arrogant and tyrannical.’
Paradise said: ‘Why is it that only the weak and insignificant among people enter me?’
Allah, Blessed and Exalted, said to Paradise: ‘You are My mercy. Through you I show mercy to whomever I will among My servants…’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4850) and Muslim (2846:36).

13261
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in the lengthy hadith concerning the vision of Allah:
“By the One in Whose hand is my soul, you will have no difficulty in seeing your Lord except as you have no difficulty in seeing one of these.”
Then a servant will be brought before Him, and Allah will say:
“So-and-so, did I not honor you, give you status, grant you a spouse, subject horses and camels to you, and allow you to lead and enjoy a generous share?”
He will reply:
“Yes.”
Allah will say:
“Did you think that you would meet Me?”
He will reply:
“No.”
Allah will say:
“Then I will neglect you today just as you neglected Me.”
Then another servant will be brought, and Allah will say:
“So-and-so, did I not honor you, give you status, grant you a spouse, subject horses and camels to you, and allow you to lead and enjoy a generous share?”
He will reply:
“Yes, my Lord.”
Allah will say:
“Did you think that you would meet Me?”
He will reply:
“No.”
Allah will say:
“Then I will neglect you today just as you neglected Me…”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2968).

13262
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī and Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
Allah says: “Might is My garment and greatness is My cloak. Whoever contends with Me regarding either of them, I will punish him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2620).

13263
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated—Sufyān said: “I know it only as being from the Prophet ﷺ”—concerning the words:
“Or a remnant of knowledge,”
that he said:
“Writing.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1992).

13264
Umm al-ʿAlāʾ, an Anṣārī woman who had pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Emigrants were distributed among the Anṣār by drawing lots, and ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn fell to their household.
She said:
We accommodated him in our homes. He then became ill with the sickness from which he died.
When he died, was washed, and was shrouded in his garments, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered.
I said:
“May Allah have mercy upon you, Abū al-Sāʾib. I testify concerning you that Allah has honored you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“How do you know that Allah has honored him?”
I replied:
“May my father be sacrificed for you, O Messenger of Allah! Then whom would Allah honor?”
He replied:
“As for him, certainty has come to him. By Allah, I certainly hope for good for him. But by Allah, although I am the Messenger of Allah, I do not know what will be done with me.”
She said:
“By Allah, after that I never again declared anyone to be pure and assured of his final outcome.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1243).
ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn died after participating in the Battle of Badr, during the second year after the Hijrah.
He was the first of the Emigrants to die in Madinah and the first of them to be buried in al-Baqīʿ.
Regarding the Prophet’s ﷺ words:
“By Allah, although I am the Messenger of Allah, I do not know what will be done with me,”
al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said in Fatḥ al-Bārī (3/115–116):
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said this in accordance with the words of Allah in Sūrat al-Aḥqāf:
‘Say: I am not something unprecedented among the messengers, nor do I know what will be done with me or with you.’ [Sūrat al-Aḥqāf 46:9]
This was before the revelation of the words:
‘So that Allah may forgive you what preceded of your sin and what will follow.’ [Sūrat al-Fatḥ 48:2]
This is because Sūrat al-Aḥqāf is Makkan and Sūrat al-Fatḥ is Madinan, without disagreement concerning either of them. It is also authentically established that he ﷺ said that he would be the first to enter Paradise, along with other explicit reports conveying the same meaning.”
I say: perhaps the Prophet ﷺ said this out of humility before Allah, the Exalted. There are also other interpretations. See Nawāsikh al-Qurʾān by Ibn al-Jawzī and other works.

13265
ʿAwf ibn Mālik al-Ashjaʿī narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ set out, and I was with him, until we entered a Jewish place of worship in Madinah on the day of one of their festivals. They disliked our entering upon them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to them:
“Jewish people, show me twelve men among you who will testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will remove from every Jew beneath the surface of the sky the anger with which He is angry at him.”
They remained silent, and no one answered him.
He repeated it to them, but no one answered.
He said it a third time, but no one answered him.
Then he said:
“You have refused. By Allah, I am indeed al-Ḥāshir, I am al-ʿĀqib, and I am al-Muqaffī, whether you believe me or reject me.”
Then he departed, and I was with him.
When he had nearly left, a man behind us called:
“Remain where you are, O Muḥammad!”
That man then asked:
“Jewish people, what kind of man do you know me to be among you?”
They replied:
“We do not know of any man among us more knowledgeable of the Book of Allah or possessing greater understanding than you, nor was there anyone more knowledgeable than your father before you or your grandfather before your father.”
He said:
“Then I testify by Allah that he is the Prophet of Allah whom you find described in the Torah.”
They replied:
“You are lying!”
They rejected his testimony and began speaking evil of him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You are lying, and your statement will not be accepted. A moment ago you praised him with all the good qualities you mentioned, but once he believed, you declared him a liar and said what you have now said. Therefore your statement will not be accepted.”
ʿAwf said:
We left as three people: the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, myself, and ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām.
Then Allah revealed concerning him:
“Say: Have you considered: if it is from Allah and you disbelieve in it, while a witness from the Children of Israel testifies to something like it and believes, while you remain arrogant? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23984), Ibn Ḥibbān (7162), and al-Ḥākim (3/415–416).

13266
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
“I never heard the Prophet ﷺ say concerning anyone walking upon the earth that he was among the people of Paradise except ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām.”
He said:
“And concerning him this verse was revealed:
‘And a witness from the Children of Israel testified…’”
The narrator said:
“I do not know whether Mālik said that this statement about the verse was part of the hadith or was an additional comment.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3812) and Muslim (2483). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Regarding Saʿd’s statement:
“I never heard the Prophet ﷺ say concerning anyone walking upon the earth…”
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ certainly knew that the Prophet ﷺ had given ten Companions glad tidings of Paradise.
The scholars answered that Saʿd made this statement after most of those who had been given the glad tidings of Paradise had died. ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām lived after them, while of the ten only Saʿd himself and Saʿīd ibn Zayd remained.
This interpretation is supported by some versions of the hadith, such as Muslim’s wording:
“concerning a living person walking upon the earth.”
See Fatḥ al-Bārī.

13267
Yūsuf ibn Māhak narrated:
Marwān was governor over the Ḥijāz, having been appointed by Muʿāwiyah.
He delivered a sermon in which he began mentioning Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah, seeking to have the people pledge allegiance to him as successor after his father.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr said something in response.
Marwān ordered:
“Seize him!”
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān entered the house of ʿĀʾishah, and they were unable to reach him there.
Marwān then said:
“This is the one concerning whom Allah revealed:
‘The one who says to his parents: Uff to you both! Do you promise me that I will be brought forth…?’”
ʿĀʾishah replied from behind the curtain:
“Allah did not reveal anything in the Qur’an concerning us except that Allah revealed the declaration of my innocence.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4827).
The correct view is that this verse was revealed concerning a disbeliever who was disobedient and disrespectful toward his parents.
This is indicated by Allah’s words immediately afterward:
“Those are the ones against whom the Word has become binding,”
meaning that punishment has become due upon them.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr was a believer and among the excellent Muslims, so he cannot be one of those upon whom the decree of punishment became binding.

13268
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
I never saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laugh so fully that I could see the back of his mouth. He would only smile.
She said:
Whenever he saw clouds or wind, concern could be seen upon his face.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, when people see clouds, they rejoice, hoping that they contain rain. Yet when you see them, I can see concern upon your face.”
He replied:
“O ʿĀʾishah, what assurance do I have that they do not contain punishment? A people were punished by the wind, and when that people saw the punishment approaching, they said: ‘This is a cloud bringing us rain.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4828, 4829), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (899:16).

13269
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
Whenever the wind blew violently, the Prophet ﷺ would say:
“O Allah, I ask You for its good, the good contained within it, and the good with which it was sent; and I seek refuge in You from its evil, the evil contained within it, and the evil with which it was sent.”
She said:
When clouds gathered in the sky, his complexion would change. He would go out and come back in, moving forward and backward.
Then when rain fell, his concern would disappear, and I could see the relief upon his face.
ʿĀʾishah said:
I asked him about this, and he replied:
“Perhaps, O ʿĀʾishah, it could be as the people of ʿĀd said: ‘When they saw it as a cloud approaching their valleys, they said: This is a cloud bringing us rain.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3206) and Muslim (899:15). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

13270
Al-Ḥārith ibn Yazīd al-Bakrī narrated:
I set out intending to complain about al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥaḍramī to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
When I passed through al-Rabadhah, I found an elderly woman from Banū Tamīm stranded there.
She said:
“Servant of Allah, I have a need with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Will you take me to him?”
So I carried her with me and arrived in Madinah.
There I saw black banners.
I asked:
“What is happening with the people?”
They said:
“He intends to send ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ out on a mission.”
I sat until the Prophet ﷺ had finished.
He then entered his home—or the narrator said, his tent.
I sought permission to enter, and he gave me permission. I entered and sat down.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked me:
“Has there been anything between you and Banū Tamīm?”
I replied:
“Yes. We had the upper hand over them. On my way here I passed through al-Rabadhah and found an elderly woman from among them stranded there. She asked me to bring her to you, and she is now at the door.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave her permission to enter.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, place al-Dahnāʾ as a boundary between us and Banū Tamīm.”
The old woman became angry, sprang to her feet, and said:
“Then where will you force your people of Muḍar to go, O Messenger of Allah?”
I said:
“I am like the saying: ‘A goat carried its own death!’ I brought this woman without realizing that she would become my opponent! I seek refuge in Allah and His Messenger from being like the envoy of ʿĀd.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“And what was the envoy of ʿĀd?”
I replied:
“You have asked one who knows the matter well!”
He encouraged me to continue the story.
I said:
The people of ʿĀd were struck by drought, so they sent a man named Qayl as their representative.
He stayed with Bakr, who gave him wine to drink for a month, while two slave girls called al-Jarādatān sang for him.
Then he went out toward the mountains of Mahrah and called:
“I did not come to treat a sick person, nor to ransom a prisoner. O Allah, give ʿĀd the rain that You used to give them!”
Black clouds then passed before him, and a voice called out from them for him to choose.
He pointed toward one of the darkest clouds.
A voice from it called:
“Take it as ash-gray destruction, leaving no one from ʿĀd alive.”
The woman used to say:
“Do not be like the envoy of ʿĀd!”
Al-Ḥārith continued:
“O Messenger of Allah, it has reached me that the amount of wind sent against them was no more than the opening of this ring of mine.”
Abū Wāʾil said:
“That is likewise what reached me.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3274), Aḥmad (15954), and al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (10/276). The wording is that of al-Ṭabarī.

13271
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
A group of jinn descended upon the Prophet ﷺ while he was reciting the Qur’an in the valley of Nakhlah.
When they heard it, they said:
“Listen attentively.”
They said to one another:
“Be silent.”
There were nine of them, one of whom was called Zawbaʿah.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“And remember when We directed toward you a group of the jinn listening to the Qur’an. When they attended it, they said: ‘Listen attentively…’”
continuing until:
“…those are in clear error.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/456).

13272
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not deliberately recite the Qur’an to the jinn on this occasion, nor did he see them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out with a group of his Companions heading toward the market of ʿUkāẓ.
At that time, the devils had been prevented from obtaining news from heaven, and shooting stars had been sent against them.
The devils returned to their people, who asked them:
“What has happened to you?”
They replied:
“We have been prevented from receiving news from heaven, and shooting stars have been sent against us.”
They said:
“This must be because something new has happened. Travel throughout the eastern and western regions of the earth and discover what it is that has come between you and the news of heaven.”
So they set out throughout the east and west of the earth.
A group of them who had traveled toward Tihāmah passed by the Prophet ﷺ at Nakhlah while he was on his way to the market of ʿUkāẓ. He was leading his Companions in the dawn prayer.
When they heard the Qur’an, they listened attentively to it and said:
“This, by Allah, is what has come between us and the news of heaven.”
They returned to their people and said:
“Our people, indeed we have heard a wondrous Qur’an. It guides to right conduct, so we have believed in it, and we will never associate anyone with our Lord.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ:
“Say: It has been revealed to me that a group of the jinn listened and said: ‘Indeed, we have heard a wondrous Qur’an.’” [Sūrat al-Jinn 72:1]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (773, 4921) and Muslim (449:149). The wording is that of Muslim.

13273
ʿĀmir narrated:
I asked ʿAlqamah:
“Was Ibn Masʿūd present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of the Jinn?”
ʿAlqamah replied:
I myself asked Ibn Masʿūd:
“Was any one of you present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of the Jinn?”
He replied:
“No.”
But one night we were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and suddenly lost track of him.
We searched for him throughout the valleys and mountain paths, and we said:
“He has been carried away, or he has been assassinated!”
We spent the worst night that any people could spend.
When morning came, there he was, approaching from the direction of Ḥirāʾ.
We said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we lost you and searched for you but could not find you. We spent the worst night any people could spend.”
He replied:
“A caller from the jinn came to me, so I went with him and recited the Qur’an to them.”
Then he took us with him and showed us their tracks and the traces of their fires.
The jinn had asked him for provisions, so he said:
“Every bone over which the name of Allah has been mentioned that falls into your hands will become covered with as much meat as possible for you, and every piece of dung will be fodder for your animals.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“So do not clean yourselves after relieving yourselves with either of these, for they are the food of your brothers.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (450).

13274
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I used to carry a water vessel for the Prophet ﷺ for his ablution and personal needs.
While I was following him carrying it, he asked:
“Who is this?”
I replied:
“It is Abū Hurayrah.”
He said:
“Find me some stones with which I can clean myself, but do not bring me a bone or dung.”
So I brought him some stones, carrying them in the edge of my garment, and placed them beside him. Then I moved away.
When he had finished, I walked with him and asked:
“Why not bones or dung?”
He replied:
“They are among the food of the jinn. A delegation of the jinn of Naṣībīn came to me—and excellent jinn they were—and asked me for provisions. So I supplicated to Allah for them that whenever they passed by a bone or a piece of dung, they would find food upon it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3860).

13275
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
I never heard ʿUmar say about anything:
“I think it is such-and-such,”
except that it generally turned out as he had thought.
While ʿUmar was sitting one day, a handsome man passed by him.
ʿUmar said:
“Either my judgment has failed me, or this man is still upon the religion he followed during the pre-Islamic period, or he used to be their soothsayer. Bring the man to me.”
The man was brought to him, and ʿUmar told him what he had said.
The man replied:
“I have never seen a Muslim addressed in such a manner as I have been today.”
ʿUmar said:
“I insist that you tell me.”
He replied:
“I used to be their soothsayer during the pre-Islamic period.”
ʿUmar asked:
“What was the most remarkable thing your female jinn-companion ever brought you?”
He replied:
One day while I was in the marketplace, she came to me, and I could clearly recognize fear in her.
She said:
“Have you not seen the jinn and their despair, their hopelessness after being overturned, and their following after young camels and their saddlecloths?”
ʿUmar said:
“He has spoken the truth.”
Then ʿUmar continued:
While I was once near their idols, a man came with a calf and slaughtered it.
A voice cried out from it with a sound louder than any voice I had ever heard, saying:
“O Jalīḥ! A successful matter! An eloquent man is saying: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah!’”
The people sprang up.
I said:
“I will not leave until I know what lies behind this.”
Then the voice called again:
“O Jalīḥ! A successful matter! An eloquent man is saying: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah!’”
I got up, and it was not long before it was announced:
“This is a Prophet.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3866).

13276
Al-Miqdām ibn Maʿdīkarib narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The martyr has six distinctions with Allah: he is forgiven with the first flow of his blood; he is shown his place in Paradise; he is protected from the punishment of the grave; he is made safe from the Greatest Terror; he is adorned with the garment of faith; he is married to the maidens of Paradise; and he is permitted to intercede for seventy of his relatives.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2799), whose wording is given here, Aḥmad (17182), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (20/266–267).

13277
Qays al-Judhāmī, a man who had Companionship with the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The martyr is granted six things with the first drop of his blood: every sin is expiated from him; he is shown his place in Paradise; he is married to the maidens of Paradise; he is made safe from the Greatest Terror and from the punishment of the grave; and he is adorned with the garment of faith.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17783).

13278
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every sin of the martyr is forgiven except debt.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1886).

13279
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believers will be delivered from the Fire, then they will be detained upon a bridge between Paradise and the Fire. Retribution will be taken from some of them for wrongs committed against others in the world.
When they have been refined and purified, permission will be given to them to enter Paradise. By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, each one of them will be more readily guided to his dwelling in Paradise than he was to his dwelling in this world.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6535).

13280
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
On that day we encountered the polytheists. The Prophet ﷺ stationed a force of archers and appointed ʿAbdullāh as their commander.
He said:
“Do not leave your position. If you see us defeating them, do not leave your position; and if you see them defeating us, do not come down to help us.”
When we met the enemy, they fled. I even saw their women running up the mountain, lifting their garments from their lower legs so that their anklets became visible.
The archers began saying:
“The spoils! The spoils!”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“The Prophet ﷺ instructed me that you must not leave your position.”
But they refused.
When they disobeyed, the course of the battle was turned against them, and seventy Muslims were killed.
Abū Sufyān then appeared above them and called:
“Is Muḥammad among the people?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not answer him.”
He called:
“Is the son of Abū Quḥāfah among the people?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not answer him.”
Then he called:
“Is the son of al-Khaṭṭāb among the people?”
He then said:
“These men have been killed. Had they been alive, they would have answered.”
ʿUmar could no longer restrain himself and said:
“You lie, enemy of Allah! Allah has preserved for you that which will disgrace you.”
Abū Sufyān cried:
“Exalted be Hubal!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Answer him.”
They asked:
“What should we say?”
He said:
“Say: Allah is higher and more majestic.”
Abū Sufyān said:
“We have al-ʿUzzā, while you have no ʿUzzā.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Answer him.”
They asked:
“What should we say?”
He replied:
“Say: Allah is our Protector, and you have no protector.”
Abū Sufyān then said:
“A day for a day at Badr; war goes back and forth. You will find mutilation among your dead. I did not order it, but neither did it displease me.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4043).

13281
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The disbeliever eats with seven intestines, while the believer eats with one intestine.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5394) and Muslim (2060). The wording is that of Muslim.
In al-Bukhārī’s wording:
“The disbeliever—or the hypocrite, and ʿUbaydullāh was unsure which of the two was said—eats with seven intestines.”

13282
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Muslim eats with one intestine, while the disbeliever eats with seven intestines.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Ṣifat al-Nabī ﷺ (9) and al-Bukhārī (5396).

13283
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ hosted a disbelieving guest.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that a sheep be milked for him, and he drank its milk. Then another was milked, and he drank that too. Then another, until he had drunk the milk of seven sheep.
The next morning, the man embraced Islam.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that a sheep be milked for him, and he drank its milk. Then another sheep was milked, but he was unable to finish it.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“The believer drinks with one intestine, while the disbeliever drinks with seven intestines.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Ṣifat al-Nabī ﷺ (10) and Muslim (2063).

13284
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man used to eat a great deal. Then he embraced Islam and began eating only a little.
This was mentioned to the Prophet ﷺ, and he said:
“The believer eats with one intestine, while the disbeliever eats with seven intestines.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5397).

13285
Jābir and Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believer eats with one intestine, while the disbeliever eats with seven intestines.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2061).

13286
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The believer eats with one intestine, while the disbeliever eats with seven intestines.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2062).

13287
Nāfiʿ narrated:
Ibn ʿUmar would not eat until a poor person was brought to eat with him.
I once brought a man to eat with him, and the man ate a great deal.
Ibn ʿUmar said:
“Nāfiʿ, do not bring this man to me again. I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
‘The believer eats with one intestine, while the disbeliever eats with seven intestines.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5393) and Muslim (2060:183). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13288
ʿAmr narrated:
Nahīk was a man who ate a great deal.
Ibn ʿUmar said to him:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘The disbeliever eats with seven intestines.’”
Nahīk replied:
“I believe in Allah and His Messenger.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5395).

13289
Muʿāwiyah ibn Ḥaydah al-Qushayrī narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“In Paradise there is a sea of milk, a sea of water, a sea of honey, and a sea of wine; then the rivers branch out from them afterward.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2571) and Aḥmad (20052).

13290
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and His Messenger, establishes the prayer, and fasts Ramadan, then it is a right upon Allah that He admit him into Paradise, whether he migrated in the cause of Allah or remained in the land in which he was born.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we not tell the people this?”
He replied:
“Indeed, in Paradise there are one hundred levels that Allah has prepared for those who strive in His cause. The distance between every two levels is like the distance between heaven and earth.
So when you ask Allah, ask Him for al-Firdaws, for it is the middle and highest part of Paradise. Above it is the Throne of the Most Merciful, and from it the rivers of Paradise spring forth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7423).

13291
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gesture with these two fingers—the middle finger and the index finger—and say:
“I and the Hour have been sent like these two.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4936) and Muslim (2950).
Regarding the words:
“But how will remembrance benefit them when it comes upon them?”
how will they be able to benefit from remembering and taking heed once the Hour has already arrived?
The proper time for taking heed will have passed.
This resembles Allah’s words:
“On that Day man will remember, but how will remembrance benefit him then?” [Sūrat al-Fajr 89:23]
Their repentance, remembrance, and attempts to seek another opportunity will not benefit them at all on that Day.

13292
ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal narrated from ʿAbdullāh ibn Sarjis, who said:
I saw the Prophet ﷺ and ate bread and meat with him—or he said, tharīd.
ʿĀṣim said:
I asked him:
“Did the Prophet ﷺ seek forgiveness for you?”
He replied:
“Yes, and for you as well.”
Then he recited:
“Seek forgiveness for your sin and for the believing men and believing women.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Sarjis continued:
Then I went around behind him and looked at the Seal of Prophethood between his shoulders.
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2346).

13293
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ used to supplicate with the following words:
“My Lord, forgive me my errors, my ignorance, my excesses in all my affairs, and whatever You know better about me than I do.
O Allah, forgive me my sins, those I committed deliberately, those committed through ignorance, and those committed in jest—all of that is found within me.
O Allah, forgive me what I have sent ahead and what I have left behind, what I have concealed and what I have made public. You are the One who brings forward and the One who delays, and You have power over all things.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6398) and Muslim (2719). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13294
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“By Allah, I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him more than seventy times every day.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6307).

13295
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Satan said: ‘By Your might, my Lord, I will continue leading Your servants astray for as long as their souls remain in their bodies.’
The Lord said: ‘By My might and My majesty, I will continue forgiving them for as long as they seek My forgiveness.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (11729, 11237, 11244).

13296
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah created the creation, and when He had finished creating them, the bond of kinship stood and said: ‘This is the standing place of one seeking refuge from being severed.’
Allah said: ‘Yes. Would you not be pleased that I maintain ties with whoever maintains you and cut off whoever cuts you off?’
It replied: ‘Yes.’
He said: ‘Then that is granted to you.’”
Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Recite, if you wish: ‘Would you then, if you were given authority, spread corruption in the land and sever your family ties? Those are the ones whom Allah has cursed, so He made them deaf and blinded their sight. Do they not reflect upon the Qur’an, or are there locks upon their hearts?’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4831) and Muslim (2554). The wording is that of Muslim.

13297
Abū Bakrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no sin more deserving that Allah hasten punishment for its perpetrator in this world, in addition to what He stores up for him in the Hereafter, than transgression and severing family ties.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4902), al-Tirmidhī (2511), Ibn Mājah (4211), Aḥmad (20398), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (4/162).

13298
Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“The one who severs family ties will not enter Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5984) and Muslim (2556).
The word “one who severs” here means one who severs the ties of kinship.

13299
Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever would like his provision to be expanded and his lifespan to be extended, let him maintain his family ties.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5986) and Muslim (2557:21).

13300
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Whoever is pleased that his provision be expanded and his lifespan be extended, let him maintain his family ties.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5985).

13301
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Kinship is a closely intertwined bond. Whoever maintains it, I maintain ties with him, and whoever cuts it off, I cut him off.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5989) and Muslim (2555). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Muslim’s wording is:
“The bond of kinship is suspended from the Throne, saying: ‘Whoever maintains me, Allah maintains him; and whoever cuts me off, Allah cuts him off.’”

13302
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, the bond of kinship is a branch derived from al-Raḥmān. Allah says to it: ‘Whoever maintains you, I maintain him; and whoever cuts you off, I cut him off.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5988).

13303
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The one who truly maintains family ties is not merely the one who reciprocates kindness. Rather, the true maintainer of family ties is the one who continues to maintain them even when his relatives cut him off.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5991).

13304
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited the verse:
“If you turn away, He will replace you with another people, and then they will not be like you.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, who are these people with whom we would be replaced if we turned away, and who would not be like us?”
The Prophet ﷺ struck the thigh of Salmān and said:
“This man and his people. If the religion were as far away as the Pleiades, men from among the Persians would reach it.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (21/233–234) and al-Baghawī in his Tafsīr (4/164).

13305
Zayd ibn Aslam narrated from his father that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was traveling on one of his journeys, while ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was traveling alongside him at night.
ʿUmar asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about something, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not answer him. He asked him again, but he did not answer. Then he asked him a third time, and he still did not answer.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said to himself:
“May your mother lose you, O ʿUmar! You have persistently questioned the Messenger of Allah ﷺ three times, and each time he has not answered you.”
ʿUmar said:
I urged my camel forward and went ahead of the Muslims, fearing that Qur’an might be revealed concerning me.
It was not long before I heard someone calling out loudly for me.
I said to myself:
“I feared that Qur’an had been revealed concerning me.”
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and greeted him with salām.
He said:
“Tonight a sūrah has been revealed to me that is more beloved to me than everything upon which the sun rises.”
Then he recited:
“Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Qurʾān (476) and al-Bukhārī (4177).
The word “nazarta” means:
“You persisted and pressed repeatedly.”

13306
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
When the verses:
“Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory, so that Allah may forgive you…”
through His words:
“…a tremendous triumph” [Sūrat al-Fatḥ 48:1–5]
were revealed as the Prophet ﷺ was returning from al-Ḥudaybiyyah, the Companions were overcome by sorrow and distress, and the sacrificial animals had already been slaughtered at al-Ḥudaybiyyah.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“A verse has been revealed to me that is more beloved to me than the entire world.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1786) and al-Bukhārī (4834).

13307
ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī narrated:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of the Conquest riding his she-camel and reciting Sūrat al-Fatḥ—or part of Sūrat al-Fatḥ—and he recited it with a melodious, repeated modulation.
Muʿāwiyah then recited in imitation of the recitation of Ibn Mughaffal and said:
“Were it not that the people would gather around you, I would reproduce the modulation just as Ibn Mughaffal reproduced the Prophet’s ﷺ recitation.”
I asked Muʿāwiyah:
“How did he modulate his voice?”
He replied:
“Ā, ā, ā,”
three times.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7540) and Muslim (794:237).

13308
Abū Wāʾil narrated:
Sahl ibn Ḥunayf stood on the Day of Ṣiffīn and said:
“People, question your own judgment. We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, and had we seen that fighting was required, we would have fought.”
This concerned the treaty made between the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the polytheists.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, are we not upon the truth while they are upon falsehood?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿUmar asked:
“Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿUmar said:
“Then why should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“O son of al-Khaṭṭāb, I am the Messenger of Allah, and Allah will never abandon me.”
ʿUmar left, but because of his agitation he could not remain patient, so he went to Abū Bakr and said:
“Abū Bakr, are we not upon the truth while they are upon falsehood?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿUmar asked:
“Are not our dead in Paradise and their dead in the Fire?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿUmar said:
“Then why should we accept humiliation in our religion and turn back before Allah has judged between us and them?”
Abū Bakr said:
“O son of al-Khaṭṭāb, he is the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and Allah will never abandon him.”
Then the Qur’an announcing the victory was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He sent for ʿUmar and recited it to him.
ʿUmar asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, is this truly a victory?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Then ʿUmar’s heart became content and he returned.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3182) and Muslim (1785:94).

13309
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
We were traveling with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the time of al-Ḥudaybiyyah. They mentioned that they stopped on a stretch of dahās land—meaning sandy ground.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Who will keep watch over us?”
Bilāl said:
“I will.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him:
“Then you will fall asleep.”
But they slept until the sun had risen.
The people awoke, among them various men, including ʿUmar.
We said:
“Speak aloud.”
The Prophet ﷺ then woke up and said:
“Do as you normally do.”
So we did so.
Then he said:
“Do the same whenever someone sleeps through a prayer or forgets it.”
The she-camel of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ then became lost, so I went looking for it. I found that its rope had become caught on a tree.
I brought it back to the Prophet ﷺ, and he mounted it happily.
Whenever revelation descended upon the Prophet ﷺ, it weighed heavily upon him, and we could recognize its effect upon him.
He withdrew some distance behind us and began covering his head with his garment. The experience became intense upon him, until we realized that revelation was descending upon him.
Then he came to us and informed us that the following had been revealed to him:
“Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4421) and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (400).

13310
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
“You consider the Conquest to be the conquest of Makkah. The conquest of Makkah was indeed a conquest, but we considered the true victory to be the Pledge of al-Riḍwān on the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah.”
We were with the Prophet ﷺ, fourteen hundred in number.
At al-Ḥudaybiyyah there was a well. We drew out all of its water until we did not leave a single drop in it.
When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he came to the well and sat upon its edge. He asked for a vessel containing water, performed ablution, rinsed his mouth, supplicated, and then poured the water into the well.
We left it for a short while, and afterward it provided as much water as we and our riding animals desired.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4150).

13311
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ stood in prayer until his feet became swollen.
It was said to him:
“Allah has forgiven you what preceded of your sin and what will follow.”
He replied:
“Should I not then be a grateful servant?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4836) and Muslim (2819:80). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13312
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to stand in prayer at night until his feet became cracked and swollen.
ʿĀʾishah asked:
“Why do you do this, O Messenger of Allah, when Allah has already forgiven you what preceded of your sin and what will follow?”
He replied:
“Should I not love to be a grateful servant?”
Later, when his body became heavier, he would pray while seated. Then, when he intended to bow, he would stand, complete his recitation, and then bow.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4837) and Muslim (2820). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13313
Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated:
A man was reciting Sūrat al-Kahf while there was an animal in the house, and it began to startle and move about.
The man stopped reciting, and suddenly a mist or cloud had enveloped him.
He mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ, who said:
“Continue reciting, so-and-so, for that was tranquility which descended because of the Qur’an—or descended for the Qur’an.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3614) and Muslim (795:241). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13314
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
One night, Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr was reciting in his enclosure when his horse suddenly became agitated.
He continued reciting, and the horse became agitated again. He continued reciting, and it became agitated yet again.
Usayd said:
I feared that it might trample Yaḥyā, so I went toward it.
Then I saw something like a canopy above my head, containing objects resembling lamps. It rose upward into the sky until I could no longer see it.
The next morning I went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, last night, in the middle of the night, I was reciting in my enclosure when my horse became agitated.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Recite, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
“I continued reciting, and it became agitated again.”
He said:
“Recite, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
“I continued reciting, and it became agitated again.”
He said:
“Recite, Ibn Ḥuḍayr.”
I said:
I stopped because Yaḥyā was close to the horse and I feared that it might trample him. Then I saw something like a canopy containing objects resembling lamps, which rose into the sky until I could no longer see it.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Those were the angels listening to your recitation. Had you continued reciting until morning, the people would have seen them, and they would not have hidden themselves from them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (796).

13315
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated concerning the Qur’anic verse:
“Indeed, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner,”
that in the Torah it states:
“O Prophet, indeed We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a protection for the unlettered people. You are My servant and My Messenger. I have named you al-Mutawakkil.
You are neither harsh nor coarse, nor loud and quarrelsome in the marketplaces. You do not repay evil with evil; rather, you pardon and overlook.
Allah will not take him in death until, through him, He straightens the crooked religion, so that they say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’ Through it He will open blind eyes, deaf ears, and sealed hearts.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4838).

13316
Jābir narrated:
On the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah we were fourteen hundred.
We pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ while ʿUmar was holding his hand beneath a tree, which was a samurah tree.
Jābir said:
“We pledged allegiance to him that we would not flee. We did not pledge allegiance to him specifically upon death.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1856).

13317
Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd narrated:
I asked Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ:
“Upon what did you pledge allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah?”
He replied:
“Upon death.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4169) and Muslim (1860:80).
Regarding the phrase:
“Upon death,”
other narrations state:
“They pledged to remain steadfast and not flee.”
Whoever described the pledge as being “upon death” intended what steadfastness necessarily entailed: remaining firm even if it resulted in death.
Whoever described it as “upon steadfastness” stated the literal wording and reality of the pledge.

13318
ʿUthmān ibn Mawhab narrated:
A man came to perform Ḥajj at the Sacred House and saw a group of people sitting together.
He asked:
“Who are these people sitting here?”
They replied:
“They are from Quraysh.”
He asked:
“Who is the elderly man?”
They replied:
“Ibn ʿUmar.”
So he approached Ibn ʿUmar and said:
“I want to ask you about something. Will you tell me?”
He said:
“I adjure you by the sanctity of this House: do you know that ʿUthmān fled on the Day of Uḥud?”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“Do you know that he was absent from Badr and did not attend it?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“Do you know that he was absent from the Pledge of al-Riḍwān and did not attend it?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man exclaimed, “Allāhu akbar!”
Ibn ʿUmar then said:
“Come here, and I will explain to you what you have asked me about.
As for his fleeing on the Day of Uḥud, I testify that Allah pardoned him for it.
As for his absence from Badr, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was his wife, and she was ill. The Prophet ﷺ said to him:
‘You will have the reward and the share of a man who attended Badr.’
As for his absence from the Pledge of al-Riḍwān, had there been anyone in the valley of Makkah more respected than ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān, the Prophet ﷺ would have sent that person instead. But he sent ʿUthmān.
The Pledge of al-Riḍwān took place after ʿUthmān had gone to Makkah.
The Prophet ﷺ then said, indicating with his right hand:
‘This is the hand of ʿUthmān.’
He struck his right hand upon his other hand and said:
‘This pledge is on behalf of ʿUthmān.’
Then Ibn ʿUmar said:
“Now take this explanation with you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4066).

13319
ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī said:
“I was among those who were present beneath the Tree.”
He also narrated that the Prophet ﷺ prohibited throwing small stones by placing them between the fingers and flicking them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4841) and Muslim (1954:55). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13320
Thābit ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk, who was among the Companions of the Tree, narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever deliberately and falsely swears by a religion other than Islam is as he said; and whoever kills himself with an iron weapon will be punished with it in the Fire of Hell.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1363, 4843) and Muslim (110:177).

13321
Jābir narrated:
We were fourteen hundred on the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah.
The Prophet ﷺ said to us:
“Today you are the best people upon the earth.”
Jābir said:
“Had I still been able to see, I would have shown you the location of the tree.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4840) and Muslim (1856:71). The wording is that of Muslim; al-Bukhārī mentioned only the number.

13322
Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
I set out to perform Ḥajj and passed by some people who were praying.
I asked:
“What is this place?”
They replied:
“This is the tree where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ received the Pledge of al-Riḍwān.”
I then went to Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib and told him.
Saʿīd said:
“My father narrated to me that he was among those who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ beneath the tree.
When we returned the following year, we had forgotten its exact location and were unable to find it.”
Saʿīd then said sarcastically:
“The Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ did not know where it was, yet you have discovered it? Then you must know better!”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4163) and Muslim (1859:77).

13323
Nāfiʿ narrated:
People used to come to a tree that was said to be the Tree of al-Riḍwān and pray near it.
When this reached ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, he warned them against doing so and ordered that the tree be cut down.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd in al-Ṭabaqāt (2/100).
Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib’s words:
“The Companions of Muḥammad ﷺ did not know it, while you know it, so you must know better,”
were words of rejection and sarcasm.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb had ordered the tree to be cut down, so where could that tree be now?
As for the location where it had stood, Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh had known it precisely, but after becoming blind he was unable to point it out.

13324
Juwayriyah narrated from Nāfiʿ that Ibn ʿUmar said:
“We returned the following year, yet no two of us could agree upon which tree it was beneath which we had pledged allegiance. That was a mercy from Allah.”
I asked Nāfiʿ:
“Upon what did the Prophet ﷺ take their pledge? Was it upon death?”
He replied:
“No. Rather, he took their pledge upon steadfastness.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2958).
Regarding Ibn ʿUmar’s words:
“That was a mercy from Allah,”
the meaning is that the disappearance and uncertainty surrounding the tree was a mercy, lest people become tested by it and begin believing that the tree itself possessed some power to benefit or harm.

13325
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Umm Mubashshir informed me that she heard the Prophet ﷺ say in the presence of Ḥafṣah:
“None of those who pledged allegiance beneath the Tree will enter the Fire, if Allah wills.”
Ḥafṣah said:
“But yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He rebuked her.
She then recited:
“There is none among you except that he will come to it.” [Sūrat Maryam 19:71]
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has also said: ‘Then We will save those who were mindful of Allah and leave the wrongdoers therein on their knees.’” [Sūrat Maryam 19:72]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2496).

13326
Jābir narrated:
A slave belonging to Ḥāṭib came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ complaining about Ḥāṭib and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Ḥāṭib will certainly enter the Fire.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“You are wrong. He will not enter it, for he witnessed Badr and al-Ḥudaybiyyah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2495).

13327
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Eighty armed men from Makkah descended upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from the mountain of al-Tanʿīm, intending to take the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions by surprise.
The Muslims captured them, but the Prophet ﷺ spared their lives.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah, after He had given you victory over them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1808).
Regarding the word “salan”, al-Nawawī said that it has been transmitted with two vocalizations: with both the sīn and lām open, and with the lām silent while the sīn is either kasrah or fatḥah.
Al-Ḥumaydī said that it means “peace” or “truce.”
Al-Qāḍī said that most scholars vocalized it in this way, though the first reading is clearer, meaning that they were captured.
Al-Khaṭṭābī decisively read it with both the sīn and lām open and said that it means submission and yielding, as in Allah’s words:
“And they offer you peace.” [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:90]
meaning submission. It is a verbal noun that can apply to one person, two people, or a group.
Ibn al-Athīr said:
“This interpretation best fits the account, for they were not captured through a peace treaty. Rather, they were taken by force and surrendered themselves because they were unable to resist.”

13328
ʿAbdullāh ibn Mughaffal al-Muzanī narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at al-Ḥudaybiyyah beneath the tree mentioned by Allah in the Qur’an.
Some of its branches were hanging over the back of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, while ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Suhayl ibn ʿAmr were before him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to ʿAlī:
“Write: In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.”
Suhayl ibn ʿAmr took hold of his hand and said:
“We do not recognize ‘the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.’ Write in our agreement what we recognize.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Write: In Your name, O Allah.”
So he wrote:
“This is what Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, has agreed upon with the people of Makkah.”
Suhayl ibn ʿAmr again took hold of his hand and said:
“We would indeed have wronged you if you truly were His Messenger. Write in our agreement what we recognize.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Write: This is what Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib has agreed upon—and I am the Messenger of Allah.”
So he wrote it.
While we were in that situation, thirty armed young men suddenly came out against us and rushed toward us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated against them, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, took away their sight.
We advanced toward them and captured them.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Have you come under anyone’s covenant, or has anyone granted you protection?”
They replied:
“No.”
So he released them.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah, after He had given you victory over them. And Allah sees all that you do.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (16800), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11447), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/460–461).

13329
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, the Exalted, revealed in His Book concerning a people who behaved arrogantly:
‘Indeed, whenever it was said to them, “There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,” they would act arrogantly.’ [Sūrat al-Ṣāffāt 37:35]
And Allah, the Exalted, said:
‘When those who disbelieved placed within their hearts the pride of ignorance, Allah sent down His tranquility upon His Messenger and upon the believers, and bound them firmly to the word of God-consciousness, and they were more deserving of it and worthy of it.’
The word of God-consciousness is: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah; Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah.’ The polytheists acted arrogantly toward it on the Day of al-Ḥudaybiyyah, when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was writing the agreement with them concerning the truce.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as cited by Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr (7/345), Ibn Ḥibbān (218), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Asmāʾ wa-al-Ṣifāt (195, 196).

13330
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out intending to perform ʿUmrah, but the disbelievers of Quraysh prevented him from reaching the House.
So he slaughtered his sacrificial animals and shaved his head at al-Ḥudaybiyyah.
He made an agreement with them that he would perform ʿUmrah the following year, that he would not bring weapons against them except swords, and that he would remain in Makkah only for as long as they permitted.
The following year he performed ʿUmrah and entered Makkah in accordance with the agreement.
After remaining there for three days, they ordered him to leave, so he departed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4252).

13331
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions arrived in Makkah after the fever of Yathrib had weakened them.
The polytheists said:
“Tomorrow there will come to you a people who have been weakened by fever and have suffered greatly from it.”
So they sat on the side facing al-Ḥijr.
The Prophet ﷺ instructed his Companions to walk briskly with short, quick steps during the first three circuits of ṭawāf and to walk normally between the two corners, so that the polytheists could see their strength.
The polytheists said:
“Are these the people you claimed had been weakened by fever? These people are stronger than such-and-such!”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Nothing prevented the Prophet ﷺ from ordering them to perform the brisk walk during all the circuits except his concern to spare them hardship.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4256) and Muslim (1266:240).
Regarding the words:
“With your heads shaved and your hair shortened”:

13332
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ shaved his head, as did a group of his Companions, while some of them shortened their hair.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1729) and Muslim (1301).

13333
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, have mercy on those who shave their heads.”
They said:
“And those who shorten their hair, O Messenger of Allah?”
He said:
“O Allah, have mercy on those who shave their heads.”
They again said:
“And those who shorten their hair, O Messenger of Allah?”
He then said:
“And those who shorten their hair.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Ḥajj (184), al-Bukhārī (1727), and Muslim (1301:317).
